# FIXES — Civitics Platform

Actionable improvement backlog. Every item has a priority, complexity, and enough context to hand to Qwen or Claude Code directly.

**Priority key:**
- 🔴 Critical — Bug that breaks or blocks real functionality
- 🟠 High — Meaningful product/UX gap, address soon
- 🟡 Medium — Worthwhile improvement, schedule when practical
- 🟢 Quick Win — Small effort, high visible impact (batch these)
- ⬜ Future — Phase 2+ or requires significant design/pipeline work

**Complexity key:** S = <2h · M = 2–8h · L = 1–3 days · XL = multi-day + planning

**Workflow:** Every bullet has a stable ID (`<!--id:FIX-NNN-->`). Don't remove or renumber IDs — they're the handle commits reference via `Fixes: FIX-NNN` trailers. Completion state is sourced from `docs/done.log`; regenerate this file's checkboxes with `pnpm fixes:sync`. See [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md#fixes-workflow) for details.

**Section rules:**
- Active sections — `[x]` items are fine (checked by `fixes:sync`). Periodically move completed clusters to `## COMPLETED` for readability.
- `## COMPLETED` — **`[x]` only**. A `[ ]` item here means it was moved before it shipped — move it back to the active section.
- Deferred / blocked items always stay in active sections as `[ ]`, never in COMPLETED. If a deferred item was closed by a broad "closeout" commit, add a `reopen` line to `done.log` and uncheck it.

---

## STRATEGIC PILLARS
> Directional goals, not checkable tasks. Concrete sub-tasks are threaded throughout this doc. Phase 2+ strategy, architecture, and the Social App live in `docs/ROADMAP.md`.

## BUGS — Fix These First
- [x] 🟠 S — **responsiveness route embeds nonexistent civic_initiatives relation (PGRST200 500)** — apps/civitics/app/api/officials/[id]/responsiveness/route.ts:19 embeds civic_initiatives!initiative_id(id, title, scope) on base table civic_initiative_responses, but relation civic_initiatives does NOT exist (gate g1: no civic_initiatives table). PostgREST returns PGRST200 so the route 500s. Not a one-token rename: the initiative_id FK targets proposals, and proposals has no scope column (scope lives on initiative_details). Intended shape: pull proposals(title) + initiative_details(scope) via civic_initiative_responses.initiative_id. Found by ULTRACODE untyped-ref audit (S3); see docs/audits/2026-06-28-untyped-ref-audit.md. <!--id:FIX-691-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **entity_connections metadata->>is_current filter is silent-zero (metadata empty for all rows)** — Two pipeline stale-close paths filter entity_connections on metadata->>is_current eq true, but entity_connections.metadata is EMPTY for all 5.68M rows (gate g4: zero jsonb keys) so the filter matches nothing and stale appointment edges are never closed. Sites: packages/data/src/pipelines/agency-leadership/index.ts:145 and packages/data/src/pipelines/plum-book/index.ts:238 (closeStaleConnections). Fix: filter on the first-class column .is(ended_at, null) instead (ended_at exists per g1 and the rebuild populates it, NULL meaning active). plum-book also scopes by evidence_source eq plum_book which IS valid. ULTRACODE untyped-ref audit (S4); see docs/audits/2026-06-28-untyped-ref-audit.md. <!--id:FIX-692-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Contract-flow RPCs fan out multi-industry-tag recipients (duplicate/double-counted rows)** — The two contract-flow RPCs (chord_contract_flows_full and treemap_recipients_by_contracts_full) and the FIX-838 rollup rebuild that mirrors them LEFT JOIN entity_tags on the recipient WITHOUT a DISTINCT-ON. A financial_entity carrying more than one industry tag (1058 local / 1654 prod) fans out: in the treemap it becomes N duplicate entity_id rows, each with the entity FULL total (top-500 shows 7 dup entity_ids / 493 distinct in both envs); in the chord a single award is counted into EVERY one of the recipient sectors, inflating per-sector totals and the agency cross-sector sum. FIX-838 deliberately PRESERVED this fan-out (byte-exact materialization swap) and tracked the semantics fix here. Fix: pick ONE deterministic tag per recipient via a DISTINCT-ON CTE (mirror the FIX-836 ind CTE / fetchIndustryTagsByEntityId smallest-tag pick) in both _full bodies AND refresh_contract_flow_rollups(), so an entity appears once with a single industry and each award lands in exactly one sector. Re-run the FIX-838 equality proofs against the corrected _full afterward (they will legitimately change). The recipient rollup can then revert to a plain entity_id PRIMARY KEY. Verify total_cents conservation: SUM over sectors = SUM over agencies = raw contract SUM. See [[FIX-838]] [[FIX-836]]. <!--id:FIX-873-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Audit the remaining unbounded .in() id-list reads (silent-empty-result class)** — **Bug hunt, unknown yield — needs a per-site investigation gate, NOT a blanket rewrite.** Filed by [[FIX-901]], which shipped the shared helper this audit remediates onto. **Mechanism (always the same, always silent):** supabase-js encodes an `.in()` filter into the request URL. Past roughly **200 uuids** the URL exceeds Kong's header limit and the gateway returns 414 `URI too long` **before PostgREST ever sees the query**. `withDbTimeout` converts that into `{data: null}`, and the near-universal `?? []` / `data?.x` fallback at the call site turns a hard failure into an empty result on an HTTP 200. Nothing logs, nothing 500s, the page renders — just missing. **The bound is URL LENGTH at ~200 ids. It is NOT the 1,000-row PostgREST `max_rows` cap** — do not reason about this class in terms of row counts, and do not assume a list under 1,000 is safe. **Four independent prior instances**, each found by accident rather than by monitoring: [[FIX-509]] (treemap, 414 verified at ~356 uuids / ~13 KB), [[FIX-514]], [[FIX-774]] (established the 200 bound), [[FIX-899]] (officials directory rendered ZERO tag pills for every official for months; ~1,000 ids / ~40 KB; surfaced only because [[FIX-897]] added pills to that surface and they did not appear). **Census at HEAD e5056d8b** — 248 total `.in(` call sites across `apps/` + `packages/` (excluding node_modules and tests); of those, on the order of **96–108 pass an id-list VARIABLE and are not obviously chunked** (96 under the strict single-line regex below; ~108 under a looser hand-classification that also catches multi-line and spread forms). Recipes: total = `grep -rn '.in(' --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx apps packages | grep -v node_modules | grep -v '.test.ts' | wc -l`; candidates = the same grep narrowed to `.in("col", identifier)` with chunk/batch/slice/page-named variables filtered out. **Most are probably bounded in practice — classifying them is the whole job.** Per site, the question is a BOUND ASSESSMENT: what feeds this id list, and can it exceed ~200? Note `.in("status", [...])` over a small literal enum list is fine and common — those are not in scope. Sites where the list is a fixed-arity literal, a `.slice(0, N<=200)`, or provably capped upstream should be marked bounded and left alone (ideally with a one-line note recording the bound, so the next auditor does not re-derive it). Sites where an unbounded or growth-prone collection feeds the filter are real latent bugs: migrate onto `fetchChunkedByIds` from `apps/civitics/src/lib/paginate.ts` ([[FIX-901]]), which chunks at 200 and **surfaces failed chunks in its result object instead of swallowing them**. Prioritise request-path renders and API routes that feed user-visible surfaces — those are where a silent empty result reads as 'this entity has no data' rather than as an error. **Deliberately NOT bundled into [[FIX-901]]:** that was a small mechanical PR migrating three sites whose correct behaviour was already verified; this is open-ended investigation over ~100 sites and would have swallowed it. **Explicitly out of scope here:** a lint rule or codemod banning raw `.in()`. Tempting, but it would have to distinguish id lists from small enum lists, and that discrimination is a design question of its own. <!--id:FIX-902-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **get_financial_entity_naics 504s over PostgREST — the nightly financial industry tagger cannot complete** — Surfaced while running the [[FIX-908]] run-twice verification. tagFinancialEntities() in packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts fetches its NAICS rollup with db.rpc('get_financial_entity_naics') over PostgREST. That call **504s after exactly 60.0s** on local — reproduced with zero application code in the path (raw fetch to /rest/v1/rpc/get_financial_entity_naics with the secret key: status=504, 'The upstream server is timing out'). The SAME function over a direct psql connection returns in **537ms warm** (104s cold, 1 row). callWithRetry THROWS after 5 attempts, so the phase burns ~310s (5 x 60s) and then kills the whole function before it ever reaches clear_financial_entity_rule_tags + the upserts. Net effect: **the financial-entity industry tags are never rebuilt** — runRuleBasedTagger catches the throw and calls failSync, so the nightly reports a failure but the existing entity_tags rows are simply left frozen at whatever the last successful run wrote. Fails CLOSED (no data loss — the DELETE is downstream of the throw, which is the FIX-443 ordering working as designed), but the tagger is dead. **This is the FIX-443/444/651 class, and the fix is the one already applied to this function's two SIBLING reads:** the keyword fetch was migrated to selectDirect by FIX-444 for this exact reason ('the PostgREST path could never complete'), and both official rollups were migrated to rollupJsonbDirect by FIX-651. The NAICS rollup is the one read in tagFinancialEntities still going over the capped PostgREST path. Migrate it to selectDirect/rollupJsonbDirect (direct pg.Client, session statement_timeout raised) and the 60s gateway ceiling stops applying. **Deliberately NOT bundled into [[FIX-908]]/[[FIX-909]]** — those are a vocabulary change and this is a transport bug that predates them; bundling would have made a taxonomy PR responsible for a pipeline migration. **Verify on prod before assuming local-only**: prod's service_role carries an 8s statement_timeout (tighter than local's 60s gateway), so if the function is slow there too this has been failing on prod as well — check data_sync_log for tag_rules rows with a get_financial_entity_naics error. Cross-ref [[FIX-443]] [[FIX-444]] [[FIX-651]]. <!--id:FIX-910-->
- [ ] 🟠 M — **link_officials_to_districts() misses the non-numeric tail, so the nightly openstates run erases any backfilled SLD links** — packages/data/src/pipelines/openstates-bulk/people.ts overwrites officials.metadata WHOLESALE on every upsert (metadata: { org_classification, state }), dropping district_jurisdiction_id for every state legislator on every nightly run. It then calls link_officials_to_districts() to re-derive the links. That RPC (migration 20260509000002, FIX-217) only does numeric normalisation — ltrim zero-pad plus stripping a leading 'House|Senate|Assembly|Legislative District ' prefix. It re-links ~6530 of 7373 and leaves the SAME 843-row tail unlinked every single night: NH lower 393 ('Belknap 1' vs 'New Hampshire State House District Belknap 01'), MA lower 158 ('10th Bristol'), VT lower 150 ('Addison-1'), ID lower 70 ('10A'/'10B' multi-member), MA upper 40 ('Berkshire, Hampden, Franklin and Hampshire' vs hyphenated), VT upper 30 ('Chittenden Southeast' vs 'Chittenden South East'), ME lower 2 (tribal, correctly unlinkable). CONSEQUENCE: the [[FIX-859]] backfill is NOT durable against prod — a one-shot run is erased within 24h. Durable fix: fold the tier-2..4 rules from packages/data/src/scripts/backfill-sld-district-links.ts into link_officials_to_districts() so the nightly re-link covers the tail itself, matching on parent_id + short_name prefix + normalised core rather than only the numeric form. That is a CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION migration. Until it lands, run the backfill locally only. Cross-ref [[FIX-217]], [[FIX-855]]. **CORRECTION appended 2026-07-27 (implementation session):** this bullet names only the DAILY bulk-people pipeline as the destroyer. There is a second one it misses, and it means the RPC change ALONE cannot make the linkage durable — see [[FIX-915]]. runOpenStatesPipeline (the weekly Sunday API pipeline, pipelines/index.ts line ~775) calls the SAME upsertLegislatorsBatch writer, overwrites metadata again, and NEVER calls link_officials_to_districts() — so the weekly run lands after the daily repair and undoes it with no repair of its own. Also corrected: the erasure cadence for that second path is WEEKLY (Sunday isWeekly block), not nightly. FIX-913 as scoped here is still correct and still ships — it just becomes the safety net for genuinely new or genuinely changed districts once [[FIX-915]] stops the destruction at source. Also measured during implementation: the script's join key and the RPC's join key are in PERFECT parity on prod (7373 legislators; 6530 both-agree, 0 disagree, 0 script-only, 0 rpc-only, 0 ambiguous, 843 the known tail), so adopting the script's key family in the RPC relocates nothing. <!--id:FIX-913-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **upsertLegislatorsBatch clobbers officials.metadata wholesale — BOTH OpenStates pipelines destroy district_jurisdiction_id, and the API one never re-links** — Surfaced while implementing [[FIX-913]], and it means the RPC change alone provably cannot make SLD linkage durable. packages/data/src/pipelines/openstates/writer.ts buildOfficialInsert sets metadata: input.metadata and the update path is .upsert(records, { onConflict: 'id' }), so every legislator upsert REPLACES officials.metadata rather than merging it — district_jurisdiction_id is destroyed on contact. Both OpenStates pipelines share this one writer. runBulkPeoplePipeline (daily, openstates-bulk/people.ts) at least calls link_officials_to_districts() afterwards and repairs the numeric tier. runOpenStatesPipeline (weekly Sunday block, pipelines/index.ts line ~775, gated only on OPENSTATES_API_KEY) calls the SAME writer at openstates/index.ts line ~348 and NEVER calls the linker — verified, no reference to it exists anywhere in that file. So the weekly API run lands AFTER the daily repair and undoes it with no repair of its own; a perfect [[FIX-913]] RPC still ends Sunday night with links wiped. Fix is structural: extend the existing chunked external_source_refs lookup pass in upsertLegislatorsBatch to also select id, metadata from officials for the ids it finds, and merge {...existing, ...input.metadata} on the update path (PostgREST cannot express a server-side jsonb merge; insert path unchanged). Belt-and-braces, also call link_officials_to_districts() at the end of runOpenStatesPipeline mirroring openstates-bulk/people.ts lines ~264-274, so newly-seen legislators link in the same run. Blast radius verified small: state legislators carry exactly four metadata keys across the whole population — org_classification (7373), state (7373), district_jurisdiction_id (7313), state_abbr (1); only district_jurisdiction_id is being destroyed, the others are re-supplied by the upsert. With this landed [[FIX-913]] becomes the safety net for genuinely new or genuinely changed districts rather than a nightly repair job. Follow-up worth considering separately: district_jurisdiction_id belongs in a real column, not metadata. Cross-ref [[FIX-859]], [[FIX-217]]. <!--id:FIX-915-->
- [ ] 🟠 S — **get_financial_entity_naics reads from_id but every NAICS-bearing contract is agency->entity — the NAICS pass has written zero rows since inception** — **Filed, not fixed** — found while verifying [[FIX-910]], out of scope for the [[FIX-916]] curation PR because repointing it WRITES thousands of new industry tags on prod, which is a data change needing its own before/after. `get_financial_entity_naics()` (supabase/migrations/20260530020000_financial_size_tags_serverside_443.sql) aggregates `fr.from_id AS entity_id ... WHERE fr.from_type = 'financial_entity' AND fr.relationship_type IN ('contract','grant') AND fr.metadata->>'naics_code' IS NOT NULL`. But USASpending writes contracts in the **agency to recipient** direction: measured on prod 2026-07-28, `financial_relationships` holds 3,240,205 `agency -> financial_entity` contract rows of which **3,238,246 carry a `naics_code`**, plus 582,485 `agency -> financial_entity` grant rows with none, plus exactly 54 `financial_entity -> financial_entity` grant rows — also with none. So the contractor whose NAICS code we want is `to_id`, and the function's `from_type='financial_entity'` filter matches only those 54 naics-less grant rows. **`get_financial_entity_naics()` returns `[]` on BOTH envs**, and `SELECT count(*) FROM entity_tags WHERE entity_type='financial_entity' AND tag_category='industry' AND metadata ? 'naics_code'` is **0 on prod**. The entire NAICS half of `tagFinancialEntities()` is dead code that has never emitted a tag. Consequences: (1) the ~78 contractor rows the [[FIX-909]] NAICS2_INDUSTRY sector corrections were written to protect do not exist — those corrections are still right, they are just currently unreachable; (2) the function scans the contract/grant slice of a 3.8M-row table every nightly to produce an empty array; (3) US contractors get no industry label from their own federal contract industry code, only from name-keyword guessing. Fix is to aggregate `fr.to_id` where `to_type='financial_entity'` (keep the `from_id` arm too if the 54-row financial_entity-sourced grant path is ever populated). **Size the blast radius first** — count distinct `to_id` with a naics_code and diff the resulting tag set against today's keyword tags before shipping, because these rows land at confidence 0.85/primary and will collide with keyword tags on the `(entity_type, entity_id, tag, tag_category)` unique constraint. Cross-ref [[FIX-910]] [[FIX-909]] [[FIX-443]] [[FIX-916]]. <!--id:FIX-919-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **An out-of-vocabulary `other` tag reaching an official's top-3 kills the ENTIRE nightly rule tagger — live on local, latent on prod** — **Filed, not fixed** — found while running the [[FIX-916]] verification, and it is a strictly sharper consequence of [[FIX-911]] than that bullet's 'display wart' framing. `tagOfficials()` reads official_sector_affinity_rollup, excludes ONLY `'Untagged'`, and then calls `assertIndustryVocabulary()`, which THROWS on any value outside VALID_INDUSTRIES. `other` is written into entity_tags by ai-classifier.ts ([[FIX-911]]), flows into the rollup like any other tag, and is NOT in VALID_INDUSTRIES — so the moment an `other`-tagged donor lands in an ACTIVE official's top-3 by dollars, the assert fires and takes down the **entire nightly rule tagger**: proposals, officials AND financial entities, all three phases, because runRuleBasedTagger() catches the throw at the top level and calls failSync. **This is not hypothetical — it is LIVE on local today.** Measured 2026-07-28: 6 active officials carried `other` at rank<=3 on the local prod-clone before [[FIX-916]] and 7 after, so `pnpm data:tag-rules` cannot complete on local at all, and the [[FIX-916]] run-twice proof had to invoke tagFinancialEntities() in isolation to get around it. **Prod is currently safe but only by margin, not by design**: all 8 prod `other` rollup rows sit at rank 11-13 of 13 carrying $1,000-$5,500 against rank-3 values of $83k-$745k. A read-only simulation of the post-[[FIX-916]] state confirmed prod stays safe (rank 10-17, tightest margin Dave Min at $2,000 vs a rank-3 of $31,000, ~15x) — but the local 6->7 shift proves the promotion mechanism is real, and ANY change that reduces an official's classified mass (a de-tagging, a donor merge, a cycle rolling off) can push `other` up. **The asymmetry is the bug:** `'Untagged'` is excluded at the read as 'not an industry', and `other` is exactly the same kind of thing — an abstention — yet it is allowed through to an assert that kills the pipeline. Two candidate fixes, not exclusive: (1) stop writing `other` at all ([[FIX-911]]'s suggested shape) and delete the existing rows, which removes the input; (2) exclude non-vocabulary values at the rollup read the same way `'Untagged'` is excluded, so an out-of-vocabulary tag degrades to 'no pill' instead of 'no nightly'. (2) is the durable one — it makes the pipeline robust to the NEXT out-of-vocabulary writer rather than just this one — but it must not silently swallow real drift, so pair it with a logged count. Note the assert itself is correct and worth keeping; what is wrong is that a display-layer vocabulary violation is fatal to an unrelated data pipeline. Cross-ref [[FIX-911]] [[FIX-897]] [[FIX-916]] [[FIX-908]] [[FIX-890]]. <!--id:FIX-920-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **oil_gas contamination sweep — 50 non-fossil donors re-assigned or de-tagged** — Second curated-override cohort, seeded into public.financial_entity_industry_overrides by migration 20260729010000 from docs/audits/2026-07-28-oil-gas-escapees.tsv. Same mechanism as [[FIX-916]] — no producer change needed, tagFinancialEntities() already applies the table as the last word — so this is a pure data-state change. **Why 50 and not the 15 a cohort-derived list would have found:** the first pass was derived from the HR 26 roll-call cohort; sweeping the WHOLE tag's 128 donating donors found 50 escapees, so a cohort-derived list would have left two thirds of the contamination in place. Composition: 23 utilities (electric/gas/renewables/nuclear — Portland General Electric, NV Energy, Evergy, Eversource, PacifiCorp, Clearway, Apex Clean Energy, Holtec), 15 mining (coal + hardrock — Arch Resources, Core Natural Resources, Murray Energy, Warrior Met, Newmont, Rio Tinto, Barrick), 4 manufacturing (Chromalloy, Komatsu Mining, Mitsubishi Power, Ashland), 2 agriculture (Corn Refiners, American Olive Oil Producers), 1 finance (CNG Holdings — Check n Go consumer lending, the original false positive that made oil_gas look like it covered payday lenders), and 5 NONE (leadership/abstract PACs plus US Ecology). Measured on local 2026-07-29: oil_gas falls 128 → 78 donating donors and $47,966,759 → $43,205,506 on the donation ledger scoped to officials. **STAYS OPEN after the seeding commit** — it is a data-state change and the prod half needs the seed applied AND a full backfill_official_sector_affinity_rollup() run, because the sector-affinity rollup's incremental dirty set keys off financial_relationships.updated_at and a tag-only change never enters it (the same trap that made [[FIX-916]] invisible on prod for 11 days). Cross-ref [[FIX-916]] [[FIX-908]] [[FIX-918]] [[FIX-919]]. <!--id:FIX-921-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **matchRow accepts a single-element name pool with no first-name check** — packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/index.ts matchRow() step 2 (weball name fallback) narrows the last-name pool by CAND_OFFICE_ST and then returns `pool[0]` outright when `pool.length === 1` — **the first names are never compared on that branch**. First-name narrowing only runs in the `pool.length > 1` arm below it, so the state narrowing makes the bug WORSE rather than safer: it collapses an ambiguous same-surname pool to one and thereby removes the very ambiguity guard that would otherwise have forced a skip. Consequence is a wrong binding between a FEC CAND_ID and a real named person on a public page, not a missing one. **Live case on the prod clone:** Ohio has three officials surnamed Brown. Before the `cn{yy}` candidate-tier row for S6OH00163 existed, the OH Brown pool was effectively just the sitting House member, so Sherrod Brown's SENATE CAND_ID bound to **Shontel M. Brown** (D-OH-11, source_ids congress_gov B001313). Her page carries 43,960 donation rows / 38,964 distinct donors / $50,998,289 spanning 2018-10-17..2026-03-31, against $2.26M on her own committee H2OH11169; her rendered top donors include NRSC ($52,800, cycle 2024) and OHIO SENATE VICTORY 2024 ($49,000). Because the writer upserts on financial_relationships_relcycle_unique (relationship_type, from_id, to_id, cycle_year), a later corrected binding writes a NEW row and never retires the old one — so every historical mis-binding is still resident. Fix is a first-name agreement gate on EVERY name-fallback match including single-element pools, mirroring the `normalizedLast|first3|state` key the sibling fallback loop at ~line 960 already uses correctly (do not modify that loop). A missed match is strictly better than a wrong match: an unbound official renders $0 plus a sync note; a mis-bound one renders another person's donors under their name. Enumeration of the residue is [[FIX-930]]; cleanup is a separate PR. <!--id:FIX-929-->
- [ ] 🔴 M — **87+ officials hold FEC donation money with no FEC binding — enumerate before cleanup** — Residue of [[FIX-929]]. financial_relationships upserts on (relationship_type, from_id, to_id, cycle_year), so when a CAND_ID→official binding CHANGES — because a cn{yy} candidate-tier row got minted, or the name-fallback pool composition shifted — the writer creates a NEW row under the new to_id and **never retires the old one**. Every historical mis-binding is therefore still resident in the table. Signal for a suspect: an official holding relationship_type='donation' rows whose metadata->>'source' starts with 'fec_bulk', but whose source_ids carries NEITHER fec_candidate_id NOR a role-prefix-matching fec_id (Senator→S, Representative→H, mirroring buildMatchIndex) — i.e. an official the current match index would never select, yet who holds FEC money. Enumerated read-only by packages/data/src/scripts/audit-fec-orphan-attribution.ts (pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:audit:fec-attribution); output at docs/audits/2026-07-29-fec-orphan-attribution.{tsv,md}. **Remediation is deliberately NOT in the FIX-929 PR** — the population is far too large to delete on a heuristic and splits into three branches that need OPPOSITE treatments: (1) SAME-PERSON DUPLICATE — twin's first name agrees, the same human exists as both an elected and a candidate row and both hold the money (Jon Ossoff 1376dc1e-f697-40b2-8c0f-780f8fe8ea00 shares 15,779 donor-cycle pairs with candidate row 4719d31a-7db4-4f6f-b933-8442a1fb1f76 / S8GA00180; note the ELECTED row is the stale one, ending 2026-03-31 vs 2026-06-04); (2) CROSS-PERSON MISATTRIBUTION — twin's first name disagrees, the severe branch (Shontel M. Brown f29bbd4e-944f-4840-adbd-16a4706a3c02 sharing 42,681 pairs with Sherrod Brown d758a091-fa36-47d4-b2b0-e264d41f1fc2); (3) UNIQUE HOLDER — no meaningful overlap, most likely a CORRECT binding whose persistNewFecIds step never ran because the fec phase gets SIGTERM'd at its GHA budget. **Branch 3 must not be treated as garbage** — its remediation is to WRITE the missing source_ids id, not to remove rows. Branches 1 and 2 need a merge/re-point that also carries the FIX-544 FK surface (evidence_cards, entity_comments, official_donor_totals, official_sector_affinity_rollup, entity_connections). Re-derive the counts on PROD before acting — the audit figures are clone-measured. Cross-ref [[FIX-544]], [[FIX-761]], [[FIX-808]]. <!--id:FIX-930-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Merge SAME-PERSON duplicate officials — 47 pairs, $219.5M of double-counted FEC donations retired** — PR 2a of the [[FIX-930]] remediation. **LOCAL APPLIED 2026-07-30; PROD PENDING — leave open.** Implemented by `packages/data/src/scripts/merge-same-person-official-dupes.ts` (`pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:merge:official-dupes`, dry-run by default, `--apply` to commit, `--allow-prod` required for prod). Mechanism: `matchRow` binds a FEC CAND_ID to an official in memory and the writer upserts on `financial_relationships_relcycle_unique (relationship_type, from_id, to_id, cycle_year)`, so a CHANGED `to_id` writes a NEW row and never retires the old one — when the `cn{yy}` stage minted a candidate-tier row for a sitting member, that member's FEC money SPLIT across two officials rows and has been double-counted since. [[FIX-929]] stopped new bad bindings; this closes the residue for the one branch where remediation is mechanical (both rows hold the SAME human's money, and the survivor is decidable from role rather than from a name comparison). **The ELECTED row always survives** — votes, committee memberships, bioguide id, career history, promises, comments and every inbound user-facing link hang off it; the candidate row is the one the FEC pipeline minted. Note this is the OPPOSITE direction from `promote_candidate_to_elected()`, which rewrites elected→candidate because its job is a genuine promotion; reusing that RPC here would have moved a sitting Senator's whole record onto a stub literally named `T Ossoff`. Order is load-bearing: `fec_candidate_id` is merged onto the survivor FIRST (the `persistNewFecIds` server-side jsonb-merge shape, writer.ts:953), because moving the money while leaving the survivor unbound would let the next Sunday run re-mint the candidate row and re-split. Collisions resolve to the FRESHER row by `updated_at`, never summed — the two rows are the same FEC data under two bindings. **No officials row is deleted**: the duplicate ends up holding zero `financial_relationships` rows, which keeps the change reversible and every FK intact, and keeps this out of the officials-dedup design question. Manifest is RE-DERIVED live from the shared [[FIX-930]] classifier (extracted to `packages/data/src/scripts/fec-orphan-classify.ts` so the audit and the remediation can never diverge) and every pair is re-gated STRUCTURALLY server-side before any write; the 3 name-decided state-mismatch merge-blockers are excluded and go to [[FIX-934]]. Local measured: 47 pairs, 127,289 colliding losers deleted, 133,637 FR rows moved (donation + `ie_support` + `ie_oppose` — the IE money was split across these pairs too), platform donation dollars on officials $5,023,195,233 → $4,803,650,534, drop $219,544,699 = deleted-loser sum EXACTLY, zero `official_donor_totals` diffs outside the manifest. Reference case Jon Ossoff $14,585,287 + $14,627,225 → $15,633,810 (union taking the fresher side on each of 15,779 colliding pairs — a $29.2M result would have meant summing). Rebuilt in-transaction: `donor_rollup_rebuild_recipients` (official_donor_totals, official_donor_rollup_mv, official_small_dollar_rollup, official_sector_affinity_rollup, treemap_individuals_rollup, official_donor_bracket_totals), `rebuild_official_donation_totals`, `financial_entity_donation_totals_rebuild` + `donor_party_rollup_rebuild_donors` over the 105,732 affected donors (their incremental pg_cron paths key off `financial_relationships.updated_at` and a DELETE bumps nothing, so relying on them would silently skip any donor whose only change was a deletion), and 6 money-reading MVs; post-commit `rebuild_financial_entity_ie_totals`, `refresh_group_donor_rollup`, `rebuild_entity_search_index`, `refresh_treemap_individuals_global`. `entity_connections` money edges pointing at a duplicate are deleted (provably false once that row holds no money) and the twice-weekly rebuild repopulates the survivor; `entity_connection_stats(_mv)` and `browse_facet_counts` stay stale until their own pg_cron. Cross-ref [[FIX-930]], [[FIX-929]], [[FIX-934]], [[FIX-935]], [[FIX-936]], [[FIX-937]], [[FIX-938]], [[FIX-761]], [[FIX-544]]. <!--id:FIX-933-->

These are not double-counted copies of the official's own money; they are **someone else's donors rendered under this official's name**, so the remediation is the opposite of [[FIX-933]]'s: DELETE the mis-bound rows rather than merge them. Because deletion is destructive and name-only evidence is exactly what the [[FIX-930]] investigation proved unreliable (FEC files legal names — Ted/Rafael Cruz, Jack/John Reed — so a first-name disagreement is NOT proof of a different person), **this branch needs a reviewed, row-level manifest before anything is deleted**, not a heuristic sweep. Worst live cases from the clone: Shontel M. Brown (Representative, OH) holds 43,960 rows / $50,998,289 sharing 42,681 donor-cycle pairs with **Sherrod** Brown's S6OH00163; David Porter, a sitting federal judge, holds $7,391,766 of **Katherine** Porter's money; Heather Cooke (Austin, inactive) holds $6,001,754 of **Rebecca** Cooke's. Also in scope for this PR: the **3 name-decided merge-blockers** [[FIX-933]] deliberately excluded, where the jurisdiction does NOT match the state in the twin's CAND_ID (a shared name across state lines, needs hand confirmation): Scott Wiener (SF) → Scott Wiener (H8CA11116); Christine Jones (AUS) → Chris Jones (H6AR02286); Connie Chan (SF) → Connie Chan (H6CA11268) — $3,202,261 between them. And the **47 low-confidence UNIQUE HOLDERs** that clear the audit's fraction cut but not its absolute floor (grep the TSV for `overlap_frac >= 0.28` with `branch=UNIQUE HOLDER`) — they are the ambiguous corner, not confident singletons, and the non-destructive default filed them into branch 3. **Final step for any pair this PR resolves must be retiring the duplicate's claim on the CAND_ID** — see the mechanism in [[FIX-941]]; 153 CAND_IDs are still held by two officials rows because both still hold money, and [[FIX-933]]'s reconciliation deliberately refuses those. Re-derive every figure on prod before acting; the audit is clone-measured and re-runs via `pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:audit:fec-attribution`. - [ ] 🟠 M — **PR 2c — write the missing FEC ids for the 92 UNIQUE HOLDERs ($16.6M of correct bindings with no stored id)** — **File only — not implemented by [[FIX-933]].** Third branch of the [[FIX-930]] audit: 92 officials holding **$16,613,717** of `fec_bulk`-sourced donation money with no meaningful donor-cycle overlap against any same-surname FEC-bound twin. **This branch is NOT garbage and must not be deleted.** The likely cause is the opposite of a mis-binding: the binding was CORRECT, the money landed, and `persistNewFecIds` never ran because the FEC phase gets SIGTERM'd at its GHA budget before the end-of-run id persist (see `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/writer.ts` `persistNewFecIds`, and the resume-state investigation in `investigate-fec-resume-state.ts`). So the remediation is to **WRITE the missing `source_ids` id**, not to remove rows — the same jsonb-merge shape [[FIX-933]] uses. Consequence of leaving it: every one of these officials is invisible to `loadOfficialsByFecIds`, so the `cn{yy}` stage can mint a candidate-tier row for their CAND_ID on any future run and re-split their money — i.e. this branch is a *generator* of the [[FIX-933]] population, and closing it is the durable prevention rather than another cleanup. Deriving the id to write is the actual work: the audit only knows these officials hold `fec_bulk` money, not which CAND_ID it came from, so the writer needs to recover the binding from the donation rows themselves (`financial_relationships.metadata`, `fec_filing_id`, or a re-run of the weball match with the FIX-929 first-name gate) rather than from the surname pool that caused the problem. **Do not reuse the audit's surname-twin join to pick the id** — that is the mis-binding mechanism. - [x] 🟠 S — **matchRow's state-narrowing falls back to the UNFILTERED NATIONAL surname pool** — **File only.** `matchRow` in `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/index.ts` (~line 474) narrows the weball name-fallback candidate pool by state, then does `statePool.length > 0 ? statePool : candidates` — so when state narrowing yields NOTHING, the pool silently widens back to **every official in the country with that surname**. That is the inverse of the intended safety property: the case where narrowing failed is precisely the case where a name match is least trustworthy, and the code responds by making the pool maximally permissive. [[FIX-929]]'s first-name gate mitigates it but does NOT remove it — a coincidental national single-match whose first names happen to agree still binds, and the [[FIX-930]] audit showed first names agreeing by coincidence is common at national scale. This fallback is also the write path that produced [[FIX-937]]'s population: an official whose jurisdiction `short_name` is not a two-letter state code can NEVER satisfy state narrowing, so for them the fallback is not an edge case, it is the only path. Fix is to tighten to **"no state match ⇒ no name match"**, or to require a stronger corroborator (committee linkage via `ccl`, district agreement, or an existing `external_source_refs` binding) before a national-pool single-match is allowed to bind. Prefer the strict form: the cost of refusing to bind is a row in [[FIX-935]]'s UNIQUE HOLDER branch (recoverable, non-destructive), while the cost of a wrong bind is another person's donors rendered under an official's name ([[FIX-934]]). Cross-ref [[FIX-929]], [[FIX-930]], [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-934]], [[FIX-937]]. <!--id:FIX-936-->
- [ ] 🔴 M — **Non-federal-role officials are FEC-matchable at all — 150 officials / $71.1M, 32 ACTIVE and rendering today** — **File only.** Surfaced by the [[FIX-930]] audit. State narrowing in `matchRow` can **never** succeed for an official whose jurisdiction `short_name` is a city or `US` rather than a two-letter state code — confirmed values `AUS`, `SEA`, `SF`, `US` — so via [[FIX-936]]'s national-pool fallback those officials are matched **on surname alone**. **The write path has no role check at all.** `buildMatchIndex`'s S/H/P prefix rule is on the *read* side and correctly refuses these ids, while `matchRow` writes them via `newFecIds` with `storageKey: "fec_id"` and checks nothing — so the pipeline writes a binding, refuses to read it back, and re-derives it by name on the next run, forever. Clone-measured: **118 inactive non-federal-role officials hold $51,267,444; 32 ACTIVE hold $19,833,485 and render on live pages today; 12 inactive federal-role hold $19,661,753.** Largest live case is **David Porter, a sitting federal judge, holding $7,391,766** — Katherine Porter's money; nine federal judges hold ~$17.6M between them **with no `fec_id` stored at all**, which is the read/write asymmetry made visible. Municipal examples: Joy Hollingsworth (Seattle council, ACTIVE) holds `H6IN09176` and $1,490,344 — Trey Hollingsworth's; Heather Cooke (Austin) holds `H4WI03169` and $6,001,754 — Rebecca Cooke's. Two separable pieces: (1) **the existing-data cleanup**, which overlaps [[FIX-934]]'s CROSS-PERSON manifest and should be scoped with it rather than separately; (2) **the design decision** — should non-federal-role officials be in the FEC match pool at all? A city council member or an Article III judge holds no federal seat, so an `H*`/`S*`/`P*` CAND_ID can never legitimately be theirs, and excluding them from the pool up front is both cheaper and stricter than any name heuristic. [[FIX-929]] closes this class going forward for the first-name-disagree case only; the role-blindness is untouched. Note the judges arrive via the CourtListener pipeline and the municipal rows via Legistar, so neither population has any FEC identity to legitimately match. Cross-ref [[FIX-930]], [[FIX-936]], [[FIX-934]], [[FIX-929]], [[FIX-933]]. <!--id:FIX-937-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Senate vote writer binds by lastname:state with last-write-wins — 1,755 votes across 49 senators landed on invisible candidate stubs** — **File only — found while implementing [[FIX-933]], and it is live on the clone today.** `buildOfficialMaps` in `packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts` (~line 300) builds `senatorByNameState` by iterating every official in the Senate governing body `.order("id")` and doing `senatorByNameState.set(lastName.toLowerCase() + ":" + stateAbbr, s.id)` — **no tier filter, no is_active filter, and last-write-wins**. The query's own comment already says "candidate pollution included", so the pool is known to contain the `cn{yy}`-minted candidate-tier rows; nothing then stops one from taking the map slot. Because uuids are random, roughly **half** of contested `(surname, state)` slots resolve to the candidate row rather than the sitting Senator, and the Senate branch (unlike the House branch, which correctly keys on bioguide from `@_name-id`) has no stable identifier to fall back on. **Measured on the local prod-clone 2026-07-30: 1,755 `votes` rows sit on 49 candidate-tier officials, and 48 of those 49 have an elected same-surname twin that also holds votes.** The split is cleanly dated — Jon Ossoff's elected row holds 1,801 votes spanning 2021-01-20 → **2026-05-20** while his candidate row holds 57 spanning **2026-06-01** → 2026-07-23 — so this began the moment the candidate rows joined the Senate governing body and **every sitting Senator on the losing side of the map has had roughly two months of votes missing from their public page ever since**, with no error anywhere: the insert succeeds, it just points at a row nobody can see. Fix is small and should mirror the House branch's determinism: filter the senator pool to `tier = 'elected'` (and/or `is_active`) before building the map, and make the collision explicit — refuse to overwrite an existing slot and log it, rather than silently taking the last row. Prefer resolving Senators by bioguide (`source_ids->>'congress_gov'`) where the Senate XML permits it, so the name key is a fallback rather than the primary. **Do NOT simply move the existing 1,755 votes to the elected rows without fixing the map** — the next nightly would re-insert them onto the candidate row and re-split, since `votes` is unique on `(roll_call_id, official_id)`. [[FIX-933]] deliberately left these votes in place for exactly that reason. Same last-write-wins map-collision class as [[FIX-941]]. Note `official_vote_stats` is materialized off `votes`, so bipartisan/attendance figures for the affected Senators are understated too. Cross-ref [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-941]], [[FIX-939]], [[FIX-545]], [[FIX-837]]. <!--id:FIX-940-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **loadOfficialsByFecIds is last-write-wins on a duplicated CAND_ID — harden it to prefer the elected row** — **File only — the data-side hazard is already closed by [[FIX-933]]; this is the belt-and-braces code fix.** `loadOfficialsByFecIds` in `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/candidates.ts` (~line 395) does an unconditional `map.set(candId, { officialId, tier })` for every row carrying `source_ids->>'fec_candidate_id'`, iterating `.order("id")` — so when two officials rows claim the same CAND_ID, **the one with the higher uuid wins, arbitrarily**. Note the `fec_id` branch immediately below it already gets this right (`if (!map.has(fecId))`, with a comment explaining that an existing `fec_candidate_id` mapping is more authoritative) — the `fec_candidate_id` branch itself has no such guard, so the function is internally inconsistent. Why it matters: writing the FEC id onto the elected survivor — which [[FIX-933]] must do FIRST, or the `cn{yy}` stage re-mints the candidate row — leaves the SAME id on both rows, and for roughly half of pairs the duplicate then wins the map slot and the next Sunday run writes the money straight back onto the candidate row. **The merge would silently undo itself.** Measured before [[FIX-933]]'s reconciliation ran: 83 elected+candidate pairs shared a CAND_ID with the candidate row holding zero money, and **21 of the 83 had the duplicate winning the map slot** — i.e. this was already live and actively re-splitting money for those 21, independent of the merge. [[FIX-933]] closes it in the data by retiring the duplicate's claim (moving it to `merged_fec_candidate_id`) so exactly one row claims each CAND_ID, and it asserts that invariant before commit. The remaining code exposure: a **tier-preference guard so the map can never resolve a CAND_ID to a candidate row when an elected row claims it**, plus a logged warning on collision so a recurrence is visible instead of silent. Cheap, and it protects the [[FIX-933]] result against any future path that re-creates a duplicate claim. **153 CAND_IDs are still held by two rows on the clone** — all elected+candidate pairs where BOTH still hold money, so [[FIX-933]]'s reconciliation correctly refused them; they resolve as part of [[FIX-934]]/[[FIX-935]], and until then those are exactly the ids this guard would protect. Same last-write-wins map-collision class as [[FIX-940]]. Cross-ref [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-934]], [[FIX-935]], [[FIX-940]], [[FIX-929]], [[FIX-759]]. <!--id:FIX-941-->
- [ ] 🟠 S — **officials.total_received_cents has NO scheduled maintainer — two graph surfaces render unbounded-stale money** — **Found while shipping [[FIX-933]].** `officials.total_received_cents` is a denormalized money column with **no owner**: 21 pg_cron jobs and not one of them touches it, `rebuild_official_donation_totals()` appears nowhere outside a comment and [[FIX-933]]'s own script, and `data:rebuild:received-totals` (FIX-675) rebuilds **financial_entities**, not officials. So the column drifts from the moment any relationship write lands and never converges. The asymmetry is the tell: [[FIX-702]]/[[FIX-726]] deliberately moved the heavy `rebuild_financial_entity_donation_totals` / `_received_totals` full rebuilds out of the FEC phase and onto incremental dirty-scoped pg_cron jobs (`financial-entity-totals-incremental`, Tue) because they saturated Pro Small — but the **officials** equivalent was left with no replacement at all. The comment block at `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/index.ts` ~1897 names only the financial-entity pair as moved, and `rebuild_official_donation_totals` is mentioned there purely as "pattern mirrors". Measured pre-[[FIX-933]] on the local prod-clone: Jon Ossoff's column read **$11,555,275** against a live rollup of **$14,585,287**, and his candidate row read **$0** against **$14,627,225** — i.e. the column was already ~21% low on a sitting Senator before any merge. **Read sites that render it directly:** `/api/graph/treemap` (route.ts ~400-513 — selects it per official and builds `totalByOfficial`, which sizes the treemap's cells) and `/api/graph/small-dollar` (route.ts ~87-120 — `totalReceivedCents` is the DENOMINATOR of the small-dollar share, so a low column inflates the reported small-dollar percentage). Also `/api/search/entity`, `/api/browse/typeahead`, `/donors/[id]`, and `rebuild_entity_search_index` (which copies it into `entity_search_index`, so search sort/facets inherit the drift). **[[FIX-933]] incidentally repaired this platform-wide on local** — it calls `rebuild_official_donation_totals()` in-transaction, and the local clone now shows **0 disagreements across all 4,321 officials with donation rows** (abs gap $0). That is a side effect of one PR, not a fix: the next relationship write re-opens the gap, and prod stays stale until [[FIX-933]]'s prod run does the same one-shot repair. Durable fix: give the column a scheduled owner, shaped like the [[FIX-702]]/[[FIX-726]] incremental jobs rather than a full-table UPDATE — note `rebuild_official_donation_totals()` is a bare full-table `UPDATE … FROM (SELECT … GROUP BY to_id)`, so it is both expensive on Pro Small AND unable to zero an official whose last donation row was deleted (no aggregate row → no UPDATE → stale non-zero forever; [[FIX-933]] has to zero its duplicates explicitly for exactly this reason). Either fold it into the existing `donor-rollup-refresh` daily job (which already recomputes `official_donor_totals` per dirty recipient and is the natural place), or drop the column and read `official_donor_totals.total_cents` at the call sites — the rollup is already materialized, per-official, and correctly maintained. Prefer the latter: a denormalized copy of a materialized aggregate is a second source of truth with no benefit. Cross-ref [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-702]], [[FIX-726]], [[FIX-836]], [[FIX-675]], [[FIX-219]]. <!--id:FIX-942-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Prod financial-entity industry tags truncated and ALL curated overrides missing after a cancelled nightly** — **Live prod data regression, found while gating the [[FIX-921]] prod close-out.** Prod donor-side industry tags have been half-applied since **2026-07-30 05:30 UTC**. Measured 2026-07-31 00:40 UTC against prod, with local (healthy, post-[[FIX-921]]) as the control: local carries **40,148 `rule` + 425 `curated`** industry rows, prod carries **24,000 `rule` + ZERO `curated`**. All **792** override donors currently carry **no industry tag at all**, and `utilities` / `manufacturing` / `mining` / `media` are **absent from the prod donor side entirely** — those four reach `entity_tags` ONLY via curated overrides, which is what `financial_entity_industry_overrides` exists for. **Cause:** the nightly `enrichment-tail-phase` job (GHA run `30515825656`) was **cancelled** at 05:30:11 while `tag_rules` was mid-upsert — not a timeout, that job budgets 90 min. The industry write path in `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts` (~line 1424) is `clear_financial_entity_rule_tags(['industry'])` → `clearCuratedIndustryTags()` → **one batched `upsertTags()`** of the whole recomputed set, and `applyIndustryOverrides()` **appends the 425 curated rows to the END of that array** — so a kill part-way through the upsert lands the keyword/NAICS head and drops the curated tail while **both DELETEs have already committed**. The timestamps pin it: last `rule` tag written 05:30:07.042, `nightly_killed` row completes 05:30:07.984. `tag_rules` was left `status='running'` and later reaped as `reaped_orphan`. **Blast radius — already propagating.** The [[FIX-704]]/[[FIX-832]] incremental sector-affinity path re-buckets from live tags, so the 1,128 officials it refreshed on 07-30 lost their curated-derived buckets: officials-side `utilities` **833→595**, `manufacturing` **862→611**, `media` **660→468**, `mining` **492→343** against the [[FIX-916]] close-out baseline. Confirmed by freshness band — **every** surviving new-key rollup row sits in the 07-29 band, **zero** in the 07-30 band. Each further incremental erodes more. **Why it blocks [[FIX-921]]:** a full `backfill_official_sector_affinity_rollup()` in this state would rebuild every official from the truncated tag set and take those four buckets to **zero platform-wide**, while C2 of `scripts/verify-fix916-closeout.sql` would report **PASS** (a full backfill did run, and it ran after the last `tag_rules`). That is precisely the fresh-but-wrong trap the gate was written to prevent, in a worse form than anticipated — C3 is what actually caught it (0 / 425 / 0 / 367 / 0 against an expected 425 / 0 / 0 / 367 / 0). **Repair — no data migration needed:** one green `tag_rules` run rewrites the entire industry category from scratch (clear + rebuild), restoring all 425 curated rows; **then** run the full sector-affinity backfill, which now has more to fix than it did before. Gate on C3 = 425 / 0 / 0 / 367 / 0 first. Last known-good `tag_rules` was 2026-07-29 05:28→05:31 UTC (64,407 rows); **both** nightlies since were cancelled (07-30 05:09 scheduled, 07-30 13:21 dispatch). **Hardening candidate, separable:** make the industry rebuild atomic, or write the curated rows in their own statement **before** the bulk keyword/NAICS upsert, so an interrupted run can never strand the DELETEs without their INSERTs. Related: [[FIX-290]] added the mark-killed observability row but it does not cover this partial-write shape, and post-[[FIX-920]] fail-open behaviour means a partial industry pass no longer announces itself loudly. Cross-ref [[FIX-921]], [[FIX-916]], [[FIX-920]], [[FIX-911]], [[FIX-704]], [[FIX-832]], [[FIX-290]]. <!--id:FIX-945-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Rule-tagger clears at tagProposals and tagOfficials are non-atomic and swallow clear errors** — Sibling exposure to [[FIX-945]], which landed on the financial-entity path only because that is where the cancelled nightly happened to fall. All three rule taggers in `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts` rebuild their category as clear-then-upsert with NO transaction — `bulkUpsert()` in `lib/direct-pg-upsert.ts` autocommits per chunk and the clears run in their own separate transactions first, so the destructive window is exactly as long as the upsert on every one of them. `tagProposals` (~781, ~16.5k rows) and `tagOfficials` (~1099, ~7.1k rows) carry a SECOND, independent defect the financial-entity site does not: their clears are PostgREST `.delete()` calls whose error is `console.error`'d and then execution continues into the upsert — writing the fresh set on top of a table that was never cleared, which is the exact stale-table failure mode [[FIX-443]]'s ordering discipline was written to prevent, never applied here. (`tagFinancialEntities` clears via `callWithRetry`, which throws.) Fix: each tagger opens one `withDirectClient` transaction around clear + upsert, with the clears moved to direct-pg inside it — that buys atomicity AND makes a failed clear throw rather than return `{error}`. `bulkUpsert` itself must stay autocommit-per-chunk: [[FIX-754]]'s `startRowOffset` resume premise is that chunks commit independently, so wrapping the helper would silently break FEC-indiv and LittleSis resumability — the transaction belongs at the call site. NOT in scope: adding a `tag_category` scope to `tagOfficials`' DELETE — it is unscoped deliberately per [[FIX-897]] so no other job can write official industry rows unnoticed. Cross-ref [[FIX-945]], [[FIX-443]], [[FIX-754]], [[FIX-897]], [[FIX-651]]. <!--id:FIX-949-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **No FEC cycle-2020/2022 candidate rows exist — the FIX-934 DIVERTED branch is unresolvable as specified** — Surfaced by the [[FIX-934]] phase-1 manifest (`docs/audits/2026-08-01-fix934-cross-person-manifest-local.md`, `pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:audit:cross-person`). **Measured, platform-wide: NO `tier='candidate'` officials row holds a single cycle-2020 or cycle-2022 `financial_relationships` row.** Candidate rows carry 2024 (1,915 rows / 931,178 FR) and 2026 (2,205 / 435,842) money only, because the `cn{yy}` candidate-master stage (FIX-246) was only ever ingested for cn24/cn26. Independently, cycles 2020 and 2022 hold **zero** `fec_bulk_indiv` rows platform-wide (85,610 and 92,886 rows respectively, 100% PAC) — the `indiv{yy}` stage never ran for them either. **Consequence for [[FIX-934]]:** a 2020 or 2022 row sitting on a mis-bound official can NEVER have a same-surname FEC-bound counterpart, so it falls into the 'held by nobody / DIVERTED' bucket **by construction, regardless of whose money it actually is**. That bucket is $10,239,256 across the 59-official cross-person manifest and is **99.8% cycles 2020+2022 and 99.6% PAC rows** (2020 $4,936,977 / 1,506 rows; 2022 $5,285,529 / 1,429; 2024 $5,450 / 8; 2026 $11,300 / 3). It is therefore NOT evidence of diversion — it is an artifact of cycle coverage, and the $0-candidate-row signal [[FIX-934]] was specified around cannot identify its owner. Verified heterogeneous by inspection: Al Green's 2020/2022 PAC money is mostly his own (Realtors, Machinists, CWA, UNITE HERE, ILA — standard senior-House-Democrat money) but includes FREEDOM CAUCUS FUND and SEAL PAC, which are **Mark Green's** (R-TN); Shontel M. Brown's 2022 slice (plumbers, AFT, Huntington Bancshares, Rocket PAC) is unambiguously her own OH-11 money; David Porter's 2020/2022 ($1,673,007, JStreetPAC / PAC to the Future / Democrats of Orange County) is Katie Porter's HOUSE money, whose row `H8CA45130` sits at $0. Nothing in `financial_relationships` records which CAND_ID a row was written for — measured metadata keys across the whole fec_bulk donation population are exactly `source`, `tx_count`, `aggregated`, `donor_fingerprint`, `fec_committee_id` — so the owner is not recoverable from the money. **Two separable pieces:** (1) **backfill** cn20/cn22 (tiny, ~10k rows each) and optionally indiv20/indiv22, which would materialise the counterpart rows and let the existing collision test decide these rows the same way it decides 2024/2026; (2) until then, [[FIX-934]] phase 2 must leave the DIVERTED bucket **untouched** — it can neither delete it (only copy) nor move it (owner unknown). Note the backfill re-runs the FIX-929-gated matcher, so it will not re-create the mis-bindings. Cross-ref [[FIX-934]], [[FIX-930]], [[FIX-935]], [[FIX-937]], [[FIX-246]], [[FIX-929]]. <!--id:FIX-952-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Officials with a LEGITIMATE FEC binding can still hold another person's money — FIX-930's predicate cannot see them (Shontel Brown, $48.2M)** — Surfaced while running [[FIX-934]] phase 2 after the [[FIX-952]] cn20/cn22 backfill. **Shontel M. Brown (`f29bbd4e-944f-4840-adbd-16a4706a3c02`, Representative OH-11) still holds 43,001 rows / $48,198,974 that Sherrod Brown's `S6OH00163` row also holds — 97.8% of everything on her page — and she is now INVISIBLE to the [[FIX-930]] audit.** Cause: the backfill's `persistNewFecIds` correctly wrote her own `fec_candidate_id: H2OH11169`, and [[FIX-930]]'s suspect predicate begins `AND o.source_ids->>'fec_candidate_id' IS NULL`. Writing the missing id is the [[FIX-935]] remediation and is *right*; the side effect is that it removes the official from the contamination audit while the contamination is untouched. So the audit's population is not 'officials holding someone else's money', it is 'officials holding someone else's money **who also have no FEC binding**' — and the second clause is doing unintended work. **Do NOT fix this by widening the predicate to all fec_bulk holders.** Measured on the post-backfill local clone: that yields 1,023 officials / $1,071,474,470 at >=50% overlap, and applying the [[FIX-934]] name+seat CROSS test still leaves 497 officials / $288,957,409 (361 / $219,207,435 of them carrying a `fec_candidate_id`). That set is unusable as a remediation manifest for two proven reasons: (1) **the overlap relation is symmetric and carries no direction** — the same query flags Sherrod Brown against Shontel, and flags **Marjorie Greene** (the rightful holder of `H0GA06192`) against Richard Greene (the Seattle council member who is actually mis-bound); acting on it would delete the true owner's money half the time. (2) **the seat signal collapses when both parties hold the same chamber+state**, so Robert Garcia (Rep, CA-42) vs Mike Garcia (`H0CA25105`, Rep, CA-27) scores SAME and two genuinely different people are merged by the classifier. [[FIX-930]]'s orphan predicate is what supplies the missing asymmetry — it identifies which side of a symmetric overlap the matcher would never legitimately have selected — so the real work here is **deriving a direction signal that survives both parties having a valid binding**, not relaxing a filter. Candidate signals to evaluate: per-row `created_at`/`updated_at` (the mis-binding predates the corrected one), whether the CAND_ID whose seat the row's cycle belongs to is held by the other party, and the ratio of a holder's overlapped rows to their own independently-bound rows (Shontel: $48.2M overlapped vs $2.26M via her own `H2OH11169`). Note the same-CAND_ID duplicate claim is also live here — Shontel and `M Brown` now BOTH carry `H2OH11169` ([[FIX-941]]), and that pair is [[FIX-953]]. Until this is designed, Shontel M. Brown's page renders $50.9M of which $48.2M is Sherrod Brown's. Cross-ref [[FIX-934]], [[FIX-930]], [[FIX-952]], [[FIX-953]], [[FIX-935]], [[FIX-941]], [[FIX-929]]. <!--id:FIX-954-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Every FIX-933 merge is UNDONE by the next FEC run — the pipeline is blind to merged_fec_candidate_id ($309M re-split on local)** — **Proven on the local clone 2026-08-01 while running the [[FIX-952]] cycle backfill; this is a general defect, not a one-off.** [[FIX-933]] neutralises a same-person duplicate by moving the candidate row's `fec_candidate_id` to `merged_fec_candidate_id` — 'provenance kept, merge reversible, and the key the read path looks at is unambiguous again'. **Nothing in the FEC pipeline knows that marker exists:** `grep -rn 'merged_fec_candidate_id' packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/` returns ZERO hits. So on the next run, `matchRow` re-matches the retired candidate row by NAME — and the [[FIX-929]] first-name gate correctly *passes*, because it genuinely is the same person — and `persistNewFecIds` (writer.ts:953) writes the claim straight back with an unconditional `source_ids || jsonb_build_object(...)`. The money then re-splits across the elected/candidate pair exactly as before the merge. **Measured after one `FEC_CYCLES=2020,2022` pass on a clone where [[FIX-933]] had already been applied: 76 candidate rows carrying BOTH `merged_fec_candidate_id` AND a freshly re-written `fec_candidate_id`, all 76 holding money again; 95 candidate rows / 30,622 rows / $309,080,435 of NEWLY double-counted money that an elected row already held.** Reference case: `Steny Hoyer` (candidate, `H2MD05155`, `merged_fec_candidate_id` set by [[FIX-933]]) and `Steny H. Hoyer` (elected, same CAND_ID) each hold an identical 510 rows for 2020 and 404 for 2022; the candidate row's copies are stamped with the backfill run, the elected row's with the prior run. **[[FIX-933]]'s own self-heal cannot recover this**: its step-0 `reconcileSql` only re-retires claims on candidate rows holding NO financial_relationships, and deliberately refuses pairs where the candidate still holds money — which is precisely the state this bug produces. **Consequence for the roadmap: [[FIX-933]]'s prod half is still PENDING, and applying it to prod without this fix buys one week — the next Sunday FEC run re-splits it.** Fix has two parts, both needed: (1) `buildMatchIndex`/`matchRow` must REFUSE any officials row carrying `merged_fec_candidate_id` for that CAND_ID, so the retired row is never selected again; (2) `persistNewFecIds` must not write `fec_candidate_id` onto a row whose `merged_fec_candidate_id` equals the id being written. Add a regression test alongside `match-row.test.ts` asserting a retired row stays unmatched. Local currently carries the $309M re-split and needs a repair pass after the code fix — note the re-split pairs are NOT findable via the [[FIX-930]] suspect predicate (the elected row now carries a valid `fec_candidate_id`), the same blind spot as [[FIX-954]]. Cross-ref [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-952]], [[FIX-954]], [[FIX-929]], [[FIX-941]], [[FIX-939]]. <!--id:FIX-955-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **fec-bulk per-cycle weball name-fallback re-monies retired FIX-933 stubs — $132.9M duplicated on prod** — Found by the prompt-39 section-3 preflight (2026-08-02), which STOPPED the FIX-952/934/954 prod run before any write. The Sunday 2026-08-02 prod fec_bulk — the FIRST weekly run after the FIX-933 merge retired ids on prod (~07-30) — wrote $132,878,137 / 32,988 duplicate donation rows onto 79 retired merge-stubs (Guthrie $5.5M, Speaker Johnson $4.9M, Emmer $4.8M, Jeffries $4.7M, Schumer, Cruz, Warner, Hoyer…), split $72.6M cycle-2024 / $60.3M cycle-2026, all created_at in the 05:39–05:58 UTC fec window. Every row has a (from_id, cycle_year) counterpart on the elected survivor (32,987 of 32,988 amount-identical) — money was DUPLICATED, not moved; survivors intact. MECHANISM: the per-cycle weball name-fallback loop (packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/index.ts ~1084–1118, added 2026-04-30 e9829432 for multi-cycle) selects noFecIdOfficials on '!fec_candidate_id && !fec_id' only — it never consults merged_fec_candidate_id, so the [[FIX-955]] guard (df3edc70) covers matchRow/buildMatchIndex/persistNewFecIds but NOT this second fallback path; the loop then does an UNCONDITIONAL index.byFecId.set(candId, official.id) that steals CAND_IDs already correctly matched byFecId to the elected survivor (79 stolen in cycle 2024 = exactly the 'Name fallback matched: 79' log line, +3 in 2026). persistNewFecIds' SQL guard then silently refused re-stamping the retired id — so the stubs hold money WITHOUT a live id (the inverse of local's both-markers signature; detection queries keying on both-ids miss this class). Side class: Banks/Budd/Cotton 'Candidate for Senator' stubs (retired SENATE ids) name-matched their old HOUSE ids (stamped, since stamped≠retired passes the SQL guard) and took duplicate copies of the House-stub money (+$451,756 / 107 rows) — now BOTH stubs claim the same House CAND_ID; grows the [[FIX-956]] class. Ossoff's retired stub took $970,595/170 of S8GA00180 money (included in the $132.9M) with no stamp. THE FIX: (a) exclude officials with merged_fec_candidate_id from noFecIdOfficials (or consult hasRetiredClaim per weball row), (b) make the fallback's byFecId.set non-clobbering — never overwrite an existing byFecId mapping, (c) regression tests for the per-cycle loop mirroring the FIX-955 matchRow set. THE REPAIR (prod, after the code fix ships): data:merge:official-dupes:repair:prod re-merge of the 79 + the 3 Senate-stub House-id copies — Craig's decision per prompt-39 decision 2, NOT improvised. BLOCKS the prompt-39 prod sequence (FIX-952 backfill would pile 2020/2022 House-era money onto the same stubs). Until repaired, prod platform totals are inflated ~$133.3M and next Sunday's run re-lands the dupes even after a bare data repair — code fix must ship first. <!--id:FIX-960-->
- [ ] 🟠 M — **Eight incremental rollups key their watermark off updated_at columns stamped by NOW() in a BEFORE trigger — rows written by a transaction that starts before and commits after a watermark read are excluded permanently** — **Found by the 2026-08-07 site-wide efficiency audit (`docs/audits/2026-08-07-efficiency-audit.md`), class 5 — lying timestamps (population 153 routines).** Unlike the rest of class 5 this is a **correctness** defect, not an observability one: it silently drops rows from incremental rollups. **The mechanism.** `financial_relationships.updated_at` and `votes.updated_at` are stamped by a `BEFORE` trigger (`financial_relationships_updated_at` → `public.set_updated_at()`) using `NOW()`, which is `transaction_timestamp()` — **the moment the writing transaction BEGAN**, not the moment it committed. An incremental rollup then reads `WHERE updated_at > last_watermark` and advances the watermark to the read time. Any transaction that **began before** the watermark read but **committed after** it stamps its rows with a timestamp *below* the new watermark while being invisible at read time — so those rows are never picked up by that run, and never by any later run either, because the watermark has already moved past them. **The loss is permanent and silent.** **Eight watermark read sites, enumerated from live bodies:** `donor_rollup_rebuild_bulk:101,127`; `rebuild_entity_connections_donations:26,28`; `rebuild_entity_connections_votes:23,27`; `refresh_donor_party_rollup_incremental:54,64`; `refresh_donor_party_rollup_mv:16,25`; plus `refresh_financial_entity_totals_incremental`. **Exposure window = the duration of the longest concurrent writing transaction on that table.** That is not small here: the FEC bulk pipeline's `INSERT INTO financial_relationships` measured `calls=1549, mean_exec_time=29,436 ms` on prod `pg_stat_statements` — a **~29-second mean per statement**, inside longer transactions — and the [[FIX-933]] money move ran ~12 minutes atomic. So a rollup firing during an FEC load can miss any row whose transaction spans the read. **Why it has not obviously bitten yet:** most of these rollups are also re-derived by periodic full passes, which paper over the gap — which is exactly the [[FIX-968]] shape where a frozen rollup makes deltas look clean while the absolute is wrong (playbook **E1**). **Fix shape:** the standard remedies, cheapest first — (a) subtract a safety lag from the watermark (`watermark - interval '1 hour'`), trading a little re-work for correctness, which is the smallest change and fits every one of the 8 sites; (b) stamp `updated_at` with `clock_timestamp()` so the value at least reflects statement time rather than transaction start — necessary but **not sufficient**, since the commit still lands later; (c) the rigorous fix is to advance the watermark to `min(backend_xmin)`-safe territory rather than to wall-clock, i.e. key the incremental read off a transaction-id horizon rather than a timestamp. **Verify before choosing:** confirm against a known-count run whether rows are actually being dropped today (playbook E2 — validate the sizing metric against a run whose true count is independently known), because the safety-lag fix costs re-work on every run and should be justified by a measurement rather than by the argument alone. Cross-ref [[FIX-972]], [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-968]], [[FIX-974]], [[FIX-944]], [[FIX-702]], [[FIX-726]]. <!--id:FIX-983-->
- [ ] 🟠 S — **Nine unscoped DELETEs rebuild from relationship types that have zero rows — the lobbying arm deletes all 10,431 live edges and reinserts nothing, and a search predicate matches a value the CHECK domain forbids** — **Found by the 2026-08-07 site-wide efficiency audit (`docs/audits/2026-08-07-efficiency-audit.md`), class 7 — equal-by-coincidence predicates; SURVIVED adversarial verification with every number re-derived.** Enumerated by mechanism: all **88** live `refresh_|rebuild_|backfill_|reconcile_|promote_|merge_` bodies, **274 predicate sites**, each compared against its column's actual value domain. **(1) Delete-then-rebuild-from-nothing.** `rebuild_entity_connections_lobbying` DELETEs by `connection_type` alone — **10,431 live lobbying edges, 100% of them `evidence_source='external_relationships'`** — then reinserts `WHERE relationship_type = 'lobbying_spend'`. An **exact count** (not an EXISTS probe) over `financial_relationships` returns **0 rows** for `lobbying_spend`, and likewise 0 for `gift`, `honorarium`, `owns_stock`, `owns_bond`, `property` — **6 of 13 enum labels are empty**, so `_lobbying`, `_gifts` and `_holds` all delete real edges and insert exactly nothing. The edges exist today only because a *later* arm in the same array happens to restore them from `external_relationships` (603,745 rows, behind that arm's own 15-min `statement_timeout`). **This is not theoretical — the precedent is realized:** `cron.job_run_details` jobid 22 runid 156 **died at 6h00m** on an FR `WITH agg` cancel on 2026-08-03, and the last successful EC rebuild was **2026-07-29**. If a run dies between the DELETE and the restoring arm, the lobbying edges are simply gone until the next successful full rebuild — which has not happened in over a week. The graph renders `entity_connections`. **(2) A predicate matching a value the CHECK forbids — the [[FIX-073]] `'not_voting'` trap exactly.** `rebuild_entity_search_index`'s contract/grant money branch is gated on `f.entity_type IN ('corporation','organization')`, but `financial_entities_entity_type_check` permits exactly `individual, pac, super_pac, corporation, union, party_committee, small_donor_aggregate, tribal, 527, nonprofit, other` — **`'organization'` is absent from the domain, and a `GROUP BY` over all 3.64M `financial_entities` returns zero rows carrying it.** The literal appears in **both** occurrences in the live body (the `amount_cents` branch and the `amount_label` branch). Blast radius re-derived from the columns the function actually reads: **`entity_type='other'` 35 entities holding 679,195,200 cents** and **`entity_type='nonprofit'` 2 entities holding 638,587,500 cents** — all 37 with `total_ie=0` and `total_donated=0`, so all 37 render `amount_cents = 0` and `amount_label='donation'` in the search index. Real, permanent, silent, and small. (The 5 `super_pac` rows that do carry spend take the `ie>0` precedence branch and are unaffected.) **Fix shape:** (a) an arm must not DELETE what it cannot rebuild — either assert its source is non-empty before deleting, or scope the DELETE to the rows it actually owns (this is the [[FIX-808]]/[[FIX-692]] co-ownership rule applied to the EC arms); (b) fix the `'organization'` literal to `'other'`/`'nonprofit'` or drop the second arm, and add a **write-boundary vocab guard** the way [[FIX-889]]/[[FIX-891]] did for tag categories, so a predicate can never again name a value outside its own CHECK constraint — that guard is the mechanism-level fix and would have caught both this and FIX-073; (c) consider whether the six empty enum labels should be removed from the type or backfilled, since every predicate referencing them is dead code today. Cross-ref [[FIX-073]], [[FIX-889]], [[FIX-891]], [[FIX-808]], [[FIX-692]], [[FIX-969]], [[FIX-974]]. <!--id:FIX-985-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **merge-same-person-official-dupes runs phase 3 after phase 2 self-aborts on degraded I/O — caused the 2026-08-11 prod outage** — `runRollups()` catches its own budget/timeout abort, prints '✗ PHASE 2 ABORTED — … stopping before the remaining work makes it worse', and then **returns normally** (merge-same-person-official-dupes.ts:1302). `main()` unconditionally calls `runMvsAndVacuum(client)` immediately after (:1996), so the script proceeds to run the SIX materialized-view refreshes plus four VACUUMs — the heaviest I/O in the whole run — into an instance it has just diagnosed as I/O-degraded. Measured on the [[FIX-953]] prod apply 2026-08-10/11: `refresh_treemap_individuals_global()` hit its 2400s statement_timeout at chunk 53/64 and aborted phase 2 correctly at ~23:10 UTC; phase 3 then ran `refresh_official_sector_dollars_mv` 119.6s, `refresh_official_homepage_stats_mv` 326.2s, `refresh_homepage_stats_mv` 122.1s, `refresh_chord_industry_flows_mv` 205.1s — 25-70x their local timings (4.6s/4.7s/0.8s/20.3s) — and prod went fully unresponsive: pooler returning ECHECKOUTTIMEOUT, effectively every PostgREST GET returning Cloudflare 522 for 30+ minutes, requiring a manual project reset. The abort message is therefore actively misleading: it tells the operator the run stopped when it did not. Fix: make the phase-2 abort propagate (return a status from `runRollups` or rethrow) and SKIP phase 3, printing the `--mvs-only` resume hint instead. Note the MVs in `MV_REFRESH_FNS` are all also refreshed by the 06:00 UTC `refresh_derived_mvs('daily')` pg_cron job, so skipping them costs nothing — which is exactly why proceeding was pure downside. Cross-ref [[FIX-953]], [[FIX-1002]], [[FIX-943]]. <!--id:FIX-1017-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **The FEC role/prefix rule has three different spellings — loadOfficialsByFecIds' substring includes() is the loose one** — Noticed while shipping [[FIX-937]]'s pool exclusion. One rule — 'may this official hold an FEC House/Senate/President CAND_ID?' — is now implemented three ways in the same package: (1) `buildMatchIndex` pass 2 (fec-bulk/index.ts) uses EXACT `role_title === 'Senator'` / `=== 'Representative'`, with no President arm; (2) `loadOfficialsByFecIds` (fec-bulk/candidates.ts) uses SUBSTRING `role.includes('Senator')` / `includes('Representative')` / `includes('President')`; (3) [[FIX-937]]'s new `FEC_ELECTABLE_ROLE_TITLES` is an exact allow-list of seven strings. Where (1) and (3) disagree is benign — (1) is stricter and only gates a STORED id. Where (2) diverges is not: `'State Senator'.includes('Senator')` is true, so a state legislator carrying an `S*` `fec_id` would be honoured by the cn{yy} stage's existence check while both other paths refuse them. Clone-measured 2026-08-12: the exposure is LATENT, not live — zero `State Senator` / `State Representative` rows carry an `fec_id` today, and the 119 non-federal rows that do are all `Council Member`, which `includes()` correctly refuses anyway. Note also that (2) is the only path with a President arm, so it and (1) disagree about presidential rows in the opposite direction. Fix is to collapse all three onto the single exported `isFecElectableRole` predicate plus an office-prefix companion, so the read side and the write side cannot drift again — the same class of enumeration gap [[FIX-960]] was filed for. Cross-ref [[FIX-937]], [[FIX-941]], [[FIX-929]], [[FIX-246]]. <!--id:FIX-1025-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **pg_cron refresh_treemap_individuals_global() runs with NO effective statement_timeout — it took prod's REST API down for 56 minutes** — PROD INCIDENT 2026-08-18 09:45-10:41 UTC: the public PostgREST API returned 503 PGRST002 ("Could not query the database for the schema cache") for ~56 minutes. Root cause measured, not inferred: refresh_treemap_individuals_global() fired by pg_cron job26 at 08:15 and was still running at 10:02 — 1h47m54s — starving the box so badly that the catalog query PostgREST needs to build its schema cache timed out (measured: SELECT count(*) FROM pg_proc JOIN pg_namespace FAILED 57014 at 60,247ms; pg_timezone_names, another PostgREST startup query, took 24,788ms then later FAILED at 99,868ms). PostgREST cannot serve ANY request without that cache, so the whole data layer went down. THE MECHANISM: FIX-965 characterises this proc as unable to complete inside a 2400s budget, but that measurement was a MANUAL CALL with statement_timeout set in the session. On the pg_cron path there is no such session setting, and per the FIX-1056 finding a SET statement_timeout inside a CALLed procedure is DECORATIVE — statement_timeout is armed at CALL start and a nested function cannot re-arm it. So the scheduled firing is effectively UNBOUNDED: instead of failing at 40 minutes it runs until something else breaks. FIX-965 has been reasoning about a budget the scheduled job does not have. RESOLUTION APPLIED: pg_cancel_backend on the treemap pid (ran 1h47m54s) restored the catalog query from a 60s timeout to 28ms immediately; refresh_financial_entity_totals_incremental was also cancelled at 1h31m58s; PostgREST did not self-recover so the project was restarted via the Management API and REST returned 200 in 0.12-0.25s. FIX SHAPE: set a hard statement_timeout on the pg_cron job COMMAND itself (the only layer that can arm it), e.g. schedule the job as SET statement_timeout=1800000; CALL ... — or wrap in a SECURITY DEFINER function whose ALTER FUNCTION SET statement_timeout applies at the outermost call. Audit every other CALL-based pg_cron job for the same gap; jobs 17/24/25/26/28 are all CALL-shaped. Cross-ref FIX-965, FIX-1056, FIX-943, FIX-1030, FIX-961. <!--id:FIX-1063-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **audit-fec-orphan-attribution reference-case guard is mis-specified for a legitimate official — exits non-zero on a CLEAN signal** — The Shontel Brown reference case in packages/data/src/scripts/audit-fec-orphan-attribution.ts now fails, and the failure is in the GUARD, not the data. Measured on prod 2026-08-18: the substantive signal is CLEAN — CROSS-PERSON MISATTRIBUTION 0 suspects / $0, confirmed independently by audit-cross-person-misattribution.ts which also reports 0 — but the script prints "A reference case is missing or landed in the wrong branch — the signal is wrong" and exits non-zero. MECHANISM: the guard accepts a reference case being ABSENT from the suspect population only with positive remediation evidence, and for a "delete" remediation (FIX-934) it demands !holds_fec_money AND !has_cand_id, i.e. the official row must be completely empty. Shontel M. Brown (f29bbd4e-944f-4840-adbd-16a4706a3c02) is a REAL elected official who legitimately carries her own CAND_ID H2OH11169 and legitimately holds her own FEC money — $5.9M across cycles 2022/2024/2026 created between 05-03 and 08-17. FIX-934 deleted the rows where her official row was mis-bound to SHERROD Browns money; it did not and should not have emptied her row. So the delete-evidence predicate describes a phantom/duplicate row, not this one, and can never pass. NOT CAUSED BY THE FIX-961 BACKFILL: the 2026-08-18 cycles=2020 run contributed only 6 rows / $5,750 to her total. TIMELINE CONFIRMS IT IS LATENT SINCE THE REMEDIATION: docs/audits/2026-07-29, -07-30 and -07-31 all show the case landing IN the CROSS-PERSON branch with shared=42,681 and a green check; FIX-934 shipped 08-05 and deleted those rows; there was NO audit run between 08-05 and 08-18, so this is simply the first execution after the remediation. FIX SHAPE: the delete-evidence predicate must assert the absence of the MIS-BOUND money specifically (no fec_bulk rows carrying Sherrod Brown CAND_ID / no shared-donor overlap above the boundary), not the absence of all money. Cross-ref FIX-934, FIX-960, FIX-930, FIX-961. <!--id:FIX-1064-->
