## DB PERFORMANCE / COMPUTE
- [x] 🟡 M — **Prod statement_timeout audit + costed compute-tier upgrade memo** — Structural root of the whole timeout class. Audited prod 2026-06-14 (read-only psql, pg_roles.rolconfig): anon=3s, authenticated=8s, authenticator=8s(+lock 8s), service_role=none→inherits authenticator 8s, postgres-via-pooler=2min; shared_buffers=256MB, effective_cache_size=768MB, work_mem=3.4MB, max_connections=60. A role-level bump is UNSAFE (raising authenticator/service_role also raises request-path API routes using the admin client → slow queries hold buffers longer, worsening cache starvation — the [[FIX-505]] hazard) and wouldn't help the 100s-gateway class anyway. Direct-pg ([[FIX-586]]/[[FIX-587]]) is the durable per-pipeline fix. Remaining deliverable: a short costed memo — current Pro Small (256MB shared_buffers vs ~12.5GB top-3 working set, 2% buffer-hit on seq scans, see docs/audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md) → next compute tiers, shared_buffers/RAM each gives, expected effect on the timeout class, monthly $ delta. The only fix that addresses the cause vs routing around it. Cost decision is Craig's. <!--id:FIX-589-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Capture unmatched-target IE money (Schedule E completeness)** — FIX-673 measured that ~5.7% of valid IE $ (~$595M across 2020/22/24/26; 1-2% recent cycles, larger historically) is silently dropped because the target candidate (cand_id) isn't a matched Civitics official. Options to capture: (a) insert candidate officials rows for unmatched IE targets the way cn{yy} candidate-master does (tier='candidate', keyed on fec_candidate_id), then the IE writer resolves to_id; (b) write IE relationships with to_type=null/placeholder and surface as 'spending against unlinked candidate'. Most hidden $ is historical (2020/22 targets no longer active officials), so low priority. Verify via pnpm data:analyze:ie-drop top-10 dropped cand_ids. Surfaced by [[FIX-673]]. <!--id:FIX-674-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **get_quality_counts() runs 13.7s mean x70 via PostgREST from /api/claude/status — contention candidate, materialize only if confirmed** — pg_stat_statements (prod 2026-06-27) shows `get_quality_counts()` called **via PostgREST** (`WITH pgrst_source …`) from `apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts:429` — 70 calls, mean 13.7s, max 30s, ~964s total. This is the internal Claude status/admin endpoint, NOT a user-facing page, and the cron snapshot path (`status-snapshot.ts`) is protected by a 60s function timeout — so this is NOT a source of user-facing timeouts. BUT a 13.7s scan running concurrently with page loads on the IOWait-bound Small instance is a plausible *contention* contributor. Per the diagnostic brief: do NOT materialize blindly. ACTION: confirm whether `/api/claude/status` is being polled (if it's hit only on-demand by an operator, leave as-is). Only if it's a recurring concurrent load, materialize to a single-row snapshot refreshed by the existing cron, RPC reads snapshot with live-compute fallback (pattern in `packages/db/CLAUDE.md`). Lower priority than [[FIX-680]]/[[FIX-681]], which are the actual user-facing timeout source. <!--id:FIX-682-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **Skip query_districts geometry RPC for empty-leaf /districts/[id] (FIX-683 item 4 deferral)** — Deferred from [[FIX-683]]. FIX-683 shipped the heavy-RPC skip for empty /jurisdictions/[id] (cache-miss → base-row shell, no boundary read) and empty /officials/[id] (skip get_official_page), but NOT for /districts/[id]. The district page's heavy read is query_districts (the simplified-geometry PostGIS RPC — the 492MB geometry), and it runs in BOTH loadDistrict (page) AND generateMetadata. For an empty-leaf district (not in jurisdiction_page_cache — FIX-683 already noindexes these via lookupJurisdictionCache), skipping the geometry RPC and rendering a light 'no boundary data yet' shell would remove the last cold-read class. NOT done in FIX-683 because: (a) the boundary map is the district page's primary content even for districts with no cross-linked officials (the OpenStates name-match gap), so a product call is needed on whether empty-by-our-cache districts should still show their boundary; (b) generateMetadata currently calls loadDistrict (geometry + officials + parent) just to build a title — that should be slimmed to a cheap name fetch regardless. Scope: gate loadDistrict's query_districts call on lookupJurisdictionCache membership (fail-open), and replace district generateMetadata's loadDistrict call with a name-only fetch + the existing membership lookup. Verify: empty district renders shell + no query_districts in pg_stat_statements; content district unchanged. <!--id:FIX-684-->

- [x] 🔴 S — **entity_connections stranded at autovacuum_enabled=false on prod — 9.5M dead tuples, 0.9% all-visible, every index-only scan degraded to a full heap probe** — FIX-650's detector reports it right now: check_rebuild_autovacuum_status() returns stranded:[entity_connections], rebuild_active:false. pg_stat_user_tables on prod (2026-07-24): last_vacuum NULL, last_autovacuum NULL, vacuum_count 0, autovacuum_count 0, n_live_tup 5,218,696, n_dead_tup 9,505,759 — nearly 2x more dead than live. pg_class: relallvisible 3,608 of 395,252 pages = 0.9% all-visible, heap 3,088 MB / 6,517 MB total. reloptions carries autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor=0.05, autovacuum_analyze_scale_factor=0.02, autovacuum_enabled=false — the FIX-590 rebuild pause left stranded again, exactly the 2026-06-21 recurrence FIX-650 built the detector for (the detector fires; nothing acted on it). CONSEQUENCE: no index-only scan on entity_connections can ever stay index-only — the visibility map is empty, so every tuple needs a heap visibility check. Surfaced by FIX-883: its cohort aggregation reads entity_connections through the FIX-497 partial COVERING index and prod EXPLAIN ANALYZE shows Index Only Scan ... rows=34,552 / **Heap Fetches: 34,534** / Buffers read=33,053 = 20.5s of the query's 22.1s, against 500ms locally for the same row count (local VM is warm: 4,716 heap fetches of 34,285). This also means FIX-497's covering-index design has never delivered its intended plan on prod. FIX-883's own financial_entities probe index is unaffected (that table is 98.1% all-visible) and dropped its share from 55.6s to sub-100ms, proving the remaining cost is entirely the visibility map. Fix: re-enable autovacuum on entity_connections and run one VACUUM (not FULL) to build the visibility map; then verify relallvisible approaches relpages and re-measure. Heavy prod I/O — schedule off-peak, outside the Sun/Wed 08:00 UTC rebuild window. Durable follow-up: make the rebuild's re-enable path crash-safe (the GHA 4h SIGKILL skips the finally) or have the canary escalate rather than just report. Cross-refs [[FIX-590]] [[FIX-591]] [[FIX-650]] [[FIX-497]] [[FIX-883]]. <!--id:FIX-884-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Make the entity_connections rebuild's autovacuum re-enable crash-safe, and have the canary escalate instead of just reporting** — FIX-884 cleared the stranded state on prod (autovacuum re-enabled, one VACUUM: 9,505,759 dead tuples to 0, all-visible 0.9% to 100.0%, 2m54s) but did NOT fix the mechanism that stranded it. This is the SECOND recurrence: FIX-590 pauses autovacuum on entity_connections for the rebuild's duration and re-enables it in a finally plus a startup reconcile (FIX-591), but a GHA SIGKILL at the 4h cap skips the finally, and the manual 2b recovery path (run-rebuild-chunks-prod.ts) historically never managed the flag. It stranded on 2026-06-21 (that IS FIX-650) and again by 2026-07-24, undetected for an unknown window. FIX-650 built check_rebuild_autovacuum_status() for the daily canary to catch exactly this, and the detector WAS returning stranded:[entity_connections] when FIX-883 stumbled onto it — so detection works and escalation does not. Two pieces of work: (1) make the re-enable crash-safe rather than finally-dependent — e.g. have the rebuild record an intent row and reconcile it unconditionally at every startup regardless of how the prior run died, and make run-rebuild-chunks-prod.ts manage the flag on the same contract; (2) make the canary FAIL (non-zero exit / alert) on a non-empty stranded[] with no rebuild_active, instead of reporting into output nobody reads. Consider also a cheap standing assertion on relallvisible/relpages for entity_connections, since the visibility-map collapse — not the dead-tuple count — is what actually degrades every index-only scan on the table (FIX-497's covering index and FIX-883's cohort aggregation both silently lost their intended plans). Cross-refs [[FIX-590]] [[FIX-591]] [[FIX-650]] [[FIX-884]] [[FIX-497]] [[FIX-883]]. <!--id:FIX-885-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Hot-path tables accumulate dead tuples below their autovacuum trigger — six untuned tables at 42.7–85.4% all-visible** — **Found while preparing the [[FIX-933]] prod run.** **⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-08-01 — the original bullet's central number was wrong; see below.** Originally filed as "`financial_relationships` carries 411,555 dead tuples against a **1,663,805** threshold, so autovacuum mathematically never fires". **That arithmetic used the CLUSTER default scale factor (0.2), but `financial_relationships` already carries a per-table override of 0.05** (verified on prod AND local, 2026-08-01 — alongside `entity_connections`, `entity_tags`, `enrichment_queue`, presumably from the FIX-331/[[FIX-884]] work). Its real trigger is `50 + 0.05 × 8,208,155` = **410,458**, so the 411,555 dead tuples measured on 07-30 were ~1,100 PAST the line, not 1.25M short of it — and prod confirms autovacuum did fire (`last_autovacuum` 2026-07-30 05:28, `autovacuum_count` 7). The table was never in a "never fires" state. **The real defect is the one the wrong number pointed at anyway, and it is a CLASS, not this table:** the trigger is a *floor of tolerated bloat*, and a heap page loses its all-visible mark if ANY tuple on it is dead — these tables carry 4.6–60 tuples per page, so a few-percent dead ratio un-marks most of the heap and every index-only scan degrades to a per-row heap fetch. Sorting every large prod table by visibility map settles it: **`donor_party_rollup_mv` 42.7%, `votes` 61.1%, `external_source_refs` 63.8%, `external_relationships_review_queue` 65.0%, `financial_entities` 77.3%, `proposals` 85.4%, `external_relationships` 97.3% — every one untuned — against `financial_relationships`, `entity_connections`, `entity_tags` and `enrichment_queue` all at 100.0%, every one tuned.** The correlation is total: the tuning works and was simply never extended past the tables [[FIX-884]] happened to touch. Two tables are textbook near-misses sitting *just* under their line (`donor_party_rollup_mv` 261,534 dead vs 262,622; `proposals` 16,145 vs 17,323) and two have **never been autovacuumed in their lives** (`votes` and `external_source_refs`, `autovacuum_count` = 0 — autoANALYZE runs because its trigger is 0.1, half the vacuum trigger, so they cross the analyze line forever and the vacuum line never). The one that matters most is **`financial_entities`**: 13.12% dead, 77.3% all-visible, no custom setting at all, 2.9M rows across 19 indexes — and it is the large table in the donor-rollup join path that dominates that job's runtime today (`financial_entities_pkey`, 11,780 ms of arm 1b). **This is NOT [[FIX-884]]**, which was `entity_connections` stranded at `autovacuum_enabled=false` by the rebuild (an explicitly-disabled flag, since fixed); here autovacuum is enabled and simply never fires. Same *consequence* as FIX-884 though — a stale visibility map kills index-only scans, which is how FIX-884's 0.9% all-visible turned a clean plan into 34,534 heap fetches. Measured impact on the local prod-clone: the [[FIX-930]] audit's suspect query went from completing in ~8 min to **blowing its 600s `statement_timeout`** purely from ~260k additional dead tuples left by the [[FIX-933]] merge, and completed again after a VACUUM. So this already degrades a read path today at 411k, before any migration adds to it. Fix (shipped, `20260801000000_fix943_autovacuum_hot_path_tables.sql`): extend the per-table scale factors to the hot-path class, **sized per table from its measured write pattern and index footprint rather than a blanket value** — `financial_relationships` 0.05→0.02 (410,458→**164,213**), `financial_entities` →0.05 (582,987→**145,784**), `votes` →0.05 (193,681→**48,458**), `donor_party_rollup_mv` →0.02 (262,622→**26,307**), `external_source_refs` →0.05 (114,191→**28,585**), `proposals` →0.05 (17,323→**4,368**). Note the original bullet's `autovacuum_vacuum_threshold = 50000` suggestion is a **no-op for this purpose** — the formula is `threshold + scale_factor × reltuples`, i.e. additive, so the threshold only raises the trigger and only matters on small tables; lowering the *scale factor* is the only lever on a large one. `entity_connections` is deliberately **left at 0.05** (not 1.07M — it too is already tuned, real trigger 259,648): 2,919 MB heap / 3,544 MB across 10 indexes means every extra pass is real standing I/O on an already-I/O-bound Small instance, and it has both the monthly `ec-vacuum-analyze` backstop and the [[FIX-885]] startup reconcile. Five self-maintaining rollups (`entity_connection_stats_mv`, `entity_search_index`, `group_donor_rollup`, `official_donor_rollup_mv`, `treemap_individuals_rollup`) are untuned yet all at 100.0% all-visible — their rebuilds delete millions of rows and clear even the 0.2 trigger every run — so they are left alone, as is pipeline-only `external_relationships_review_queue`. Note Pro Small has limited I/O and a VACUUM on this table took **>17 min on local Docker**, so tune the thresholds rather than scheduling blanket full-table VACUUMs, and prefer off-peak (cf. the no-heavy-prod-ops-during-active-hours rule). [[FIX-933]]'s script ends with an explicit `VACUUM (ANALYZE)` on `financial_relationships`/`entity_connections`/`officials` — filed here as "a workaround local to one script, not the fix", and that framing was **too modest**: autovacuum tuning narrows the window but cannot close it (a bulk rewrite lands its whole dead-tuple load inside the remaining floor at once), and that vacuum running on 07-31 02:35 is the *only* reason [[FIX-944]]'s four-day backlog cleared. So it is now a **standing convention, not a workaround** — "any script that bulk-rewrites a table ends by vacuuming what it rewrote", landed in root `CLAUDE.md` (Data-state changes) with the reference table and sizing rules in `packages/db/CLAUDE.md`. **[[FIX-934]] is the next bulk `financial_relationships` rewrite and must carry a vacuum tail regardless of these settings.** Also shipped: `check_rebuild_autovacuum_status()` ([[FIX-650]]/[[FIX-885]]) now watches the whole tuned class instead of only `entity_connections` — which is how `donor_party_rollup_mv` sat at 42.7%, *below the canary's own 50% escalation floor*, completely unseen — and gains `bloat`/`bloat_degraded` keys reporting dead tuples against each table's computed trigger. `bloat_degraded` is **report-only by design**: a table can sit under its trigger harmlessly, so `vm_degraded` remains the escalating signal and this exists to make the cause greppable before the consequence arrives. Cross-ref [[FIX-884]], [[FIX-650]], [[FIX-885]], [[FIX-590]], [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-934]], [[FIX-944]], [[FIX-702]], [[FIX-726]]. <!--id:FIX-943-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **donor_rollup_refresh has failed every day since 2026-07-27 — the whole per-official donor rollup family is stale on prod** — **Found while preparing the [[FIX-933]] prod run; pre-existing and unrelated to that merge.** The `donor-rollup-refresh` pg_cron job (`0 9 * * *`, `CALL refresh_official_donor_rollup_incremental()`) has **not completed successfully on prod since 2026-07-26**. Measured from `data_sync_log` on 2026-07-30: 07-22 complete in 1h54m, 07-23/24/25 complete in ~0.05-0.11s (empty dirty set), then **07-27 failed after 20h15m, 07-28 failed after 20h22m, 07-29 failed after 20h09m, 07-30 failed after 4h22m**. Every failed row carries `error_message='reaped_orphan'`, `rows_inserted=0`, and `metadata.dirty_recipients` pinned at **8,382-8,383 — the SAME count three days running**, which is the tell: the watermark never advances, so each run re-attempts an identical dirty set, runs for ~20 hours, and is reaped rather than finishing. The reap timing is not coincidental — 07-27/28/29 all have `completed_at` equal to the following nightly-sync's start (05:15, 05:22, 05:09), i.e. **the nightly's startup reaps the still-running rollup**, and today's was reaped at 13:22:16 when a manual nightly dispatch started at 13:21:52. So the job can never win: it needs longer than the gap to the next nightly. **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-06 from `cron.job_run_details` (authoritative; the durations above came from `data_sync_log` and are wrong): those four runs took 6h08m / 6h00m / 6h00m / 6h00m and all died at ~15:00 from the 6h `statement_timeout` on the `postgres` role — already dead ~14h before the nightly that supposedly reaped them. The "20h" figures are `started_at`→`reaped_at` gaps on stale `running` rows, i.e. bounded by when the REAPER ran, not when the work stopped (07-30's "4h22m" is wrong the other way, actual 6h00m). The nightly never killed this job and the "can never win vs the nightly gap" premise is falsified — as is the [[FIX-950]] framing that cites it as nightly-vs-session contention. The resumability FIX ITSELF was correct and worked (07-31: 8,381 recipients in 3h08m). Never quote a `reaped_orphan` span as a runtime. Numbers: `docs/audits/2026-08-06-fix968-cron-window-envelopes.md` §6b.** **Blast radius is the entire per-official money read path**, because `donor_rollup_rebuild_recipients()` is the single entry point that rebuilds `official_donor_rollup_mv`, `official_donor_totals`, `official_small_dollar_rollup`, `official_sector_affinity_rollup`, `treemap_individuals_rollup` AND `official_donor_bracket_totals` ([[FIX-836]], [[FIX-776]], [[FIX-777]], [[FIX-779]], [[FIX-868]]) — all six have been frozen for 4+ days. Corroborating measurement: `SUM(official_donor_totals.total_cents)` = **$4,963,134,492** against a live `financial_relationships` donation sum of **$5,052,387,823** — the materialized rollup is **$89.3M (1.8%) stale**, and that gap is what officials' pages, the treemap, the chord and the sector-affinity surfaces are all rendering. Two things to establish before fixing: (1) **why 8,382 dirty recipients now takes >20h when 07-22 did its work in 1h54m** — candidates are the [[FIX-943]] dead-tuple/visibility-map condition on `financial_relationships`, a per-chunk cost regression, or a genuinely larger per-recipient workload; get an EXPLAIN on one chunk before assuming. (2) **the reaper is masking the failure** — `reaped_orphan` is indistinguishable from a real error in `data_sync_log`, so 4 days of total failure looked like ordinary noise; the reaper should record that it killed a job that was still making progress, and the [[FIX-234]]-style canary should alert on `donor_rollup_refresh` never reaching `complete`, not just on `nightly_cron`. Likely fix shape mirrors [[FIX-702]]/[[FIX-726]]: the proc already commits per chunk, so it should be made **resumable across runs** — advance the watermark per successfully-committed chunk instead of only on a fully clean run, so a reaped run keeps its progress and the next run starts where it stopped rather than repeating all 8,382. Cross-ref [[FIX-836]], [[FIX-943]], [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-234]], [[FIX-702]], [[FIX-726]], [[FIX-942]]. <!--id:FIX-944-->
- [ ] 🟠 S — **officials.total_received_cents is a second source of truth for money the graph still reads — reconcile it against official_donor_totals or retire it** — **File only — surfaced while implementing [[FIX-940]]; the reported drift did NOT reproduce on the clone, so prod must be re-derived before anything is rebuilt.** `officials.total_received_cents` is a denormalized BIGINT column (migration `20260508000000_official_donation_totals.sql`) holding the same number `official_donor_totals.total_cents` now holds authoritatively (FIX-836). Two request-path graph routes still read the COLUMN, not the rollup: `/api/graph/treemap` (route.ts:408, and again at :513 where it seeds `totalByOfficial`) and `/api/graph/small-dollar` (route.ts:87 → :120, where it is the DENOMINATOR of the small-dollar share). So any drift renders as wrong money on two graph surfaces and as a wrong percentage on the third. **The structural hazard is in the refresh function, not the value:** `rebuild_official_donation_totals()` — the variant the FEC pipeline calls after each wave — is `UPDATE officials … FROM (SELECT … GROUP BY to_id) agg WHERE o.id = agg.official_id`, i.e. it only touches officials that still HAVE donation rows. An official whose `financial_relationships` rows were all deleted (exactly what [[FIX-933]]'s merge does to the neutralised duplicate) keeps its last non-zero value forever; only `rebuild_official_donation_totals_full()`, which zeroes first, can clear it, and nothing on the schedule calls `_full()`. **Measured on the local prod-clone 2026-07-31 (post-FIX-933): ZERO drift** — all 4,321 officials in `official_donor_totals` match the column exactly (Ossoff $15,633,810 both sides), and ZERO officials carry a non-zero column value with no surviving donation rows. The prompt that surfaced this reported Ossoff at $11,555,275 in the column against $15,633,810 in the rollup; that gap is not present locally, so **re-derive on prod first** — if prod also measures clean, the bullet collapses to the design question alone. That design question is the durable part and is worth answering either way: now that `official_donor_totals` exists, is refreshed daily and is authoritative, the column is a second source of truth for the same quantity with a refresh function that structurally cannot converge. Options: (a) point the two graph routes at `official_donor_totals` and leave the column as a legacy no-read; (b) swap the pipeline's post-wave call from the incremental to `_full()` so it can converge; (c) drop the column and its `officials_total_received_cents` index. Note (c) also touches `20260715000000_fix776_materialize_official_small_dollar_rollup.sql`, which references it. Cross-ref [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-836]], [[FIX-702]], [[FIX-776]], [[FIX-940]]. <!--id:FIX-947-->
- [ ] 🟡 M — **Senate roll-call XML carries lis_member_id — resolve Senators by stable id instead of lastname:state** — **File only — the gate result from [[FIX-940]]. Do NOT attempt the crosswalk ingest as part of that PR.** Senate LIS roll-call XML carries a **stable per-member identifier the writer has never read**. Verified 2026-07-31 against two live 119th-Congress rolls (`vote_119_1_00100.xml`, `vote_119_2_00010.xml`): every `<member>` block has exactly seven fields — `member_full`, `last_name`, `first_name`, `party`, `state`, `vote_cast`, and **`lis_member_id`** (e.g. `S428` for Alsobrooks, `S409` for Luján). `packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts` reads only `last_name`, `state` and `vote_cast`, which is why the Senate branch fell back to a name key at all — the House branch resolves by bioguide from `@_name-id` and is correct precisely because it has a stable id. [[FIX-940]] fixed the name path (tier filter + collision refusal + diacritic folding) but left it the PRIMARY key; this makes it the FALLBACK. **What it needs: a crosswalk.** `grep` confirms the repo stores no LIS id anywhere today — not on `officials.source_ids`, not in any migration. The `unitedstates/congress-legislators` dataset carries `id.lis` alongside `id.bioguide`, so the work is: ingest that mapping, persist it as `officials.source_ids->>'lis_member_id'` (same JSONB convention as `congress_gov`/`fec_id`), then key the Senate map on it with the name map retained for members the crosswalk misses. **Why it is worth doing even though [[FIX-940]] closed the live bug:** the name key is intrinsically ambiguous — two sitting Senators sharing `(surname, state)` is legal and [[FIX-940]]'s guard deliberately REFUSES that slot rather than guessing, which turns the ambiguity into dropped votes instead of misfiled ones. An id-keyed map has no such failure mode. It also removes the diacritic dependency [[FIX-940]] had to add: the XML spells every surname ASCII-only while `officials` stores "Luján", and he was the ONLY sitting Senator affected — a fragile one-of-one that a stable id makes moot. **Cheaper intermediate if the crosswalk is deferred:** the XML also carries `first_name`, which the writer ignores today; folding it into the key as a tie-breaker would disambiguate most same-surname/same-state collisions with no new data source, though it is still name-matching and inherits FEC-legal-name skew (Ted/Rafael Cruz, Jack/John Reed both appear in this exact dataset). Cross-ref [[FIX-940]], [[FIX-545]], [[FIX-837]]. <!--id:FIX-948-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Donor-rollup throughput numbers in FIX-944's migration comment and the sweep script are a measurement artifact (2.5x undercount)** — **Found while running the [[FIX-944]] per-arm regression diagnosis (2026-08-01).** The "~1,000 recipients per 6h window" figure that FIX-944's migration comment and `packages/data/src/scripts/donor-rollup-sweep.ts` both state as measured fact is a **measurement artifact**, and it is the number a future session would use to justify a 50-hour break-glass sweep that is not needed. The proxy was "count DISTINCT official_id in `official_small_dollar_rollup` / `official_donor_totals` with `updated_at` inside the run window". Both tables hold **4,336 rows total**, because only recipients that actually have donation rows get one — so the proxy can never report more than 4,336 no matter how many recipients a run processes, and it undercounts every partial run by the same unknown factor. Falsified against a run whose true count is known: the 2026-07-31 09:00 run (`cron.job_run_details` runid 135) processed **8,381 recipients in 42 chunks, status `complete`** — and the same proxy reports **3,339**, a 2.5x undercount on a fully successful run. The downstream claims are therefore all wrong: "a 6h window completes ~1,000", "8,382 needs ~50h of compute", "about 9 nightly runs". Corrected numbers, measured: 07-22 = 1,992 recipients / 10 chunks / 1h54m = **685 s/chunk**; 07-31 = 8,381 recipients / 42 chunks / 3h08m = **269 s/chunk**, i.e. the job is now **2.5x faster per chunk than the 07-22 baseline**, not 6-8x slower, and it clears the whole dirty set in **one 6h window with 2h52m to spare**. Two artifacts carry the bad numbers: `supabase/migrations/20260731000000_fix944_resumable_donor_rollup.sql` (the "Why it can never converge" and "Convergence" comment blocks) — **frozen, it is an applied migration and must not be edited** — and the sweep script's header comment, which is editable and should be corrected. Also worth correcting the procedure's `COMMENT ON` text in a future migration if one touches it anyway. **Lesson worth keeping**: when a proxy metric is used to size a multi-hour remediation, validate it against a run whose true count is independently known before quoting it as measured — a rollup table's row count is a ceiling on any "rows touched" proxy built from it. Cross-ref [[FIX-944]], [[FIX-943]], [[FIX-776]], [[FIX-836]]. <!--id:FIX-951-->
