- [x] 🟠 M — **Expand the donor-industry vocabulary to 16 keys; consolidate four duplicate label maps into one** — A full dollar-weighted audit of all twelve donor-industry tags (2026-07-27, read-only on the local prod-clone; docs/audits/) found **not one tag above 79% accuracy by dollars, seven below 50%, median 41%**. Root cause is NOT dirty keywords: ai-classifier.ts forced a **12-way choice made from the donor name alone**, and the vocabulary had no bucket at all for electric utilities, industrial manufacturing, chemicals, autos, steel, mining or media. A model asked to place *Duke Energy PAC* into those twelve answers oil_gas and is not wrong to — it is the nearest available label. That single gap is the largest error mass in eight of the twelve tags. **Adds four keys** (utilities, manufacturing, mining, media; VALID_INDUSTRIES 12 to 16) and **renames pharma to health** — a blanket key rename, correct for the unaudited tail too, because the entire keyword list feeding the tag is health-sector and the tag measured **20.4% pharmaceutical but 91.2% health-sector** (hospital/physician assocs 32.0%, health trade assocs 20.7%, health insurers 11.6%). manufacturing already existed as a 1-donor out-of-vocabulary tag (NAM-PAC) and is PROMOTED, not deleted. **oil_gas keeps its key AND stays narrow** — widening it to Energy and Utilities would take it 40.7% to 91.8% honest but would destroy the money_vote_influence HR 26 measure, which needs fossil-fuel money specifically; the fossil core is coherent (upstream 18.0% + refining 9.1% + pipeline 6.0% + trade assoc 3.7% + fuel marketing 2.8% + oilfield svcs 1.0%) and the honest fix is moving the utilities OUT. Labels widen where contents justify (tech to Technology and Communications, legal to Legal and Professional Services, retail to Consumer Goods and Services, real_estate to Real Estate and Construction, defense to Defense and Aerospace, agriculture to Agriculture and Food, lobby to Advocacy and Lobbying, finance to Finance and Insurance). **Consolidation:** INDUSTRY_LABELS was duplicated in four places (tags/rules.ts, tags/ai-classifier.ts — which additionally carried an 'other' key never in VALID_INDUSTRIES — enrichment/queue.ts INDUSTRY_DISPLAY, and app-side CANONICAL_INDUSTRY_TOKENS); all now resolve to ONE exported constant beside VALID_INDUSTRIES in tags/topics.ts, which is already the FIX-890 drain write-boundary source. UI keeps its own key-to-lucide registry (different type, no dep edge from apps/civitics to @civitics/data) mirrored by packages/graph/src/industries.ts, pinned by a test that reads that file off disk. **No donor is re-assigned by this change** — the curated override list that actually moves donors into the new buckets is a separate follow-up. Cross-ref [[FIX-890]] [[FIX-889]] [[FIX-897]] [[FIX-896]]. Explicitly NOT bundled: [[FIX-873]]/[[FIX-875]] industry-tag dedup, and the DISTINCT ON alphabet-gradient tie-break. <!--id:FIX-908-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **NAICS sector-map corrections + clean the out-of-vocabulary industry singletons** — Companion to [[FIX-908]]. Two pieces. **(1) NAICS2_INDUSTRY in pipelines/tags/rules.ts had five sector-level category errors** — the same mis-bucketing the [[FIX-908]] audit found in entity_tags, written down a second time, where it would reseed on every nightly. '22' mapped to oil_gas but **NAICS 22 IS Utilities**; '31'/'32'/'33' mapped to agriculture/pharma/defense but **NAICS 31-33 IS Manufacturing**; '56' mapped to labor but **NAICS 56 is Administrative/Support/Waste Services** — staffing, security and waste firms, not unions — so the entry is REMOVED rather than repointed (no right bucket exists and an untagged row is the honest answer). '62' follows the pharma-to-health rename. '21' stays on oil_gas per the [[FIX-908]] decision to keep that tag narrow. In NAICS_OVERRIDE, '325'/'326' (bulk chemicals, plastics/rubber) move from pharma to manufacturing with a new 4-digit '3254' entry preserving Pharmaceutical and Medicine Manufacturing on health. Touches only ~78 contractor rows so dollar impact is nil — this is about not re-writing the error. **(2) Deletes the remaining out-of-vocabulary industry tags**: energy (3 rows) and pharmaceutical (1 row), both generated_by='ai' residue predating the [[FIX-890]] write-boundary guard, so nothing can write them now. The three energy entities (Yellowstone Electric, GE Renewables, Ameresco) are USASpending contractors with **zero donation rows**, so the deletion cannot move any dollar figure; pharmaceutical is AHORA PAC, a Hispanic-outreach political PAC classified pharmaceutical at confidence 0.95 — a flat hallucination. manufacturing is NOT deleted: [[FIX-908]] promotes it to a real key and NAM-PAC is correctly tagged. **Scope finding not in the original plan:** a blanket vocabulary key rename must also rewrite the derived stores that PERSIST the key, not just entity_tags. official_sector_affinity_rollup is a TABLE refreshed incrementally off the donor dirty set, and rules.ts assertIndustryVocabulary() THROWS on any value outside VALID_INDUSTRIES — so renaming entity_tags alone would have left stale keys for every official whose donors did not change and taken down the entire nightly official tagger on the next run. Six persisted stores are rewritten in the migration (entity_tags, official_sector_affinity_rollup, official_donor_rollup_mv, donor_party_rollup_mv, entity_search_index, contract_agency_sector_rollup, contract_recipient_rollup); the two true materialized views (chord_industry_flows_mv, official_sector_dollars_mv) cannot be UPDATEd and self-heal on their existing REFRESH cadence. Cross-ref [[FIX-908]] [[FIX-890]] [[FIX-897]] [[FIX-838]]. <!--id:FIX-909-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **ai-classifier writes 'other' as a real industry tag — out-of-vocabulary rows that bypass the write-boundary guard** — Documented at the call site by [[FIX-908]], filed here rather than fixed there because changing it changes what gets written. buildPrompt() in packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/ai-classifier.ts offers 'other' as an answer for 'if unclear', and classifyPac() ALSO coerces any unrecognised or unparseable model answer to 'other'. That value is then upserted into entity_tags as a real industry tag with display_label 'Other' — but 'other' is NOT a member of VALID_INDUSTRIES. Three consequences. (1) **It bypasses the guard**: the [[FIX-890]] write-boundary check lives in drain/vocabulary.ts and only covers the drain path; ai-classifier upserts straight over PostgREST, so the one vocabulary enforcement point in the codebase never sees it. (2) **An abstention is indistinguishable from a parse failure** — both collapse to the same string, so 'the model declined to classify this PAC' and 'the model returned malformed JSON' are the same row, and neither is visible in the skipped count. (3) **It renders a meaningless pill**: a donor labelled 'Other' is worse than an unlabelled donor, because it looks like a classification. Measured 2026-07-27: **8 rows on prod, 0 on local** — small, but it is the same out-of-vocabulary class as the energy/pharmaceutical singletons [[FIX-909]] deleted, still being actively written. The [[FIX-909]] migration's vocabulary assertion explicitly TOLERATES 'other' for exactly this reason (asserting it away would fail the migration on prod today, and would fail again on the next classifier run even after a cleanup) — that tolerance should be removed as part of this fix. **Suggested shape:** stop writing the row at all when the model abstains, record the abstention in the sync-log counters as its own category distinct from parse failures, and clean up the 8 existing prod rows in the same change. Consider also routing this upsert through checkTagVocabulary so the guard covers every writer, not just the drain. Cross-ref [[FIX-908]] [[FIX-909]] [[FIX-890]]. <!--id:FIX-911-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Curated industry overrides — 742 hand-audited donors re-assigned or de-tagged** — Follow-up to [[FIX-908]]/[[FIX-909]], which added the four missing vocabulary keys but deliberately moved no donor into them. The 2026-07-27 dollar-weighted audit hand-classified 1,438 donors covering 90-100% of each industry tag's donation dollars and produced a **742-donor override list** committed at `docs/audits/2026-07-27-industry-tag-overrides.tsv`: **380 donors carrying the wrong industry** and **362 that are not industries at all** (leadership PACs, party committees, abstract vanity PACs — WinRed, NRSC, Save America, Huck PAC, AmeriPAC were all tagged `lobby`). Decision was explicitly hand-curation, NOT an AI re-run. **New table `public.financial_entity_industry_overrides`** keyed on `fec_committee_id` (text PK), not `financial_entities.id` — UUIDs are not portable across a re-seed and FEC committee IDs are the stable external key (`financial_entities.fec_committee_id` is already UNIQUE); all 742 audited donors have one and all 742 are distinct. `industry IS NULL` is a POSITIVE ASSERTION — 'this donor carries no industry, ever' — not 'unknown', and it SUPPRESSES tagging rather than merely omitting it. **The override is applied INSIDE `tagFinancialEntities()`** in packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts, as the last word after the keyword and NAICS passes. It cannot live anywhere else: that function calls `clear_financial_entity_rule_tags(['industry'])`, which DELETEs every `entity_type='financial_entity' AND generated_by='rule' AND tag_category='industry'` row and re-inserts from the keyword list, so an override written by any other job would be shadowed by a freshly re-inserted keyword tag on the very next nightly — the same authoritative-rebuild trap [[FIX-897]] hit with the official industry pills and the co-owned-rows class of [[FIX-808]]. Override rows are written `generated_by='curated'`, confidence 1.0, visibility 'primary', with `metadata` carrying `source`, `audited_sector` and `fec_committee_id` so the number behind the label stays auditable. Because the nightly clear is scoped to `generated_by='rule'`, curated rows SURVIVE it — so the producer owns them with its own authoritative delete-then-insert over the same direct-pg connection as the upsert, making a re-run a no-op rather than an accumulation. **Exactly one industry per overridden donor**, which has a second payoff: it collapses 96.4% of the multi-tag donation dollar mass ($328.4M of $340.8M across 478 multi-tag donating donors) to a single tag at the source, leaving the alphabetical `DISTINCT ON` tie-break ([[FIX-918]]) almost nothing to arbitrate. The migration also does a **one-time delete of the surviving `generated_by='ai'` industry rows for overridden donors only** — the nightly clear never touched them, so the AI-derived tags carrying most of the dollars (93.7% of `lobby`, 71.5% of `oil_gas`, 66.4% of `defense`) would otherwise persist forever and shadow the curation. AI rows for NON-overridden donors are left alone. **KNOWN, ACCEPTED LIMITATION:** 742 rows is the audited set at 90-100% dollar coverage per tag; the unaudited tail keeps its current tag. In particular `oil_gas` retains a tail of small utility PACs (~8% of its dollars) the audit did not reach. Depends on [[FIX-910]] (the producer must actually complete) and pairs with [[FIX-917]] (without which the AI classifier re-tags the 362 de-tagged committees on its next run). Cross-ref [[FIX-908]] [[FIX-909]] [[FIX-897]] [[FIX-890]] [[FIX-873]] [[FIX-875]]. <!--id:FIX-916-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **AI classifier must exclude curated-override donors — else it re-tags exactly what was de-tagged** — The fail-open trap paired with [[FIX-916]], filed separately because it is a distinct guard with its own test. `runAiClassifier()` in packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/ai-classifier.ts builds its candidate set as 'PACs over the donation threshold that have **no** industry tag' — `allPacs.filter(r => !alreadyTagged.has(r.id))`. After [[FIX-916]]'s one-time `generated_by='ai'` cleanup, the **362 `industry IS NULL` donors have zero industry tags by construction**, which is precisely the shape the classifier reads as 'needs classifying'. On the next run the model would re-tag WinRed, NRSC, Save America, Huck PAC and AmeriPAC — the exact committees the audit de-tagged — and because that upsert writes `generated_by='ai'` it would land OUTSIDE the nightly `clear_financial_entity_rule_tags` scope and persist. The 380 re-assigned donors are protected by having a curated tag (so `alreadyTagged` covers them), but relying on that is incidental, not a guard. Fix is to subtract every entity present in `financial_entity_industry_overrides` from the candidate set — INCLUDING, and especially, the NULL-industry rows — so a positive 'no industry, ever' assertion is honoured rather than read as a gap. This is the same silent-strand class as [[FIX-884]]/[[FIX-885]]: a guard that looks fine and never runs. Pinned by a test that puts a NULL-override entity in the tagged/PAC fixtures and asserts it is absent from the candidate set, so deleting the guard fails the suite. Cross-ref [[FIX-916]] [[FIX-911]] [[FIX-890]]. <!--id:FIX-917-->
- [ ] 🟡 M — **Alphabetical DISTINCT ON tie-break silently redistributes multi-tag donor dollars across eight surfaces** — **Filed, not fixed** — surfaced by the [[FIX-916]] curation work and deliberately left alone there, because changing it moves numbers on eight surfaces whose conservation proofs would all have to be re-derived, which is not something a curation PR should do quietly. When a donor carries more than one industry tag, every consuming surface picks ONE via `DISTINCT ON (...) ... ORDER BY ... tag` — i.e. **alphabetically**. That is not a neutral tie-break, it is a systematic gradient: measured 2026-07-27, `agriculture` sorts first so it exports exactly $0.00 and keeps 100% of its dollars, while `transportation` sorts last so it imports exactly $0.00 and loses 44.4% of its dollars to earlier-sorting co-tags. A donor tagged both `agriculture` and `transportation` is counted entirely as agriculture on every surface, for no reason other than the letter it starts with. **Affected surfaces:** `sector_affinity_rebuild_officials()`, `official_sector_dollars_mv`, `chord_industry_flows_mv`, `chord_industry_flows_for_official()`, `chord_top_pacs_for_official()`, `get_official_donors()`, `refresh_group_donor_rollup()`, `fetchIndustryTagsByEntityId()`, plus the [[FIX-873]] contract-flow bodies. **[[FIX-916]] removes most of what this was mishandling** — it collapses 96.4% of the multi-tag donation dollar mass ($328.4M of $340.8M across 478 multi-tag donating donors) down to exactly one tag at the source — so the residual is the unaudited multi-tag tail, and the honest fix here is a deliberate ranking (by tag confidence, then by the donor's own dollar concentration) rather than by spelling. Note the same alphabet already bit [[FIX-908]]: renaming `pharma` to `health` moved the key's position in the ORDER BY so `health` now beats `labor`/`legal`/`lobby` where `pharma` lost to all three, moving ~$41.6M between buckets on the next incremental refresh with no data change whatsoever. Cross-ref [[FIX-916]] [[FIX-873]] [[FIX-875]] [[FIX-908]] [[FIX-872]]. <!--id:FIX-918-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **financial_entity_industry_overrides cannot address a donor with a NULL fec_committee_id** — Surfaced by the [[FIX-921]] oil_gas sweep. public.financial_entity_industry_overrides is keyed on fec_committee_id (PRIMARY KEY), so any financial_entity carrying a NULL fec_committee_id is **structurally unreachable** by the curation mechanism — it can be neither re-assigned nor de-tagged, no matter what an audit finds. Two oil_gas escapees are in exactly that position and were dropped from the FIX-921 list for this reason alone: **Cumberland Energy Action Fund ($135k)** and **Ridge Coal Legacy Trust ($15k)**, both of which the audit judged mis-tagged. **Measured 2026-07-29 — the blind spot is SMALL:** 4 industry-tagged donating donors carry a NULL fec_committee_id ($416,500 total outflow), of which 2 are oil_gas. Small enough to leave the key as-is rather than revisit the table's PK, which is why this is filed rather than fixed — but it is a real hole and it will grow with any ingestion path that mints financial_entities without a committee id (IE-only spenders, USASpending recipients, IRS 990 orgs). **Suggested shape when it becomes worth doing:** add a nullable financial_entity_id column to the override table and make the resolver prefer fec_committee_id, falling back to the UUID, so the common case keeps its portable-across-re-seed key ([[FIX-916]]'s reason for choosing fec_committee_id) while the tail is still addressable. Do NOT swap the PK to the UUID — that is the portability the current key exists to protect. **REFRAMED 2026-08-01: this is an entity-DEDUP problem, not an override-key-coverage problem.** At least two of the four NULL-key donors are duplicates of FEC-keyed entities that already exist (verified on local 2026-08-01): National Rifle Association of America Political Victory Fund exists BOTH as C00053553 (`pac`) and as a NULL-keyed `other`-type row, and Pharmaceutical Care Management Association exists BOTH as C00388819 (`pac`) and as a NULL-keyed `other`-type row. The override mechanism already reaches the FEC-keyed twin; what is broken is that the NULL-keyed duplicate splits the donor's money and tags across two rows — the [[FIX-544]] merge class. The nullable-financial_entity_id column suggested above would ADDRESS the duplicate rather than merge it. Dedup first; only then see whether any genuinely FEC-less donor (the Cumberland/Ridge pair may be the same shape) leaves a residual blind spot worth a schema change. Cross-ref [[FIX-921]] [[FIX-916]] [[FIX-544]]. <!--id:FIX-922-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **HRPAC is mis-classified as 'other' — the one surviving out-of-vocabulary industry row** — Deliberately left behind by [[FIX-911]]'s cleanup migration (20260729000000), which deleted 7 of the 8 `other` industry rows and kept this one. **HILL-ROM HOLDINGS, INC. POLITICAL ACTION COMMITTEE (HRPAC)** (fec_committee_id C00448993, $101,903 outflow) is not an abstention — Hill-Rom Holdings is a hospital-equipment and medical-technology manufacturer (patient beds, monitoring; acquired by Baxter International in 2021), which is plainly `health`. **The confidence is the tell:** 0.85, against 0.20–0.30 for the seven leadership/joint-fundraising/ideological PACs deleted alongside it. The model recognised the company; the pre-[[FIX-911]] coercion threw the answer away and kept the confidence number. This is the [[FIX-908]] root cause captured in a single row — at classification time the vocabulary had no `health` key (it was `pharma`), so a correct answer like 'healthcare' or 'medical' was not a vocabulary member and collapsed to `other`. **Fix is one row:** add C00448993 → health to public.financial_entity_industry_overrides, then re-run tagFinancialEntities() per env. Not done in [[FIX-911]] because assigning an industry is the curated-override table's job and extending that list was out of scope for a cleanup migration; not done in [[FIX-921]] because that migration asserts a row count of exactly 50 and mixing cohorts would break its drift alarm. **Until it lands, the [[FIX-909]] migration's blanket `other` tolerance cannot go to zero** — 20260729000000 replaced it with 'expect exactly HRPAC, WARN on anything else', which is tighter but not the strict assertion [[FIX-911]] wanted. Closing this closes that too. Cross-ref [[FIX-911]] [[FIX-920]] [[FIX-908]] [[FIX-909]] [[FIX-916]]. <!--id:FIX-923-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Drain vocabulary guard's documented fail-open warn was never implementable — verdict type cannot express it** — `packages/data/src/drain/vocabulary.ts`'s module header promises that an unregistered `entity_type` degrades to 'write it and warn' rather than silently discarding every tag. Only the first half existed. `VocabularyVerdict` was `{allowed: true} | {allowed: false; reason}`, so a fail-open allow (nothing was checked — no vocabulary is registered for this entity type) was byte-identical to an enforced allow (the tag really is in the vocabulary). The caller could not tell them apart, so `drain/apply.ts` wrote both in silence. This is one enum widening from being live: `QueueRow.entity_type` in `apply.ts` already includes `'agency'`, and the module header names agency tag tasks as the plausible addition. Fixed: the allowed arm carries `enforced` (`{allowed: true; enforced: true}` vs `{allowed: true; enforced: false; reason}`), and `apply.ts` emits one `console.warn` per unenforced tag naming queue id, entity type and tag, then writes the row as before. NO allow/reject outcome changed — fail-open stays fail-open, which is exactly what the FIX-896 `official: {}` reasoning depends on, and `agency` is deliberately NOT registered in `TAG_VOCABULARY`. Observability only. `allowed` stays the discriminant so every existing call site and test compiles and passes untouched; `vocabulary-official-retired.test.ts` now pins both halves (unregistered → unenforced + reason naming the type; `proposal`/`topic` → enforced) so the two allows can never be conflated again. <!--id:FIX-925-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **ai_summary_cache is never invalidated when the summary prompt changes — stale framing survives a prompt fix** — **File only.** Surfaced while shipping [[FIX-931]]. `ai_summary_cache` is keyed on `(entity_type, entity_id, summary_type)` and nothing in the key or the write path encodes **which prompt produced the text**, so editing a prompt has no effect on any already-cached summary — the old wording is served indefinitely. Concretely: cached summaries written before [[FIX-931]] still carry the pre-fix "Total raised" framing for official money, which [[FIX-931]] corrected precisely because it overstates what the FEC-itemized figure actually measures (it excludes unitemized small-dollar giving, joint-fundraising transfers, loans and other receipts). So the label on the page is now right and the AI prose next to it is still wrong, which is worse than either alone. Fix: add a **prompt-version component to the cache key** (a hash of the prompt template, or an explicit `prompt_version` column bumped when the template changes) so a prompt edit naturally misses the cache and regenerates, rather than requiring anyone to remember a manual purge. A targeted purge on prompt edit is the cheap interim, but it is the same class of "a comment/convention does not enforce" problem [[FIX-901]] documented — the durable fix is making the key carry the dependency. Check the same question for the tagger and narrative caches while in there. Note the regeneration cost is real (every affected entity re-summarised), so pair the key change with a count of what would invalidate before shipping it. Cross-ref [[FIX-931]], [[FIX-901]]. <!--id:FIX-938-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **A donor industry-tag change never triggers a sector-affinity rebuild — content-signature gate + targeted rebuild** — The FIX-704/832 incremental refresh of official_sector_affinity_rollup keys its dirty set off financial_relationships.updated_at, and a tag-only change never touches a financial relationship — so a donor industry re-tag (FIX-916, FIX-921, FIX-923, and every future audit cohort) leaves the officials-side pills frozen with no error. That is exactly the 2026-07-16→07-29 drift: the last full backfill predated FIX-916 by eleven days and 1,607 of 1,779 override-touched officials still showed pre-audit pills while every non-freshness check passed. The only remedy was a human remembering backfill_official_sector_affinity_rollup() (943.8s / 19,869 rows measured on prod 2026-07-29). **The change signal must be content-derived, never time-derived**: entity_tags has created_at and NO updated_at, and tagFinancialEntities() does an authoritative DELETE-then-reinsert of every rule industry row nightly, so max(created_at) resets to ~now on every successful run — a rollup-older-than-last-write check would fire every night forever, and a signal that always fires is a signal nobody reads. Fix: (1) an md5 **content signature** over the (entity_id, tag) set for entity_type=financial_entity AND tag_category=industry (count + hash, computed by compute_fe_industry_tag_signature()), stored in pipeline_state under sector_affinity:industry_tag_signature — the FIX-652/716 count+signature gate shape; identical content → identical signature → zero work. (2) A per-donor shadow table public.donor_industry_tag_state (~24k rows: donor_id, md5 of its ordered tag set, tag count) that the refresh procedure diffs via FULL OUTER JOIN — the diff yields exactly the donors whose industry assignment changed, including donors whose tags were REMOVED. (3) refresh_sector_affinity_from_tag_changes(): affected recipients = officials who received a donation from a changed donor (an index probe on financial_relationships (from_id) via financial_relationships_donation_size_rollup), rebuilt in 500-official chunks with per-chunk COMMIT through the existing sector_affinity_rebuild_officials() — NOT the 16-minute full backfill, which is reserved for a cold start / signature-store miss; data_sync_log rows under sector_affinity_tag_refresh name which path ran (noop / targeted / full_backfill) and why. Signature + shadow advance only on a clean run, so a failed chunk retries next night. Donor-side dirty set unchanged — the gap was officials-only. Cross-ref [[FIX-704]] [[FIX-777]] [[FIX-832]] [[FIX-916]] [[FIX-921]] [[FIX-923]] [[FIX-652]] [[FIX-716]]. <!--id:FIX-958-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Rule-tagger phase order lagged official industry pills a full night — reorder around the affinity refresh + content-keyed staleness alarm** — Companion to [[FIX-958]], split out because the order fix is independently verifiable and would otherwise hide inside the trigger diff. runRuleBasedTagger() ran tagProposals → tagOfficials → tagFinancialEntities, so tagOfficials — which reads official_sector_affinity_rollup to write the FIX-897 industry pills — executed BEFORE that night's donor tag changes existed, and any rollup rebuild came later still. **Even with a perfect trigger, pills would lag a full night.** Fix half 1 (order): the phases now run proposals → financialEntities → refresh_sector_affinity_from_tag_changes() → officials, so the targeted rebuild completes before tagOfficials reads the rollup; proven by the single-run test (flip one donor's industry via the override table, run the tagger ONCE, the receiving official's pill reflects it — not on the second run). The refresh call is deliberately NON-fatal to the tagger (a stale rollup is the pre-existing condition and a dead nightly is worse — the FIX-920 direction); the alarm below is what makes a silent strand loud. Fix half 2 (alarm): check_sector_affinity_tag_staleness() compares the LIVE content signature against the stored sector_affinity:industry_tag_signature — keyed on signature-CHANGE, never write time, so the nightly DELETE-then-reinsert of identical content can never false-fire (decision-1 discipline). A mismatch is only escalated when the STORED signature has been stuck unchanged for >26h (probe state in pipeline_state sector_affinity:staleness_probe records first_mismatch_at + the stored sig it saw; a stored sig that advanced since the probe means the refresh is alive and the mismatch is a new change in flight — probe resets). Wired into canary-check.ts beside the FIX-885/943/944 detectors: recorded in the meta row every run, escalates (email + non-zero exit → red workflow) on stale=true. This is the FIX-884/885 silent-strand class: the guard exists so the next eleven-day drift is loud. Cross-ref [[FIX-958]] [[FIX-897]] [[FIX-920]] [[FIX-885]] [[FIX-944]] [[FIX-916]]. <!--id:FIX-959-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **FIX-954 residue: Higgins ($1.64M) and Davidson ($1.09M) refused as weak-overlap — re-measure after the next Sunday fec_bulk** — File only — the FIX-954 prod run (2026-08-06 attribution sequence, closed at b0ee27c2) REFUSED two remediation candidates as weak-overlap on prod-current signals: Clay Higgins ($1.64M suspect attribution) and Warren Davidson ($1.09M). Deliberate conservatism, correct at the time: the overlap signals measured on prod-current data were too weak to act on, so both were left IN PLACE and measurable rather than deleted on a guess. This bullet exists so the refusal does not evaporate into a session report. What to do: RE-MEASURE after the next Sunday fec_bulk run — a fresh ingest rewrites updated_at across the relevant rows and will either strengthen or dissolve the three-part overlap rule signals for both officials. If the signals strengthen, any remediation goes through the FIX-954 manifest machinery (audit -> manifest -> apply -> rollup tail), NEVER a bare delete. If they dissolve, close this as no-op. Cross-ref [[FIX-954]], [[FIX-957]], [[FIX-934]]. <!--id:FIX-967-->

## OFFICIALS
- [x] 🟡 M — **Legistar municipal-council person flood — generic shared bodies aggregate hundreds of members** — Surfaced by the FIX-470 gb-membership audit (docs/audits/2026-06-03-gb-membership-audit.md §5). Legistar parks municipal officials on generic shared governing bodies that aggregate many cities'/sessions' members onto one body: prod 'City Council' (municipal_council) = 232 active members, 'City Council Addendum Agenda' = 472, 'Board of Supervisors' (other) = 397 — all `tier='elected'` (the column default; legistar/mappers.ts never sets tier). Plausible single-council size is ~9–11. Because these are `tier='elected'`, the FIX-470 current-member predicate does NOT shrink them — this is ingest-side, not consumer-side. Either (a) Legistar should resolve a distinct gb per municipality instead of a generic shared 'City Council', and/or (b) former members must be deactivated. Distinct from [[FIX-470]] (FEC candidate pollution, consumer-fixed). The 'City Council Addendum Agenda' body shouldn't be a governing body at all — that normalization is already queued separately from the 2026-06-03 brainstorm. Cross-ref [[FIX-439]] (scope-by-intrinsic-signal precedent). Ingest change has blast radius across legistar/index.ts + mappers.ts; scope carefully. <!--id:FIX-471-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **officials/[id] Government Spending renders $51269.2M — formatMoney/data-scale quirk** — Government Spending section on officials/[id] renders raw-scale figures like $51269.2M where the compactor should roll up to $51.3B — formatMoney/data-scale quirk. Pre-existing; spotted during PR-R4 print verification. File-only, not implemented in Terminal Wave 1. <!--id:FIX-721-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Retire AI issue-area tagging for officials — an official is not a document** — classifyOfficial() asked Haiku for an official's 'primary policy focus areas' while sending it full_name, role_title, party, state, a bare vote_count NUMBER, total_raised, and a PAC-vs-individual percentage string — nothing about what the official actually voted on or sponsored. For the ~600 officials with any votes the model could only recite training-data priors about a named public figure; for the 22,846 active officials with no votes, no donations and no committee the real inputs reduced to name + party + state. confidence was self-reported by the model and visibility gated on it, so the quality gate was the model grading its own homework. On a platform whose thesis is receipts, that is an unsourceable inferred claim about a named real person. Cost was never the issue (a full re-tag is ~11 dollars). Prod carries ZERO official AI tag rows, so retirement is pure removal with no cleanup. Removes classifyOfficial(), runOfficialAiTagger(), the official leg of AI_TABLE_BY_ENTITY / the cost estimate / the queue-mode staging branch, fetchOfficialsNeedingTags(), the official block of drain/prompts/tag.md, and the official-tag enqueue in enrichment/seed-backlog.ts. Official AI SUMMARIES are deliberately left intact — separate policy question. drain/vocabulary.ts gets official: {} rather than a deleted key, because checkTagVocabulary FAILS OPEN for unregistered entity types and deleting the key would silently re-permit exactly the writes being retired. Generalized rule: AI-classify only where the classification derives from text we hold. Replacement labels are derived + citeable — see [[FIX-897]]. PR 3 of 3 closing the arc opened by [[FIX-889]]/[[FIX-890]]/[[FIX-891]] and [[FIX-893]]/[[FIX-894]]/[[FIX-895]]. <!--id:FIX-896-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Derived industry labels for officials from donation sector affinity** — Replacement for the AI issue-area tags retired by [[FIX-896]]. Industry labels come from public.official_sector_affinity_rollup (FIX-777, deduped by FIX-872/875) — real donation data, already materialized, already refreshed daily off the FIX-704 donor dirty set. Written as a fourth block inside tagOfficials() in packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts, in the same allTags array as the tenure/voting/donor blocks: tag_category='industry', generated_by='rule', confidence=1.0, top 3 industries per official by total_cents, rank 1 visibility='primary' and the rest 'secondary', metadata carrying {rank, total_cents, donor_count, source} so the number behind the label is auditable. MUST live inside tagOfficials() — that function does an authoritative rebuild, DELETEing entity_type='official' AND generated_by='rule' with NO tag_category scope, so industry rows written by any other job would be silently wiped on the next nightly (the co-owned-rows failure class, cf. [[FIX-808]]). Rollup is 18,053 rows, past the PostgREST 1,000-row cap, so it is read via selectDirect (direct pg), joined to officials.is_active. The literal 'Untagged' sector — the rollup's bucket for donors carrying no industry tag, 4,174 of 4,326 officials — is NOT a vocabulary member and is excluded rather than rendered as a pill. Labels say DONATIONS, never 'funding': sector affinity is donation-scoped by design (FIX-872) and excludes independent expenditures. Zero UI work — EntityTags.tsx already carries an 'industry' entry in CATEGORY_STYLES (green pill) and CATEGORY_ORDER (position 3), and OfficialCard.tsx already renders <EntityTags tags={official.tags} />. Prod-measured 2026-07-26: 2,135 active officials get labels (4,196 have rollup rows but 2,061 of those have classified dollars only in 'Untagged'); top-3 captures 81.4% of an official's classified donation dollars on average vs 85.8% for top-4. <!--id:FIX-897-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Sweep the pending official TAG enrichment backlog — feature retired** — Data-state closeout for [[FIX-896]]. Retiring the official AI issue-area tagger stops NEW official tag tasks being enqueued, but leaves the rows already staged: prod-measured 2026-07-26, 8,886 pending official TAG tasks (plus 2,779 pending official SUMMARY tasks, deliberately untouched — official AI summaries are a separate policy question). Draining those tag rows would pay a model to classify a retired feature; leaving them pending keeps them in the FIX-820/822 partial claim indexes where a drain worker will pick them up. Marks them status='skipped_feature_retired' — a value DISTINCT from FIX-895's 'skipped_no_source_text', because the reason here is 'feature retired', not 'no source text', and FIX-894/895's reverse sweep must never resurrect a retired feature's tasks. Rows are MARKED, never deleted, following the FIX-895 recoverable-status pattern: single .sql file with forward + reverse blocks, BEGIN/ROLLBACK dry-run proving the row count before any apply, before/after counts, total-rows-conserved assertion, explicit --allow-prod gate. Scope is entity_type='official' AND task_type='tag' only. Script: pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:sweep-official-tags. <!--id:FIX-898-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Officials directory tag fetch dies with 'URI too long' — every card has rendered zero pills** — Surfaced while verifying [[FIX-897]]. apps/civitics/app/officials/page.tsx builds its tag map with a single .in("entity_id", officialIds) over ALL ~1,000 officials the index loads. That is a ~40 KB request URL; PostgREST/Kong rejects it with 'URI too long' (reproduced locally 2026-07-26: 15ms, rows null, error 'URI too long'). withDbTimeout returns {data:null} on error and the call site coalesces that null to an empty array, so the failure is swallowed whole and EVERY official is assigned tags: [] — OfficialCard then guards on tags.length > 0 and renders no pill container at all. The directory has been silently tagless; a curl of /officials returns zero pills of any category. This is the FIX-426 swallowed-error class, and the page's OWN comment 15 lines above warns about exactly this shape for the engagement rollup ('a ~40KB in(...) URL the gateway rejects, silently zeroing every badge') — the tag fetch immediately below then does it anyway. Fix: chunk the id list at <=200 per request (the [[FIX-774]] bound), wrap each chunk in its own withDbTimeout, Promise.all and merge. Not a regression from FIX-897 — it predates it — but it is what would have made the new derived industry pills invisible on the one surface built to show them. Sibling read sites are fine: proposals/page.tsx and app/page.tsx pass page-sized id lists (tens, not thousands). <!--id:FIX-899-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Remove the dead officials issue-area filter and the orphaned ISSUE_AREAS export** — Cleanup authorized after [[FIX-896]] retired AI issue-area tagging for officials. Two orphans left behind. (1) OfficialsList.tsx ISSUE_PILLS renders seven clickable issue-area filter chips (Healthcare, Climate, Finance, Defense, Tech, Labor, Agriculture) whose predicate is tag === issueFilter AND tag_category === 'topic'. Officials have never carried a topic tag on prod — entity_tags holds ZERO official rows with tag_category='topic' — so every chip has always filtered the list to empty. It was dead before FIX-896 and stays dead after, because industry is not topic. REMOVED rather than repointed at tag_category='industry': that would make it a donor-SECTOR filter wearing an issue-area label, which is exactly the conflation FIX-896 exists to end (an official's donors' industries are not that official's policy positions). If industry filtering is wanted it should be built and named as such, against the [[FIX-897]] rows. (2) ISSUE_AREAS in pipelines/tags/topics.ts reached zero references once its three consumers went (classifyOfficial, buildOfficialTagContext, and official.topic in drain/vocabulary.ts, now official: {}); it was left exported with a @deprecated marker pending this call. The vocabulary-retirement test that iterated it keeps its coverage by inlining the historical 14-value list, which is the more correct pin anyway — it must assert against what the retired feature actually emitted, not against a live constant. <!--id:FIX-900-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Remove the A-F responsiveness grade from the official profile** — The official profile publishes an A-F letter grade for "Civic responsiveness" with no minimum-sample floor. `gradeFromRate` maps a response rate to A/B/C/D/F and the only guard is `total_closed === 0 && open === 0 -> null`, so an official with exactly ONE closed response window and no response renders a public **F** — twice (header stat strip + overview card). Two problems: (1) the platform has a standing rule against public scores (no public karma or leaderboard; standing surfaces only as unlocked capabilities) and a letter grade on a public profile is a public score; (2) a second, better responsiveness surface already exists (`app/lib/engagement.ts` + `EngagementBadges`, FIX-618/FIX-633) which emits small-n-disciplined tiers rather than a number. They even collide verbally — `GRADE_CONFIG.A.label` and `engagedTier()`'s top tier are both "Highly responsive". Fix: delete the grade AND the percentage headline (a published rate is the score), keep the window ledger in full — recent windows, per-window response_type labels, initiative links, counts, breakdown bar, and the "No Response records are permanent public record. Silence is data." note. C2 Wave 1. Paired with the EngagementBadges detail-page mount. <!--id:FIX-905-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Mount EngagementBadges on the official detail page** — The tiered engagement badges (Claimed / Responsive / Active community, FIX-618 + FIX-633) render on OfficialCard in the officials directory but NOT on the official detail page. With the A-F responsiveness grade removed (see the paired FIX), the profile would carry no responsiveness signal at all while the directory listing still shows one — an inconsistency, not a fix. Mount EngagementBadges in the identity block of `apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx` using `fetchEngagementRollup(supabase, "official", [official.id])` — its docstring names "a single detail page" as the intended caller. Derive props with `engagedTier()` / `communityTier()`, fall back to `EMPTY_ENGAGEMENT` on a miss, exactly as OfficialCard does. Fails open already (withDbTimeout 3000 + console.error + empty Map; EngagementBadges returns null when there is nothing to show), so a stale or missing MV never breaks the profile. Does NOT change engagement.ts math, thresholds, or tier labels — extending the tiers with a follow-through leg and recency window is C2 Wave 4, gated on Q&A volume that does not exist yet. C2 Wave 1. <!--id:FIX-906-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **Seed New Hampshire floterial state-house districts (58 legislators permanently unlinkable)** — New Hampshire has 164 HD jurisdiction rows for a 400-member House. NH uses floterial districts — an additional overlay district spanning several base districts, electing its own representative(s) — and TIGER's SLDLST set as ingested does not carry them, so 58 NH House members have a district_name (e.g. 'Merrimack 27', 'Rockingham 31', 'Sullivan 7') with no jurisdiction row to link to. Surfaced by the [[FIX-859]] backfill, which resolves 5180 of 5240 unlinked state legislators; the residual is exactly these 58 plus 2 Maine tribal seats (Passamaquoddy Tribe, Houlton Band of Maliseet Indians — non-geographic representation, correctly unlinked forever). Do NOT invent rows in the backfill script; this needs a real seeding pass against an authoritative NH floterial boundary source, after which the existing name-match tier picks them up with no code change. Until then NH is the only state whose SLD choropleth has visible holes. <!--id:FIX-914-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Officials 'Total raised' and 'Donors on record' both describe numbers the page does not have** — Three surfaces render the same two mislabelled figures: apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx StatCells 'Total raised' + 'Donors on record', apps/civitics/app/officials/components/OfficialCard.tsx, and the prompt string in apps/civitics/app/api/officials/[id]/summary/route.ts. **'Total raised' is not total raised.** It is the ALL-CYCLE sum (2020+2022+2024+2026) of ITEMIZED donation financial_relationships only — it excludes unitemized individual contributions entirely (51% of Ossoff's receipts, and FEC never discloses them at any grain so that is not a closeable coverage gap), joint-fundraising transfers, independent expenditures, loans and other receipts. For Jon Ossoff it renders $14.6M against $77.3M of FEC receipts for the 2025–26 cycle ALONE. **'Donors on record' is not a donor count.** It sums official_donor_rollup_mv.tx_count, which counts financial_relationships ROWS — i.e. donor×cycle pairs, since the table is unique on (relationship_type, from_id, to_id, cycle_year). Measured for Ossoff: 16,646 rows vs 15,761 distinct donors vs 26,520 underlying FEC transactions — so the label overstates donors by 5.6% and is not a transaction count either. official_homepage_stats_mv.donor_count (which OfficialCard and the summary route read) is the same COUNT(*) over the same rows. Neither MV can produce a distinct-donor count without a grain change, so this is a RELABEL, not a recompute: 'Itemized donations' and 'Donor records', with the coverage caveat given a placement that survives the note slot already being contended by the IE support/oppose totals. Cycle-scoping the money figure is a schema change tracked separately as [[FIX-932]]. Cross-ref [[FIX-518]], [[FIX-836]]. <!--id:FIX-931-->
- [ ] 🟡 M — **Cycle-scoped receipts need cycle_year on official_donor_rollup_mv** — Follow-up to [[FIX-931]], which relabelled the officials money cells to describe the number that actually exists rather than building a new one. official_donor_rollup_mv carries NO cycle_year column — its grain is (official_id, relationship_type, rank) over a per_donor CTE that SUMs across every cycle — so a cycle-scoped 'raised this cycle' figure is a **schema change, not a label change**: cycle_year would have to enter the MV grain (and the tail-bucket rank-1001 rollup recomputed per cycle), plus the officials page would need a cycle selector and a default-cycle policy. Note explicitly for whoever picks this up: the current all-cycle number is **correct-but-unscoped**, so a reader comparing an official's Civitics figure against an FEC per-cycle page will ALWAYS see a gap for that reason alone, entirely independently of the ingest defects in [[FIX-929]] / [[FIX-930]] and of the itemization floor. Do not conflate the two when triaging a 'the money is wrong' report — check whether the complaint is about scope (this bullet) or about coverage (the $200 per-transaction floor in indiv.ts and the missing oth{yy} joint-fundraising transfer ingest). Sizing note: official_donor_totals (FIX-836) has the same all-cycle grain and would need the same treatment if the headline figure moves onto it. <!--id:FIX-932-->
- [ ] 🟢 S — **$0 candidate stubs left behind by the FIX-933 merge — cosmetic duplicates, but 16 of them hold real Senate votes** — **File only.** [[FIX-933]] deliberately does NOT delete any `officials` row — "neutralise the duplicate" means it ends up holding zero `financial_relationships` rows, which keeps the merge fully reversible, keeps every FK intact, and keeps that PR out of the officials-dedup design question. The residue is 47 candidate-tier rows carrying $0, a retired `merged_fec_candidate_id` for provenance, and their original `metadata.cand_status/cand_ici/fec_election_yr`. They are same-person duplicates of a sitting member (`T Ossoff` alongside `Jon Ossoff`) and will surface in search, browse facets and any same-name cluster audit. **Not purely cosmetic though:** 16 of the 83 candidate rows [[FIX-933]]'s CAND_ID reconciliation touched hold **real Senate votes** — that is [[FIX-940]], a separate live bug, and those rows must NOT be deleted until it is fixed or the votes would go with them. So the ordering is [[FIX-940]] first, then this. Options when it is picked up: (a) leave them and filter candidate-tier $0 rows out of the surfaces where they are noise; (b) fold them properly, which means deciding the general officials-dedup question — note [[FIX-325]]/[[FIX-375]] closed the *casing*-dupe merge and explicitly scoped elected+candidate clusters OUT (they collide via the [[FIX-248]] promotion path), so this is genuinely undecided territory rather than a re-run of prior work; (c) mark them with an explicit `merged_into` pointer so they are self-describing and cheap to filter. (c) is the cheapest thing that makes the state legible. Cross-ref [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-940]], [[FIX-375]], [[FIX-325]], [[FIX-248]]. <!--id:FIX-939-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Two SAME-PERSON duplicates hide inside the CROSS-PERSON branch — Shontel Brown and Al Green hold their own money twice** — Surfaced by the [[FIX-934]] phase-1 manifest, which classifies each (suspect, owner) pair SAME-vs-CROSS instead of classifying the suspect once against its single best-overlap twin. **[[FIX-933]] could not see these because cross-person contamination dominated the overlap ranking.** Two sitting House members in the [[FIX-934]] cross-person population hold money that is duplicated onto **their own** `tier='candidate'` row: **Shontel M. Brown** (`f29bbd4e-944f-4840-adbd-16a4706a3c02`, Representative OH-11) has **$2,984,275 / 881 rows** duplicated onto `M Brown` [`H2OH11169`] — *Candidate for Representative, OH, district 11*, i.e. her own seat, holding $2,261,183; her elected row's overlap ranking is dominated by **Sherrod** Brown's $47.3M, which is why [[FIX-933]] never surfaced it. **Al Green** (`e9015ab5-913e-4f2c-a917-190ebd0dde22`, Representative TX-9) has **$941,978 / 596 rows** duplicated onto `Alexander Green` [`H4TX09095`] — *Candidate for Representative, TX, district 09*, his own seat (FEC files his legal name, so the first names disagree on the surface and only the seat signal decides it), holding $1,195,809; his best twin is **Mark** Green [`H8TN07076`], a different person. Total $3,926,253. **These are [[FIX-933]] merges, not [[FIX-934]] deletes — the direction is opposite.** The elected row survives and the candidate row is neutralised; deleting the suspect's side (which [[FIX-934]]'s originally-specified 'high collision rate → DUPLICATED → delete from the suspect' rule would have done, at 97.1% and 45.0% collision respectively) would have deleted a sitting member's own donors from their own page. Both pairs fail [[FIX-933]]'s own manifest gates as written (its survivor must be an attribution orphan whose twin is the sole `fec_candidate_id` holder, and it requires `stateOk` on the classifier's single best twin), so re-running that script unchanged will NOT pick them up — the merge needs the per-owner relation from `ownerRelation()` in `packages/data/src/scripts/fec-orphan-classify.ts`. Note both officials ALSO hold cross-person money that [[FIX-934]] phase 2 removes, and 2020/2022 residue blocked by [[FIX-952]]; sequence this AFTER [[FIX-934]] phase 2 so the merge operates on a cleaned row. Cross-ref [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-934]], [[FIX-930]], [[FIX-952]], [[FIX-929]]. <!--id:FIX-953-->
- [ ] 🟠 S — **merged_fec_candidate_id is single-valued, so a House→Senate member's stub can only retire ONE id (4 pairs, $3.0M re-split)** — Surfaced by the [[FIX-955]] repair pass. [[FIX-933]] records a retired claim in `officials.source_ids->>'merged_fec_candidate_id'`, a single scalar. That is sufficient for a member who only ever held one seat, but **4 sitting Senators who previously served in the House have a candidate stub carrying TWO of their CAND_IDs**: the stub retired the Senate id and has since re-claimed the House one. Measured on the local clone: **Jim Banks** (elected `S4IN00196`; stub live `H6IN03229`, retired `S4IN00196`, 414 rows / $1,516,488), **Ted Budd** (`S2NC00505` / live `H6NC13129`, 269 rows / $1,368,057), **Jon Ossoff** (`S8GA00180` / live `H8GA06195`, 1 row / $91,883), **Tom Cotton** (`S4AR00103` / live `H2AR04083`, 7 rows / $27,198). Total $3,003,625 across 691 rows, all of it the member's own House-era money now double-held with their elected row. The [[FIX-955]] repair **deliberately excludes these four** and its post-merge assertion refuses them by design: merging while a LIVE second claim remains on the stub means the next FEC run re-binds the House id and re-splits the same money, so the merge would undo itself exactly as [[FIX-955]] describes. Note [[FIX-955]]'s pipeline guard is keyed on the single retired value too, so it does not cover the second id either. Options: (a) make the marker an ARRAY (`merged_fec_candidate_ids`) and have `hasRetiredClaim()` test membership — cleanest, but touches the [[FIX-933]] write path, the [[FIX-955]] guard and `persistNewFecIds`; (b) retire only the id whose seat the SURVIVOR does not currently hold, keeping one scalar — works for this population (a Senator's House id is always the stale one) but breaks the moment someone holds three; (c) leave them and accept a bounded $3.0M double-count. (a) is the durable answer and is small. Ordering: this is only reachable once [[FIX-955]] has landed, and it should be applied to prod in the same pass. Cross-ref [[FIX-955]], [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-941]], [[FIX-939]]. <!--id:FIX-956-->
- [ ] 🟠 M — **Own-seat merge cannot take Al Green or Austin Scott — their stubs anchor external_relationships rows the merge has no step to re-anchor** — [[FIX-953]] merged 3 of its 5 class-B own-seat pairs. The two it refused are both almost certainly the same human — FEC files legal names, so **Al Green** (Representative TX-9) appears as *Alexander* Green [`H4TX09095`] and **Austin Scott** (Representative GA-8) as *James* Scott [`H0GA08099`], and both stubs' CAND_IDs encode the survivor's exact seat including district. They are blocked by the decision-5 rule 'a stub may carry money + provenance and NOTHING else', now mechanized as a hard refusal in `verifyOwnSeatInDb`: Alexander Green's stub anchors **6** `external_relationships` rows (3 from-side, 3 to-side: LittleSis `affiliated_with` / `business_partner` / `member_of` / `owns`) and James Scott's anchors **10** (9 from, 1 to, incl. 7 `appointment`). The merge moves `financial_relationships` and deletes only `donation`/`opposition` `entity_connections` edges, so those source rows would be stranded pointing at a $0 stub — the [[FIX-940]] lesson, where 'money and nothing else' was wrong by 1,755 votes. Austin Scott fails a SECOND, independent gate and should stay out regardless of how this is resolved: his stub shares **zero** (relationship_type, from_id, cycle_year) keys with his elected row — 0.0% donor-cycle overlap — and his elected row holds $0 across 0 FR rows, so there is no money evidence tying the two at all, only the seat. Options: (a) add a re-anchor step that repoints `external_relationships.from_id`/`to_id` (and any `entity_connections` derived from them) onto the survivor, which generalizes to the [[FIX-1019]] class-A backlog too; or (b) accept stranding and record it explicitly. NOTE the 15 stale `vote_yes`/`vote_no` `entity_connections` edges on Chuck Grassley's stub were NOT a blocker and did not stop his merge — the stub's `votes` count is 0, so those edges are orphaned artifacts of [[FIX-990]]'s dead rebuild, not data. Cross-ref [[FIX-953]], [[FIX-940]], [[FIX-990]], [[FIX-1019]]. <!--id:FIX-1020-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **/api/officials/[id]/summary fails open — a DB error returns 200 with summary:null, indistinguishable from an official with no record** — Noticed while choosing a probe target for the FIX-1021 supervised firing (cc-prompt-60); the route was rejected as a liveness probe BECAUSE of this, and the probe now routes around it via `/responsiveness`. The route never returns a non-200 for an infrastructure failure. Four separate paths all emit the same `200 {"summary": null}` body: (1) the `officials` read at route.ts:79-86 — supabase-js returns `{data: null, error}` rather than throwing, so a DB outage lands on `if (!official) return NextResponse.json({ summary: null })` and is reported as 'this official does not exist'; (2) the genuine no-data case at route.ts:109-111 (`voteCount === 0 && donorCount === 0`); (3) an empty model response at route.ts:163; (4) the catch-all at route.ts:197-200, which console.errors the exception and then returns the same null body. `getMonthlySpendCents` compounds it at route.ts:38-40 by swallowing its own error and returning 0, so a failed spend-cap read silently reads as '$0 spent this month' and the cap is not enforced for that request. **Why this is wrong on its own terms, independent of the probe use:** a caller cannot distinguish 'we have nothing to say about this official' from 'the database is down', so the surface is structurally incapable of reporting an outage — the same class of signal-laundering as [[FIX-1027]], where an outage was reported as health. The CDN discipline is already correct and should be preserved: FIX-796 deliberately stamps cache headers ONLY on the two summary-bearing 200s (route.ts:64, 196), leaving every null variant unstamped so a transient null is never pinned at the edge — so this fix must not start caching an error. **Fix shape:** distinguish the cases. Return 503 (uncached) when the `officials` read or the spend-cap read errors, keep 200 `{summary: null}` for the genuine no-data and kill-switch cases, and let `getMonthlySpendCents` propagate rather than defaulting to 0 so the cap fails CLOSED. Cross-ref [[FIX-1027]], [[FIX-796]], [[FIX-311]], [[FIX-893]]. <!--id:FIX-1029-->

## PROPOSALS

- [ ] ⬜ S — **Add "Trending", "Most Commented", "New" tabs** — add to FeaturedSection; requires trending-score pipeline and comments data <!--id:FIX-029-->
- [x] ⬜ S — **Wire CRS summary backfill into a schedule for ongoing capture** — Follow-on to [[FIX-435]] Phase 2. The CRS backfill (`data:backfill-crs-summaries`, `src/scripts/backfill-crs-summaries.ts`) is resumable: it only selects congress proposals with `summary_plain IS NULL`, so re-running it captures summaries for newly-ingested bills with zero hot-path cost. It was deliberately NOT wired into the batched congress ingest path (`votes.ts → resolveBillsBatch → upsertBillProposalsBatch`) because a per-bill `/summaries` call would add ~thousands of API calls to every nightly run and break that path's round-trip-minimizing design. Wire a low-frequency (weekly) invocation — e.g. a Sunday-block step in the nightly orchestrator or a small standalone GHA workflow with `--allow-prod`, throttled to the ~5,000/hr Congress.gov ceiling. Until then, new congress bills stay `title_only` until someone re-runs the backfill manually. <!--id:FIX-447-->
