### Pipelines

- [ ] 🟡 M — **Surface attribution on graph viz routes — per-node + per-edge attribution semantics** — Deferred from [[FIX-398]] (2026-05-26). The attribution surface was wired into the four detail-page SSR loaders and a new /api/attribution/[type]/[id] route, but list endpoints and graph viz routes were intentionally out of scope because per-edge attribution semantics need their own design. Action: extend AttributionShape (or a per-edge variant) onto: (a) list endpoints — /api/search/entity, /api/graph/entities (per-row primary), (b) graph relationships — /api/graph/connections, /api/graph/spending (per-edge attribution: does a vote edge attribute to the proposal's source or the vote's roll-call source? does a donation edge attribute to FEC or to the recipient?), (c) graph viz aggregators — sankey, treemap-individuals, voting-divergence, chord (per-node attribution that survives aggregation grouping). Open design questions to resolve first: (1) per-edge attribution model — single source vs. (from_source, to_source) pair vs. relationship-typed source; (2) aggregator pass-through — when N edges collapse into one chord arc, what does attribution.primary reference? (most-common source? union? null?); (3) source_count cost on list endpoints — the cheap (primary + count) shape needs a per-row count subquery; benchmark before shipping, materialize as attribution_source_count on entity tables if it becomes a hotspot. Cross-ref [[FIX-398]] (predecessor), [[FIX-397]] (predecessor schema), [[FIX-399]] (SourceBadge), [[FIX-400]] (popover). <!--id:FIX-405-->
- [ ] 🟡 L — **Graph seeding for jurisdiction/institution/meeting results + unify graph groups with governing_bodies (brainstorm)** — Surfaced by the FIX-442 ship report 2026-06-03. The search Add-to-graph affordance forwards the result kind via /graph?addEntityIds=...&addEntityTypes=... (apps/civitics/app/search/components/SearchActionBar.tsx:24, AdvancedSearchPage.tsx:268), but the graph entity vocabulary is a closed union — official | agency | financial | proposal (apps/civitics/app/api/graph/entities/route.ts:205) — so jurisdiction/institution/meeting seeds arrive as types the graph cannot resolve (silent no-op or broken node). Two-part scope. (a) SHORT-TERM GUARD (S, can ship ahead of the brainstorm): hide/disable Add-to-graph for unsupported kinds, or strip them from the bulk-add payload, so users cannot seed types the graph will drop. (b) DESIGN SESSION (the real work, L): decide how the new types become graph citizens, resolving the existing groups/governing_bodies redundancy — graph groups today are URL-param-materialized official collections (groupId/groupName/groupIcon params on /api/graph/group, presets like Full Senate in apps/civitics/app/graph/EmptyStatePresets.tsx), while governing_bodies are DB rows describing essentially the same collections (US Senate gb = Full Senate preset). Open questions: does seeding a gb expand to its member officials (group semantics) or render the gb as a first-class node with member/jurisdiction edges (entity semantics)? Should the hardcoded presets be re-derived FROM governing_bodies rows so the two mechanisms collapse into one? Do jurisdictions seed as geographic group expansions (officials within the jurisdiction) or as nodes? Are meetings worth graph presence at all (likely defer)? What do entity_connections / get_connection_counts need for any new node types? Brainstorm needed before sizing — pair with the [[FIX-416]] gb-detail-page brainstorm (same how-do-gbs-surface-everywhere question). Cross-ref [[FIX-442]] (surfacing), [[FIX-416]] (gb brainstorm), [[FIX-405]] (graph per-edge attribution design, adjacent graph-design backlog). <!--id:FIX-468-->
- [ ] 🟢 S — **group_donor_rollup full refresh is ~6min on prod — top-N storage could cut it** — FIX-500's refresh_group_donor_rollup() full re-aggregate measured ~6min on prod under IOWait load (519,886 donor rows across 573 cohorts; the long pole is the INSERT + PK/read-index maintenance, seen as IPC/BtreePage waits). It runs off-path at the tail of the twice-weekly rebuild-entity-connections workflow (4h GHA budget, donations chunk alone ~90min), so 6min is ~1% of budget and NOT currently painful — but it is close enough to the 1800s hook timeout under heavier load to note. Cheap durable cut if it ever bites: store only top-N (e.g. 200) donors per cohort via ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY gb_id,party_key ORDER BY total_cents DESC) <= 200 — the route reads <=100, and group_donor_rollup_summary already carries the TRUE donor_count + full total_cents independent of stored rows, so top-N rows lose nothing the request path uses. Shrinks the table ~519k -> ~tens-of-thousands and makes the INSERT seconds. Gated follow-up from [[FIX-500]] decision-2 (full per-donor rows shipped as-is per 'don't pre-optimize; file a follow-up if painful'). <!--id:FIX-501-->
- [x] 🟡 L — **Design: contract-flow MVs for /api/graph/spending** — DESIGN (do not implement in the quick-win session). /api/graph/spending and chord_contract_flows / treemap_recipients_by_contracts aggregate financial_relationships contract/grant flows live over a multi-million-row table (parallel seq scan, prod cost 354k for the unfiltered amount sort; see [[FIX-503]](c)). Materialize agency→recipient contract-flow rollups (per-agency or per-recipient MV refreshed on the spending pipeline hook) and read with live fallback. Pairs with the agency-staffing OFFSET loop ([[FIX-503]](g)) which sums the same data. Follow-up to [[FIX-503]]. <!--id:FIX-507-->
- [ ] 🟡 L — **Design: district geometry snapshot for voting-divergence + /api/districts** — DESIGN (do not implement in the quick-win session). Voting-divergence and /api/districts recompute district geometry/membership on the request path. Snapshot the geometry + per-district rollup into a table refreshed on the redistricting/import hook and serve reads from it (rolling-history snapshot shape per packages/db/CLAUDE.md). Reduces repeated heavy geometry scans against the cache-starved Pro Small. Follow-up to [[FIX-503]]. <!--id:FIX-508-->
- [x] 🟢 M — **Cross-source canonical-collision residue — LS-bound FE shares canonical_name with FEC/IRS/EDGAR rows (~3.4K clusters)** — Surfaced by the FIX-380 investigation (2026-06-09). The intra-source LS×LS dupe residue is gone (0 clusters with ≥2 distinct same-type LS-bound FE, confirmed on local + prod), but the residue moved cross-source: measured **3,414** canonical_name+entity_type clusters / **~20,786** collapsible rows where exactly one LS-bound FE shares a canonical_name with one or more non-LS (FEC/IRS/EDGAR) FE rows. This is FIX-271 cross-source-merge territory, NOT FIX-380's shared-edge heuristic. **Do NOT bulk-merge** — per the FIX-273 lesson, most same-canonical multi-FE clusters are common-name distinct people that must never auto-merge. Required first step is a signal-quality investigation: sample N clusters, classify true-dupe vs common-name-collision vs legitimately-distinct, then design a gated merge. resolve_entity_by_canonical already prevents NEW collisions on ingest; this is existing residue. Numbers measured 2026-06-09; re-measure on prod before acting. See docs/audits/2026-06-09-littlesis-intra-source-dedup-finding.md. Cross-ref [[FIX-271]], [[FIX-313]], [[FIX-273]]. <!--id:FIX-544-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Cleanup: retire the graph's pre-W2 selector components + /api/graph/group/preview** — Filed by [[FIX-762]] per the no-deletion standing rule — DO NOT execute until the W2 sidebar mount has soaked. Once safe: (1) remove the graph-selector mounts of GroupBrowser.tsx, EntityBrowse.tsx, EntitySearchInput.tsx and CustomGroupForm.tsx from packages/graph/src/components (NOTE: CustomGroupForm is still consumed by apps/civitics/app/agencies/components/GroupBuilderWidget.tsx and EntitySearchInput by GraphHeader/PathFinder — either migrate those consumers to the shared browser first or keep the files); (2) retire /api/graph/group/preview (unused by the graph after W2 — live counts come from /api/browse facet totals — but still called by CustomGroupForm, so it goes when (1) goes); (3) drop the FocusTree fallback branches that render the old stack when no browserSlot is passed. Grep for consumers before deleting anything. <!--id:FIX-765-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Stamp handler-owned CDN cache headers on the public api/graph/* read routes** — The [[FIX-796]] inversion left all api/graph/* reads uncached by default (interactive, param-heavy, low expected hit rate; the graph rate bucket bounds cost) — documented as a watch item in the next.config policy block. Decision 2026-07-12: implement rather than wait for load pressure (too-early beats too-late). Apply withPublicCdnCache (src/lib/cdn-cache.ts) to the GET 200 success path of each PUBLIC graph data route — entities, connections, group, group/preview, hierarchy, committees, tag-groups, gb-list, sankey, chord, matrix, treemap, treemap-pac, treemap-individuals, sunburst, small-dollar, sector-affinity, individual-donors, agency-staffing, leadership-tenure, voting-divergence, spending, search, snapshot, pathfinder — EXCLUDING the per-user routes (me, custom-groups, my-representatives — pinned no-store) and narrative (AI, kill-switch-gated). Per-route check before stamping: GET only, 200 only, payload has zero viewer-dependence (any route reading cookies/auth must be switched to createPublicClient first or skipped — the [[FIX-788]] rule). Mechanical but 25 files; verify with the FIX-796 probe pattern (second anon hit x-vercel-cache HIT, error statuses unstamped). <!--id:FIX-800-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **sankey SCAN_LIMIT=5000 is silently capped at 1000 by PostgREST max_rows** — Noticed during [[FIX-802]] verification: api/graph/sankey pulls contracts with .limit(SCAN_LIMIT=5000) ORDER BY amount_cents DESC, but PostgREST max_rows caps any single .select() at 1,000 rows ([[FIX-428]] class) — the response reports scannedRows: 1000 on every large agency, so the 'top-N rows already cover ~99% of total spend' comment is operating on 1/5 of its intended scan window and totalCents understates accordingly. Fix: page the contract scan with .range() (fetchAllPaged shape) up to the real 5,000, or lower SCAN_LIMIT to 1000 and correct the comment + a truthful scannedRows semantics note. Amount-DESC ordering means the truncation bias is at the tail, so the visual impact is small — data-truthfulness item, not a rendering bug. <!--id:FIX-803-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Leadership Tenure gantt has no data: zero official-to-agency appointment edges exist** — Found during FIX-733 verification (G2 wave). The /api/graph/leadership-tenure route queries entity_connections WHERE connection_type='appointment' AND from_type='official' AND to_type='agency' (the FIX-211 canonical direction), but ZERO such rows exist on local OR prod — every appointment edge involves financial_entities (LittleSis board/exec appointments: fe→fe 296k, official→fe 43k, fe→official 1.6k, official→official 270). The newly-mounted Gantt viz (FIX-733) therefore renders its empty state for every agency, and the gantt-leadership-tenure preset is data-dead. Either the cabinet/agency-leadership appointment derivation never ran post-cutover or it writes a different shape now. Fix = re-derive official→agency appointment edges from career_history/officials, or repoint the route at whatever table actually holds agency leadership tenures. <!--id:FIX-808-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Scatter viz draws nothing: route returns rows but the svg renders 0 children** — Post-G2/G3 browser QA finding (2026-07-11). The Scatter viz mounts (FIX-733 render branch) and /api/graph/agency-staffing returns 200 with 122 rows on prod, but the rendered #scatter-svg has 0 child elements — a blank canvas with data in hand. Investigate the FIX-733 mount's render branch and props: GraphPage passes vizOptions={view.style.vizOptions.scatter} (undefined until the user touches a scatter setting — check ScatterGraph's defaults handle that), primaryEntityId, and primaryGroup. Also check the component's resize/draw effect ordering — the svg may be measured at 0x0 inside the absolute-inset-0 wrapper before layout settles, and never redraw. Local smoke during FIX-733 only verified the ROUTE (102 rows), not the paint. <!--id:FIX-810-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Graph focus.scope is a dead state field — Scope select gated nothing** — FIX-812 investigation finding: the legacy OPTIONS Scope select (all/federal/senate/house/state) wrote focus.scope but the field has ZERO readers — not in useGraphData, not in any /api/graph route. It was a placebo control, so the G4 IA reorg did not re-mount it (no new dead UI). The field stays in GraphView.focus for saved-state shape compatibility (presets set it). Either wire scope into the connections fetch as a real entity/edge filter (then surface the control in the Connections tab filter group) or retire the field from the type + presets. Filed by [[FIX-812]]. <!--id:FIX-816-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Save view… writes civitics_presets that nothing reads — saved custom views are unreachable** — FIX-812 investigation finding: the right-panel Save view… button (formerly Save changes / Save preset) appends the full GraphView to localStorage civitics_presets, but no code path reads that key — the viz dropdown's Custom section is a stub alert('coming in a future update'). Saved views are write-only: users can save but never load one. Wire the Custom section (and/or the right-panel Presets list) to list + apply civitics_presets entries, with a delete affordance; on read, clamp legacy depth:3 to 2 and ignore the removed per-entity highlight flag (the [[FIX-812]] back-compat contract). Longer term this merges with the Browse W2 DB-backed saved-views concept ([[FIX-763]]). Filed by [[FIX-812]]. <!--id:FIX-817-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **React #418/#422 hydration errors on every /graph prod load** — React minified errors #418 (hydration text-content mismatch) and #422 (hydration failed — server/client render divergence) fire on **every** /graph load on prod, observed both pre- and post-G4 deploy (c95af65) — so this pre-dates the G4 wave ([[FIX-811]]–[[FIX-815]]) and is not a regression from it. Non-fatal (React recovers by client-re-rendering the mismatched subtree) but noisy and a latent correctness risk. Likely an SSR/client render mismatch under a Suspense boundary in the graph route shell. Repro: load the prod /graph route with the browser console open. Next: decode the minified stack via the React error decoder to pin the mismatched component, then make that render deterministic (guard window/date/seeded-random output, or move it into a client-only boundary). <!--id:FIX-818-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Graph multi-select (shift-click) + selection pill + group-from-selection** — G5 (final /graph wave, follows [[FIX-815]]). Shift-click toggles a node in/out of a selection set (plain click keeps popup behavior; Esc / a pill button clears). Selected nodes render a dashed accent ring through the FIX-807 opacity/render pipeline. A floating bottom-center selection pill appears at selection ≥ 2 with: N SELECTED · Create group · Expand · Export CSV · Clear. Create group makes a CLIENT-ONLY member group ('Selection (N)') carrying explicit member node-ids — no server fetch (useGraphData skips memberIds groups), appears in the FOCUS list. Expand runs [[FIX-827]]'s incremental expand on each selected node, capped at 10 with a notice and ≤3 concurrent (graph rate bucket = 60/min). Export CSV is [[FIX-829]] selection-scoped. Box-select/rubber-band deliberately NOT built. <!--id:FIX-826-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Graph incremental node expansion (merge/collapse/promote)** — G5. Replaces the FIX-805 interim expand (which added the node as a whole focus entity). expandNode(id) fetches /api/graph/connections?entityId=<id>&depth=1 honoring current limit params (donation Top-N, votes_limit) and MERGES the response into client graph state (dedupe nodes+edges). Expanded-origin nodes do NOT join the focus list (no FOCUS row); expansion-added nodes render lightweight (dashed stroke) and the collapsed-hub +N badge flips to an expanded indicator. Each expansion tracks {originId, addedNodeIds, addedEdgeKeys} so NodePopup 'Collapse' removes exactly what it added minus anything a surviving edge / another expansion still references, and 'Promote to focus' converts the node to a real focus entity (full fetch) and clears its lightweight state. NodePopup wording 'Expand here (top N)' surfaces the Top-N contract. Cross-ref [[FIX-826]]. <!--id:FIX-827-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Graph edge detail sheet (aggregate pair totals)** — G5. Clicking a money edge (donation / opposition / contract_award — hasAmount types) opens a sheet over the right panel (DonorListPanel slide-in pattern): endpoint names+types, total $, tx count, edge-type label, occurredAt only when present (MV-sourced aggregate edges omit it), a 'Source: FEC — aggregated pair total' line, profile links to both endpoints, and a muted 'Full transaction ledger — coming soon' teaser (no paywall UI, no dead button). Vote edges get no sheet — clicking links to the proposal page. No new API route — everything is already in client state. Edge-click coexists with the FIX-807 hover pipeline via a transparent hit-area line layer. The full per-pair FEC schedule ledger is a deferred paid-tier feature (see [[FIX-830]]). Cross-ref [[FIX-826]]. <!--id:FIX-828-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Graph combined CSV export (nodes+edges, record_type)** — G5. Header gains Export ▾ (CSV selection / CSV all — selection entry disabled at <2 selected); the existing Screenshot button/panel stays untouched. Single file civitics-graph-<yyyymmdd>.csv with a leading record_type column (node|edge) so one file filters cleanly in Excel/Sheets. node rows: id,label,entity_type,party,state,industry,donation_total_usd,connection_count. edge rows: from_id,from_label,to_id,to_label,edge_type,amount_usd,tx_count,occurred_at. UTF-8 BOM, RFC-4180 quoting, client-side blob download (mirrors the PNG export anchor). Selection-scoped = selected nodes + edges among them (+ endpoint node rows). Cross-ref [[FIX-826]]. <!--id:FIX-829-->
- [ ] ⬜ L — **Graph edge full transaction ledger (paid tier — deferred design)** — DEFERRED DESIGN — filed by [[FIX-828]], NOT implemented this wave. The edge detail sheet (FIX-828) shows aggregate pair totals only. A future PAID TIER surfaces the full per-pair FEC Schedule ledger (individual transactions behind a donation/opposition/contract edge) with a free lookup limit (~10–25 lookups) before gating. Depends on the monetization phasing (credit/revenue mechanism must be live first — see the platform 'do not build AI/paid features before the credit mechanism' rule). Requires a new per-pair transactions API route, gating + free-limit metering, and paywall UI — none of which exist. Do not build until monetization phase. <!--id:FIX-830-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Graph group route ignores Top-N donor limit** — Force-graph group nodes (Full Senate etc.) always fetch 50 top donors regardless of the Top-donors dropdown: the client (useGraphData.fetchGroups) never forwarded `limit` and the /api/graph/group route defaulted to 50. Wire the client to send `limit=donationLimit` and align the route default to the shared DEFAULT_DONATION_LIMIT (25). Graph Polish P1 wave 1. <!--id:FIX-842-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Graph donor-profile 404 from group/pseudo nodes** — NodePopup's 'View donor profile' link stripped only a `type:` prefix, so group-route `donor-{uuid}` node ids 404'd at /donors/{id} and tail/bracket pseudo-nodes deep-linked to the wrong entity. Extract the first UUID anywhere in the id (fallback to the prefix strip), apply the same fix in useGraphData.expandNode + ForceGraph.popupExpandState so group donor nodes become expandable, and hide the profile button entirely for `^(tail|bracket):` ids. Graph Polish P1 wave 1. <!--id:FIX-843-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Graph treemap click-for-more dead (tooltip identity churn)** — useTooltip returned fresh show/hide closures every render, so any viz listing showTip/hideTip in a d3-render dep array (TreemapGraph) rebuilt the whole SVG on every mousemove — a real-mouse click died because the cell was torn down and rebuilt between mousedown and mouseup (motionless automated clicks worked). Stabilize show/hide with useCallback at the hook source; the setState setter is stable so deps become []. Graph Polish P1 wave 1. <!--id:FIX-844-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Graph treemap labels aggregate individual donors as 'Other'** — Every named individual donor has industry_label NULL in official_donor_rollup_mv (675k/675k local), so they and the small-dollar tail all landed in a treemap cell literally named 'Other'. Rename to 'Individual donors': tail cell name/industry/entity_type ('individual_aggregate'), an entity-type-aware industry fallback (individual → 'Individual donors', else 'Other'), and prettyDonorType cases. Bracket-split of the cell deferred — chord_donor_brackets_for_official buckets institutional donors too and can't filter to individuals [[FIX-848]]. Graph Polish P1 wave 1. <!--id:FIX-845-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Footer renders on /graph (breaks 100vh, adds scrollbar)** — The root layout renders <Footer /> on every route, so /graph (which owns the full viewport like NavBar's suppressed routes) got a footer and a page scrollbar. Add a thin client FooterGate wrapper mirroring NavBar's usePathname('/graph') suppression — Footer stays a server component, untouched elsewhere. Graph Polish P1 wave 1. <!--id:FIX-846-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Force graph node size uncapped; add size-scale option** — Force node radius used base + sqrt(donationTotal/100k)*2 uncapped — a $100M donor reaches ~+632px and swallows the canvas. Add sizeScale ('sqrt'|'log'|'linear', default 'sqrt') to ForceOptions threaded through getNodeRadius at both draw + resize call sites, a log branch (base + log10(v+1)*K), a hard radius cap (~72px) in ALL modes, a legend that computes from the same scale fn (was hardcoded sqrt), and a 'Size scale' select in GraphConfigPanel ForceSettings copying the treemap control. Graph Polish P1 wave 1. <!--id:FIX-847-->
- [x] 🟢 M — **Split treemap 'Individual donors' cell into size brackets** — Follow-up to [[FIX-845]]: split the aggregated 'Individual donors' treemap block into size-bracket cells ($200–1k / $1k–10k / etc.). Blocked on data: chord_donor_brackets_for_official (migration 20260509000003) buckets ALL donors by size with no entity_type filter, so it can't produce individual-only bracket totals. Needs a new/parameterized RPC that restricts to financial_entities.entity_type='individual' (and correctly attributes the small-dollar tail by bracket), then TreemapGraph renders the bracket children under the Individual donors group. <!--id:FIX-848-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Canonical graph node-id scheme + shared helper** — Graph used THREE un-normalized node-id conventions — connections route emits type-prefixed `official:{uuid}` / `financial_entity:{uuid}`, group route emitted `donor-{uuid}` + raw official uuids, FocusEntity ids are raw uuids. Raw-vs-prefixed `===` made the same real-world entity render as 2-3 distinct nodes by path. Fix: one shared `packages/graph/src/nodeId.ts` (makeNodeId/extractUuid/matchesFocus/isFocusNode), canonical scheme = `type:{uuid}` everywhere; migrate group route (donor-/raw-official/raw-agency/raw-gb ids) + GraphPage repNodes + all ForceGraph ad-hoc `fe.id===n.id || n.id.endsWith(':'+fe.id)` sites to the helper. FocusEntity ids stay raw (saved-view back-compat). Post-G5 wave 2. <!--id:FIX-849-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Graph per-focus ownership tracking: prune + SharedConnectionsBar** — Two removal defects rooted in raw-vs-prefixed id drift [[FIX-849]]. (1) Entity removal was a state NO-OP — deleting a second focus entity left the graph unchanged. (2) Group removal OVER-pruned — it dropped every edge incident to a member, killing surviving focuses' edges to shared nodes. (3) SharedConnectionsBar was permanently empty (`focusIds.has(e.fromId)` compared raw uuid set vs `official:{uuid}` endpoints). Fix: record {addedNodeIds,addedEdgeKeys} per focus id at fetch time (mirror FIX-827 expansions machinery); on removal drop edges owned solely by the removed focus, then prune nodes with no surviving edge and no surviving owner; SharedConnectionsBar matches via the helper. Post-G5 wave 2. <!--id:FIX-850-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Graph field-wise node merge (focus-set-invariant donation identity) + live depth chip** — Adding a second focus entity clobbered the first's node payloads via last-write-wins `Map.set` merge — e.g. add Kamala (FFPAC $498M donor), then add Trump (FFPAC opposition-scoped, NO donationTotal) and FFPAC visually lost its Kamala identity. Fix: field-wise merge — derive donationTotal/connectionCount in one post-merge pass over the edge set so they're invariant to fetch order; collapsed = prev && incoming. Also: the depth chip [1|2] in FocusTree was inert — `updateEntity(id,{depth})` changed state but fetchedIds skipped the already-fetched entity, so nothing refetched. Fix: a depth change evicts that entity from fetchedIds + its owned nodes/edges [[FIX-850]] and refetches it surgically. Post-G5 wave 2. <!--id:FIX-851-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Graph depth-2 connector invariant + edge-guard + client zero-edge drop + truncation badge** — Depth 2 on most officials scattered dozens of tiny 2-node islands. Root cause: the individual-donor bracket loop keyed buckets per-recipient (`c.to_id`) and suppressed donor nodes/edges GLOBALLY, so a hop-2 connector donor (gave to focus A AND neighbor B) got deleted and replaced by an unlinked `bracket:{B}:{tier}` island — B lost its only tie to the focus component. Fix (server): restore the connector invariant — an individual whose donation rows reach >=2 distinct recipients IN THE RESPONSE passes through as a real node (reuse individualMode==='connector' logic); only add a donor to skipEntityKeys when ALL its rows were aggregated (fixes the edge-guard dropping non-donation rows whose endpoint was bracketed). Fix (client): drop non-focus/non-user/non-group nodes with zero incident edges after merge; consume meta.depth2Truncated / partial as a canvas truncation badge (read by nothing today). Post-G5 wave 2 [[FIX-849]]. <!--id:FIX-852-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Treemap group scoping ignores governingBody (silent global fallback)** — Treemap group mode forwarded only chamber/party/state, but every built-in congress preset (Full Senate/House, chamber+party) filters on governingBody, so nothing was forwarded and the route fell through to the GLOBAL top-500 officials (House + state legislators included). Fix: treemap route learns a governingBody param resolving the cohort via officials.governing_body_id (currentGoverningBodyMembers, the FIX-495/FIX-470 non-polluted path — role_title counts Arizona state rep David Marshall as the 437th US House member); chamber=senate|house aliases to the federal gb slugs. Client maps federal gb slugs to chamber, other gb slugs to governingBody, and shows an explicit unsupported empty state (committee/official_role/empty filter) instead of the silent global request. Cross-ref [[FIX-849]]. <!--id:FIX-853-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Chord: surface untagged donor money, RPC errors, honest empty state + dedup RPC** — Per-official chord dropped the untagged bucket (chord/route.ts entity aggregate), so an all-untagged official (e.g. the whale: $268M/309k donors untagged vs ~$10M tagged) rendered as an empty 200 with a misleading 'pipeline processing' platitude; RPC errors/timeouts were ALSO swallowed into empty 200s (FIX-839 measured 6.8s aggregate / 8.8s top-pacs for the whale on warm local — over the 8s service_role ceiling). Fix: render untagged as one neutral 'Untagged donors' arc; when it is the ONLY money, an honest empty state ('$83M from 755 donors not yet industry-tagged'); RPC failures return a 502 the client renders as 'couldn't load'. Plus a migration deduping chord_industry_flows_for_official — its GROUP BY included display_icon/display_label so an industry with inconsistent icons across donors (labor: '' + 👷; lobby: '' + 🏛) split into two rows; now groups by industry key only, picking display fields deterministically. Cross-ref [[FIX-839]]. <!--id:FIX-854-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Choropleth: honest measure/legend label, degenerate-domain overlay, settings section, congressional band** — voting-divergence colors by party lean but the legend said 'Party cohesion'; the client showed a uniform fallback color with no 'no data' signal when every district value was null or the domain was degenerate (extent min===max); choropleth/scatter/gantt had NO settings entry in GraphConfigPanel. Fix: legend/measure label reflects party-control (diverging blue-white-red); explicit 'No data for this band/measure' overlay on degenerate/all-null domains; Choropleth settings section (band + measure). Chosen data path = Path B (point the Voting Divergence preset at the congressional band): sizing found congressional-district geometry is ALREADY present on prod AND local (436/436, not 'missing' as previously believed) and fed-rep linkage is 361/443 prod — so Path B is a one-line preset band change (S), no geometry load. Path A (state-legislature district_jurisdiction_id backfill) is filed separately. Cross-ref [[FIX-217]]. <!--id:FIX-855-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Viz scope field: partition pickers into Focus/Platform + canvas 'ignores focus' badge** — Several viz silently ignore focus (scatter/sankey/choropleth/alignment are platform-wide) or scope only partially (hierarchy/chord), with no signal to the user that focusing an entity does nothing. Fix: add scope: 'focus' | 'platform' | 'hybrid' to VizDefinition; assign force/treemap/sunburst/matrix/gantt=focus, sankey/scatter/choropleth/alignment=platform, hierarchy/chord=hybrid; partition both viz pickers (GraphHeader dropdown + GraphConfigPanel picker) into Focus/Platform sections; show a canvas badge ('Platform-wide data — not affected by focus') when focus is non-empty but the active viz is platform-scoped. Cross-ref [[FIX-853]]. <!--id:FIX-856-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Sankey agencyId wiring + agency-spending preset; scatter peer-class filter + rings for all focused agencies** — Sankey never sent a scope param even though the server has supported ?agencyId= since FIX-218 (a fully-built, never-called capability); scatter ignored focus entirely (primaryGroup prop declared, never used). Fix: when the primary focus entity is an agency, sankey sends ?agencyId= (mirrors hierarchy's rootEntityId wiring) and labels the pill with the scope; add one preset 'Agency spending flows' (agency-focused sankey); scatter highlights/ghosts by the focused agency's agencyType peer class (field already in every payload row) and draws rings for ALL focused agencies, not just the primary. No new API routes. Cross-ref [[FIX-218]], [[FIX-856]]. <!--id:FIX-857-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Scatter blank render: no ResizeObserver -> draw-once-at-mount blanks on prod** — ScatterGraph drew exactly once when data loaded, using the container size at that instant, and never redrew — the only viz in the FIX-733 trio (with choropleth/gantt) lacking a ResizeObserver that every working viz (force/treemap/chord/sunburst/sankey) has. On prod the /api/graph/agency-staffing endpoint returns 122 rows with 26 agencies plottable on the default log-log axes (fte x contract), so it was a render-timing gap under the next/dynamic ssr:false async mount, not missing data (local plots ~25 fine, so not reproducible locally). Fix: refactor the draw into a useCallback invoked by both the data effect AND a ResizeObserver whose initial observe() fires at real container size. Verify on prod browser QA. Cross-ref [[FIX-810]], [[FIX-857]]. <!--id:FIX-858-->
- [ ] 🟡 M — **Choropleth Path A: complete state-legislature district_jurisdiction_id backfill + refresh local clone** — Follow-up to [[FIX-855]] (which shipped Path B — the congressional choropleth band). Path A completes the SLD choropleth: on PROD 6524/7459 (87%) state legislators already have metadata->>'district_jurisdiction_id' (so sld_u/sld_l largely render there), but the LOCAL prod-clone is stale at 11/7465 — refresh the clone first so SLD is testable locally. The remaining ~935 unlinked need a NORMALIZED join: officials.district_name is 100% populated but raw ('66', 'Ward 6', 'Strafford 21', 'Chittenden-18') while jurisdictions district rows key on metadata->>'district_id' zero-padded ('066') + short_name ('HD 066') + name — the naive name/short_name join matches 0. Numeric-normalized join (pad/strip) covers most; the non-numeric tail (NH floterial 'Strafford 21', VT 'Chittenden-18', DC 'Ward 6', multi-member) is per-state and needs special-casing. Data-state change: idempotent backfill script deriving ids via joins (no hardcoded uuids), local -> verify -> confirm with Craig -> prod. Cross-ref [[FIX-217]]. <!--id:FIX-859-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Choropleth black map — resolveToken rgb() form unparseable by d3-color** — Choropleth painted an all-black map. `resolveToken` (packages/graph/src/tokens.ts) returned space-separated `rgb(R G B)`; browsers parse it but d3-color does not (`d3.color('rgb(43 74 139)')` -> null -> `interpolateRgb` emits `rgb(0,0,0)`). Prod census: all 369 valued districts painted black, the 67 null districts got the direct .attr fill. Fixed at the tokens layer to emit comma-separated `rgb(R, G, B)` — valid CSS everywhere AND d3-parseable — which also un-blackens every other d3-interpolated fill (treemap blend, sunburst darken). Consumer sweep: withAlpha/toHexColor already tolerant (split on [\\s,/]+). Unit test pins d3.color(formatRgbTriplet(...)) !== null. Rider: scatter Y-axis displays dollars not raw cents. <!--id:FIX-860-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Header entity search hardcodes type:official — agency scoping dead from search** — `handleHeaderEntitySelect` (apps/civitics/app/graph/GraphPage.tsx ~636) hardcoded `type:'official'` for every header-search add, so agencies/financial entities/proposals became officials. Sankey/Hierarchy/Gantt agency scoping (FIX-857) therefore never fired when the agency was added from the header search. The search API already returns `type` per row (official/agency/proposal/financial_entity). Fix: thread the row's entity type through GraphHeader's onEntitySelect callback and map to FocusEntity type. Recents re-add already threads stored type (GroupBrowser). Stale recents keep their old type — acceptable staleness, no migration. Rider: FIX-856 platform badge moved off bottom-center so it stops overlapping the scatter X-axis label. See [[FIX-857]]. <!--id:FIX-861-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Treemap: super-PAC IE money as a separate "Independent support" group** — The official treemap only showed direct donations; super-PAC independent-expenditure money (ie_support) was invisible. The entity route now reads official_donor_rollup_mv for BOTH donation and ie_support (ie_oppose stays excluded from Top Donors) and renders ie_support as its own "Independent support" group (named spenders + a rolled-up tail, badged with a lightning glyph) rather than folding it into donor cells (Craig decision 2b). Lands alongside the [[FIX-848]] bracket work in apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap/route.ts + packages/graph/src/TreemapGraph.tsx. <!--id:FIX-862-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Force graph: wire the per-type donation/contract floor into edgeVisible (fix the placebo)** — The per-type "Min $" floor (connections[type].minAmount) only filtered the treemap route; on the force graph it was a placebo (nothing read it). edgeVisible now hides named money edges (hasAmount types) whose amountUsd is below the floor, but EXEMPTS bracket/tail aggregate edges (their amountUsd sums many small donors, so a floor that hides one small donor must not hide the "12,000 smaller donors" rollup). A minAmountKey effect re-applies on floor change; the slider helper text notes it applies to named donors. packages/graph/src/visualizations/ForceGraph.tsx + GraphConfigPanel.tsx. <!--id:FIX-863-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Force graph: Contracts node-size encoding (contract_total)** — Adds contract_total to the force nodeSizeEncoding. useGraphData stamps node.contractTotal by summing amountUsd over incident contract_award edges (dollars, not cents) so getNodeRadius stays a field read; the dropdown option enables only when contract edges are loaded (mirrors the donations gating). Legend notes the total is a lower bound over the loaded top-500-by-amount contract edges. packages/graph/src/{visualizations/ForceGraph.tsx,hooks/useGraphData.ts,types.ts,components/GraphConfigPanel.tsx}. <!--id:FIX-864-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Force graph: domain-aware linear node scale + live-distribution legend; drop placebo size options** — The linear node-size branch was base+v (v in dollars/1000), so every node past ~56k dollars pinned at the 72px cap and a mid donor looked identical to a whale. Now base+(v/domainMax)*(MAX-base) over the live max, so sizes are differentiated. The node-size legend reference values are computed from the live node distribution (nice-rounded min>0 / geometric mid / max) via the SAME scaledRadius fn under EVERY scale mode, replacing the hardcoded 100K/1M/10M — and it now covers contract_total too. Pure size helpers extracted to packages/graph/src/nodeSize.ts with a unit test. The placebo votes_cast/bills_sponsored/years_in_office options (they silently fell through to connection_count) are removed from the dropdown; enum values kept for saved-view back-compat and coerced to connection_count on load. <!--id:FIX-865-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Treemap: correct NodePopup identity payloads (cure the /donors/{name} 404)** — Every treemap donor tooltip/popup passed a blanket type "financial"; a PAC-hierarchy cell whose id fell back to a display name then deep-linked to /donors/{name} and 404d (Craig observed). Donor cells now map entity_type to a real NodeType (individual/pac/corporation); bracket/tail/IE aggregates use bracket:/tail: pseudo ids and the individual_bracket type; PAC cells without a real id use a pseudo id (never a display name); and NodePopup suppresses the profile button for ANY id lacking a uuid. The bracket->DonorListPanel drill is deferred (treemap has no DonorListPanel wiring; the dead button is gated off). packages/graph/src/{TreemapGraph.tsx,components/NodePopup.tsx}. <!--id:FIX-866-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Treemap: applicable with empty focus (global officials mode)** — The viz-registry treemap isApplicable required donations-in-graph or an official/PAC focus, so Treemap greyed out in the empty state even though the route serves a global officials-by-party treemap with no focus (and the empty-state preset card offers it). Adds an empty-focus escape (focus.entities.length === 0 -> applicable) while keeping the grey-out for non-donor focuses like proposal-only. packages/graph/src/visualizations/registry.ts. <!--id:FIX-867-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Donation-side industry-tag fan-out: multi-tag donors double-counted across sectors on graph money surfaces** — Sweep rider from the FIX-873 session (which fixed the contract-RECIPIENT-side multi-industry-tag fan-out). The DONATION/donor side has the same class unfixed on 5 surfaces that JOIN entity_tags(industry) on the DONOR and aggregate money WITHOUT a single-tag-per-entity pick, so a donor carrying more than one industry tag has its dollars counted under EVERY tag (or emitted as N duplicate donor rows). Measured multi-tag financial_entities: 1058 local / 1654 prod; of those, 478 local are donation donors (from_id). Sites, all donation donor-side: (1) official_sector_dollars_mv (FIX-506) INNER JOIN entity_tags, GROUP BY (official, et.tag) counts a multi-tag donor under each tag AND drops untagged donors entirely (INNER JOIN); consumers get_group_sector_totals + chord_sector_vote_for_officials. (2) chord_industry_flows_mv (FIX-207/222, nightly) LEFT JOIN, GROUP BY (COALESCE(et.tag,untagged), party_chamber) double-counts multi-tag donors in the platform-wide industry-party chord. (3) chord_industry_flows_for_official(uuid) (FIX-854) GROUP BY COALESCE(et.tag,untagged): FIX-854 only collapsed SAME-tag display-metadata splits, a donor with genuinely DIFFERENT tags still fans out. (4) chord_top_pacs_for_official(uuid,int) GROUP BY fe.id + tag: a multi-tag PAC emitted as N duplicate rows each with full total, LIMIT can be consumed by dups. (5) get_official_donors(uuid) GROUP BY fe.id + tag: multi-tag donor emitted as N duplicate donor rows. Do NOT bundle one fix: each surface has its own display semantics (per-donor row list vs per-sector aggregate vs an INNER-JOIN MV that also drops untagged) and needs its own equality analysis + before/after delta, exactly as FIX-873 did for the contract side. The deterministic fix per site is the FIX-836/777/873 smallest-tag DISTINCT ON pick (or the refresh_group_donor_rollup _gdr_ind MIN() pick, already the SAFE pattern). Verified SAFE this sweep (already single-tag): refresh_group_donor_rollup (_gdr_ind MIN(display_label) per donor), sector_affinity_rebuild_officials (FIX-777/872 DISTINCT ON), and the FIX-704/518/836 donor-rollup machinery. Cross-ref [[FIX-873]] [[FIX-506]] [[FIX-854]] [[FIX-780]] [[FIX-839]] [[FIX-777]]. <!--id:FIX-875-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Group route live donor fallback pages full entity_connections fan-out — budget-blown on whale cohorts; swap to a cohort-aggregation RPC** — Any official cohort that is NOT gb-materialized (`rollupGbId` null — ad-hoc/custom groups, state-narrowed gb groups per [[FIX-495]], legacy state/party groups, and any gb whose `group_donor_rollup` row is a miss) falls through [[FIX-500]]'s rollup-first read to the live path in `apps/civitics/app/api/graph/group/route.ts`. That path (1) pages EVERY `entity_connections` donation row for the members — including the individual-donor long tail — through `fetchPagedConnections` under `DONOR_FETCH_BUDGET_MS = 8000` with a `maxRows: 50000` ceiling, then (2) resolves every distinct donor's name/entity_type/sector via 100-id `financial_entities` chunks + `fetchIndustryTagsByEntityId`, and only then (3) discards individuals JS-side and takes top-N institutional. Individuals are ~87% of the rows fetched and are thrown away. Local floors (stale clone — prod is larger): senators average ~1,433 donation edges each, p50 79, p90 3,140, max 38,617 — so a random 4-senator cohort has roughly a 1-in-3 chance of containing a top-decile member, one whale alone means 30–50+ sequential pages inside the 8s budget, and stage 2 for thousands of distinct donors is dozens-to-hundreds of concurrent PostgREST chunk queries. `raceWithBudget` also abandons rather than aborts the losing fetch, so it keeps burning pool connections after the flagged response ships. Reproduced 2026-07-24 on `governingBody=senate&state=TX` (2 members, 34,285 edges, Ted Cruz 31,219): LOCAL `donorFetchError: true` / `donorFetchErrorCode: null` at 15.8s, PROD same flag at 8.97s; a repeat local run surfaced the stage-2 failure mode directly (`financial_entities chunk error: TypeError: fetch failed`, 20.7s). This is the [[FIX-497]] fail-open doing its job over a request-path live aggregation that should never have been on the request path. Fix: replace stages 1+2 of the official-branch live path with ONE server-side aggregation RPC over `entity_connections` — same source, same filters, so donorCount / totalDonatedUsd / the top-N list stay byte-identical to a successful live run; uses the existing `entity_connections_donation_to_official_idx` partial covering index. Same caller-swap pattern as [[FIX-774]], same request-path-materialization class the [[FIX-775]] audit closed elsewhere. NOT `official_donor_rollup_mv` ([[FIX-518]]/[[FIX-704]]/[[FIX-809]]/[[FIX-832]]), which was the first candidate: its `rank` is computed over ALL donors including individuals, so `rank <= 200 AND entity_type <> 'individual'` keeps only the institutional donors that outrank the individual long tail — measured on the TX cohort, 196 donors / $1.09M top-25 vs the live path's 1,126 / $16.9M (a 94% understatement, 9 of 25 donors overlapping; Ted Cruz alone has 174 individuals in his top-200, leaving 26 institutional slots). Independently, the MV is built from `financial_relationships` ($32.0M for Cruz) while the route reads `entity_connections` ($41.1M), a 28% source gap. Documented here so the MV is not proposed again for cohort donor display. <!--id:FIX-883-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **BUNDLE AS GROUP discards the row selection — bare-officials handoff aggregates the whole platform; add officialIds mode end-to-end** — The /search explorer's BUNDLE AS GROUP button (`ExplorerActionBar.handleBundleConfirm`) derives only a "dominant kind" from the checked rows and hands off `/graph?groupType=official&groupName=…` — **the selected row ids are discarded**. The dialog says "N items will be sent to the graph as a group", then sends none of them. GraphPage's handoff decode turns that into a bare `{entity_type:'official'}` filter; the group route's legacy no-gb path resolves up to 1,000 active officials (`.limit(1000)`) and calls `get_cohort_top_donors()` at whole-platform scale, which blows the statement timeout (57014) and surfaces as the [[FIX-497]] fail-open donor popup with a member count in the hundreds/thousands. This is the root cause of Craig's 2026-07-24 "4 random senators time out" sighting — NOT the filter-cohort perf class closed by [[FIX-883]]/[[FIX-884]]/[[FIX-885]]. ADD TO GRAPH (the `addEntityIds` path) is unaffected; the lossiness is specific to the bundle path, and predates the Browse redesign — [[FIX-751]] explicitly preserved "exact pre-W1 handoff semantics". Fix: `GroupFilter.officialIds?: string[]` serialized by `graphGroupParams`, decoded by the GraphPage handoff, and honored by the group route's official branch (validate/dedup/cap 50, skip cohort resolution, straight into `get_cohort_top_donors()` — the [[FIX-883]] RPC already takes `uuid[]`, nothing UI-side ever fed it ids). BUNDLE AS GROUP enabled only when every selected row is an official. Ids-groups are in-session only (BrowseState cannot express hand-picked ids, so no saved-view persistence). Client-only shift-click selection groups ([[FIX-826]]) are untouched. <!--id:FIX-886-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Group route: refuse degenerate/oversized live cohorts (422) instead of aggregate-and-timeout** — Guard the /api/graph/group official branch's NON-materialized (live `get_cohort_top_donors()`) path against cohorts no legitimate UI affordance produces. Two refusals, both structured 422 so [[FIX-490]]'s client error path surfaces a visible notice rather than a blank bubble: (1) a legacy no-gb cohort with NO narrowing at all (no party, no state, no officialIds) — "all active officials" is never a legitimate group, and today it is reachable only via the [[FIX-886]] bug and hand-built URLs — returns `filter_too_broad`; (2) a resolved cohort larger than a measured `MAX_LIVE_COHORT` returns `cohort_too_large` with the memberCount in the body. The gb rollup-first path ([[FIX-500]]) is untouched — materialized cohorts read a single indexed top-N regardless of size, so the guard applies only where the request-path aggregation actually runs. Cap chosen from measured RPC latency across cohort sizes on prod (warm), with clear headroom above the largest legitimate live cohort (House-class ~436 must pass). Complements [[FIX-883]], which made the aggregation server-side but left it unbounded in cohort size. <!--id:FIX-887-->
- [ ] 🟢 M — **Saved views can't persist a hand-picked ids group (BrowseState is a predicate)** — [[FIX-886]] added `GroupFilter.officialIds` so BUNDLE AS GROUP means what it says, but an ids-group is **in-session only**: `BrowseState` is a predicate (scope + facets + q), and cannot express a hand-picked id list, so `compileBrowseToGroupFilter` never emits `officialIds` and the v2 saved-view payload has nowhere to put it. Consequences today: saving a view while an ids-group is in focus silently drops the group's membership, and a shared/reloaded session resolves nothing. Deciding this needs a call on semantics that [[FIX-886]] deliberately deferred — a saved id list is a SNAPSHOT (members who leave office, get merged by [[FIX-544]], or are promoted by `promote_candidate_to_elected()` would silently drift or 404), so the options are (a) persist ids with a resolution-time reconciliation pass that reports drops, (b) persist nothing and make the UI say ids-groups are session-scoped, or (c) materialize the selection into a real `user_custom_groups` row with an explicit membership table. The coverage manifest and a compiler test currently pin option (b) as the status quo — changing it must update both. <!--id:FIX-888-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **tag-groups route silently truncates at the PostgREST 1000-row cap** — `/api/graph/tag-groups` aggregates proposal topic counts in JS over an unbounded `.select()`, on the stated assumption that 'the row volume is bounded (~1.4k topic rows total)'. That assumption expired: local now has 5,600 qualifying rows and prod ~7,500 after [[FIX-889]]. PostgREST caps a plain select at `max_rows=1000`, so the route sees ~1,000 arbitrary rows and reports counts that are wrong by roughly 6x — measured local sum of returned counts was 986 against 5,600 real rows. Every displayed tag-group count is therefore understated, and low-volume topics can fall below the `count >= 10` floor and vanish from the browse list entirely. Discovered while verifying [[FIX-889]]; pre-existing and independent of it. Fix is a server-side GROUP BY (an RPC returning one jsonb aggregate, per the standing `reference_postgrest_rpc_row_cap` rule that set-returning RPCs must not be `.range()`-paginated), not a bigger `.limit()`. <!--id:FIX-892-->

## DASHBOARD

- [ ] 🟠 L — **Add sparklines to stat cards** — build `/api/stats/trends` returning last 30 days of daily counts per metric <!--id:FIX-090-->
- [ ] 🟡 M — **DEFERRED --- Parse FIXES.md into per-phase task lists with real done state** — reads `docs/done.log`; replaces hard-coded PHASE1_TASKS <!--id:FIX-095-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Dashboard AlertBanner copy self-contradicts on prod** — Dashboard AlertBanner copy self-contradicts on prod: "System issue detected: Connections pipeline healthy · The team has been notified and is investigating" — alert condition or message interpolation is wrong; possibly related to the paused ec-rebuild ([[FIX-697]]/[[FIX-703]]). Spotted during Wave-1 prod verification. <!--id:FIX-725-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **connections_pipeline_healthy REBUILD_STALE_MS too tight for pg_cron Mon+Wed cadence** — getSelfTests in apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts used REBUILD_STALE_MS=4.5d, calibrated for the retired Sun+Wed GHA rebuild cadence (max gap ~3.5-4d). [[FIX-688]] moved the rebuild to pg_cron (rebuild-ec-full Mon 08:00 UTC, rebuild-ec-incremental Wed 08:00 UTC), so the max gap between successful runs is Wed→Mon = 5 days, plus Monday's full can run up to its 6h CALL budget before writing its completing row. On a healthy week the newest completed row reaches ~5.25d and trips the 4.5d threshold ~12h every week (recurring false alarm). Bumped REBUILD_STALE_MS to 6d and corrected the stale 'Sun+Wed GHA' comment + empty-state detail string to the pg_cron reality. Cross-ref [[FIX-725]]. <!--id:FIX-831-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Enrichment-backlog tile counts a status value that does not exist; abandoned processing claims are invisible** — The Data Health card's enrichment-backlog sub-line counted `enrichment_queue.status='in_progress'` — a value `public.enrichment_queue` cannot hold. Its vocabulary is `pending | processing | done | failed` (migration `20260420030000_enrichment_queue.sql`, FIX-064) plus `skipped_no_source_text` / `skipped_feature_retired` (FIX-895/896). `in_progress` belongs to `shadow.enrichment_queue` from the stage-1 schema (`20260421000006`) — a table that does not exist in the database at all (exactly one relation named `enrichment_queue` across every schema, in `public`; the `shadow` schema itself was retired at the 2026-04-22 cutover). The count therefore returned 0 from day one and the tile has always read 'queue idle'. A stale comment above `getPipelines()` claiming the table was 'from FIX-101 stage 1 schema' is the root cause and is corrected here. Consequence: 44 abandoned `processing` claims (all `task_type='tag'`, claimed 2026-04-29, orphaned by an interrupted drain wave) sat invisible for three months — invisible to `pending_tag`/`pending_summary` (not pending) and to `in_progress` (never existed). Fixed: `getPipelines()` now reports `processing` and `stale_processing` (claim older than `ENRICHMENT_STALE_CLAIM_MINUTES` = 60, a deliberately looser threshold than `data:drain:status`'s 10-minute reclaim default — the script answers 'what can I reclaim now', the dashboard answers 'does this look abandoned'). The threshold governs only the warn; `processing` is reported unconditionally. `backlogTotal`/`backlogTone` stay pending-driven. Note the persisted `status_snapshot` payload also carries this shape, so `/api/claude/status/core` serves the old key until the next 10-min cron write — self-healing, renders 'queue idle' in the interim. Not fixed here and deliberately out of scope: surfacing permanently-`failed` rows (148), and whether a scheduled reclaim sweep deserves a cron. <!--id:FIX-924-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Vercel billing math was wrong: Pro includes $20 of usage, so the dashboard showed $31.38 on a $0.00-overage month** — Craig-stated 2026-08-16: Vercel Pro is \$20/month AND THAT \$20 BUYS \$20 OF INCLUDED USAGE, so the bill is \$20 base + max(0, metered usage - \$20). The snapshot and the dashboard both treated the ENTIRE EffectiveCost sum as money owed. EffectiveCost is the LIST VALUE of all consumption PLUS the prorated Pro subscription line, so the headline was the gross list price of everything — and every dollar of it sat below the included credit. **Measured on prod 2026-08-16 (platform_usage_snapshot, fetched_at 03:13 UTC): the card read \$31.38/mo when the actual projected billable overage was \$0.00 with \$8.62 of the credit still unspent.** BEFORE: gross MTD \$15.1852, projected \$31.3828, displayed as the Vercel cost. AFTER: usage MTD \$5.5078 (gross minus the \$9.6774 Pro line), credit remaining \$14.49, projected usage \$11.38, projected billable overage \$0.00, projected total bill \$20.00. **This is a DETECTION defect, not a cosmetic one.** A headline that reads \$31 on a perfectly normal month cannot alarm — there is no band you can put on it that separates healthy from burning, which is part of why the 08-15 burn had nowhere to show up. **The Pro line must come out FIRST.** The FOCUS charge export carries the subscription as its own ServiceName (Pro), accruing \$20/31 = \$0.6452/day — verified against prod where day 15 read exactly \$9.6774. That line is the SUBSCRIPTION, not consumption, so it does not draw down the credit the subscription buys; counting it would peg the account at 100 pct of credit from the first of every month. Added plan_base_usd as a first-class field on VercelUsage rather than deriving it downstream from cost_breakdown, which is sliced to top-8 before persistence ([[FIX-1041]]). Matching is anchored /^(hobby|pro|enterprise)\$/i, not a substring — a substring test on pro swallows Fluid Provisioned Memory and silently inflates remaining credit by a real consumption line. **New pure module packages/db/src/vercel-billing.ts** (no clock, no network, no DB) with table-driven tests whose first row is the live prod reading, so the old interpretation cannot be reintroduced silently. **Two new platform_limits rows:** included_usage_usd (consumption vs the \$20 credit — finally a percentage bar that means something, warns at 80 pct) and billable_overage_usd (THE headline). monthly_spend_usd is KEPT unchanged as the gross list value because it is the leading indicator and the only figure that reconciles against the Vercel dashboard. The \$20 lives in platform_limits.included_limit and is READ BY the collector, so retuning the credit is an UPDATE and not a deploy. **CYCLE-BASIS CAVEAT, stated rather than hidden:** getVercelUsage queries charges from the first of the CALENDAR month and the Pro line prorates over 31, so the projection is calendar-month; the Vercel usage page separately describes an Aug 14 - Sep 14 cycle and nothing in the charges API discriminates between the two. daysInCycle is an argument, not a hard-coded 31, so correcting the basis is a one-line call-site change. **USER ITEM (open):** is there a hard page-me-at-\$X number for true overage? billable_overage_usd.included_limit is provisionally 20 (page when the bill doubles) with warning at 50 pct = \$10/mo; answering the question is an UPDATE. Cross-ref [[FIX-648]], [[FIX-1041]], [[FIX-1044]]. <!--id:FIX-1046-->
- [ ] 🟢 M — **Admin UI for editing platform_limits values and thresholds** — Craig's intent (2026-08-17, surfaced during [[FIX-1050]]): platform_limits included_limit / warning_pct / critical_pct should be tunable from the dashboard, not via SQL. Every threshold change to date has been a migration — [[FIX-1044]] derived the 3000/hr origin-request limit, [[FIX-1046]] set the 20 USD credit, [[FIX-1050]] moved R2 to 24 GiB and the Vercel page to 10 USD — and each one needed a deploy to land a number that is, by design, meant to be retuned as the platform grows. DESIGN-GATED ON TWO THINGS, both of which must land first. (1) The UI rework — this belongs inside whatever the platform-costs card becomes, not bolted onto the current section. (2) An admin-auth story, which does not exist today: every civic surface is public-read by design and platform_limits itself is publicly readable, so the read side is already fine but a WRITE path needs a real authenticated-admin boundary. Do not ship an edit control that writes through the existing public client. Note the two-row coupling [[FIX-1050]] introduced — vercel.billable_overage_usd and vercel.overage_present encode one behaviour across two rows, so an editor that lets them drift silently breaks the first-cent guarantee; either surface them as one control or validate the pair. Not scheduled. <!--id:FIX-1051-->

## INFRASTRUCTURE & PERFORMANCE

- [ ] 🟡 M — **Ratchet ESLint warnings to zero and enforce --max-warnings 0** — Follow-up to [[FIX-459]], which wired the ESLint presets across all packages + apps and promoted the tests.yml lint step to blocking by downgrading several high-volume rules from error to warn (so the gate could go green without ballooning that PR). The lint gate is now blocking but warnings are still allowed. Backlog at FIX-459 landing time: ~76 warnings — no-console x23, @typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars x21, @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any x21, no-inner-declarations x5, react/no-unescaped-entities x2, @next/next/no-img-element x2, react/no-children-prop x1, react-hooks/exhaustive-deps x1. The ratchet: (1) drive the backlog to zero — remove/underscore unused vars, replace explicit any with real types, gate console behind a logger or allow-list, fix the genuine react/no-children-prop + exhaustive-deps sites; (2) re-promote the downgraded rules in packages/config/eslint/{base,next}.js from warn back to error as each category reaches zero; (3) add --max-warnings 0 to every package lint script (or 'next lint --max-warnings 0' for apps) so new warnings fail CI. Do it rule-by-rule to keep diffs reviewable. See packages/config/eslint/base.js + next.js for the current warn-level overrides flagged with FIX-459 comments. <!--id:FIX-460-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **Wire egress to a kill switch — DEFERRED until an accurate billable-egress source exists** — Deferred by design (not a bug to fix now). [[FIX-602]] established that the dashboard egress figure is a raw-NIC UPPER BOUND written as source='estimated', not billable egress. Auto-tripping the connection_graph_live kill switch on that inflated proxy would false-fire every 10-min cron cycle — exactly the failure [[FIX-602]] removes. Supabase exposes no public Management API endpoint for billable egress (probed 2026-06-19; every egress endpoint 404s). When/if an accurate billable-egress source lands (undocumented endpoint appears, or a manual monthly figure is wired), add 'supabase.egress_bytes' to the connection_graph_live switch's metrics array (it already watches api_requests_7d + disk_used_bytes at auto_trip_threshold_pct 95) and flip the snapshot source back to 'api'. Until then this stays OPEN as a tracked dependency. <!--id:FIX-603-->

- [x] 🟠 M — **enrichment-light starves the daily AI/MV tail on weekly (Sunday-equivalent) runs** — Surfaced by the FIX-743 supervised force_weekly run (GHA 28755577316, 2026-07-05): enrichment-light-phase was SIGTERM'd at its 120-min ceiling. The full weekly stack (edgar ~4m, courtlistener ~12m, openstates API ~27m, elections ~19m, committees, agency-*, tag-industry) consumed most of the budget, then the DAILY tail (tag_rules, tag_ai, ai_summaries, and the two deferred MV refreshes refresh_donor_party_rollup_mv + refresh_entity_connection_stats_mv) got squeezed — the run was cut mid tag_rules, so ai_summaries and both deferred MV refreshes did NOT run. This is inherent to carrying weekly + daily work in one 120-min job and recurs every real Sunday, not just forced runs; the FIX-740 split (LittleSis out to enrichment-heavy, USASpending out to its own dispatch) didn't cover it. Fix: extract the daily AI/MV tail (the `if (runEnrichmentLight)` block after the weekly `if (isWeekly)` section in packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts — tag_rules/tag_ai/ai_summaries + the deferred MV refreshes) out of the weekly-carrying job, mirroring the FIX-292/FIX-740 phase-split pattern (e.g. a distinct 'enrichment-tail' phase + GHA job that needs: enrichment-light but if: !cancelled(), so the daily derived work always runs even when the weekly ingest overruns). Cross-ref [[FIX-740]], [[FIX-743]], [[FIX-739]]. <!--id:FIX-744-->

- [ ] 🟢 S — **db-query.mjs cannot CALL committing procedures — add a --call/--no-txn mode** — scripts/db-query.mjs always passes --single-transaction, so CALLing any procedure that COMMITs internally (the whole FIX-703/704/715/717/718 pg_cron-procedure family) fails with 'invalid transaction termination' — hit during the [[FIX-717]]/[[FIX-718]] local bootstraps. Workarounds used: bare psql against 127.0.0.1:54322 for local CALLs; a session-scratchpad write-capable psql wrapper for the supervised prod CALLs (prod db-query is deliberately READ ONLY and must stay that way). Durable fix: add a --call (or --no-txn) mode that drops --single-transaction; --local only by default — a prod CALL path must NOT silently weaken db-query's read-only prod contract. Either keep prod CALLs on the explicit-confirmation flow with a separate committed wrapper (promote the scratchpad prod_call.mjs shape: reads SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD from .env.local.prod, injects into the cached pooler URL, prepends SET statement_timeout='60min' for the Supavisor 2-min backend-reuse default), or gate --prod --call behind an explicit --yes-i-mean-prod style flag. Cross-ref [[FIX-704]] (the procedure pattern), [[FIX-717]] [[FIX-718]] (the surfacing case). <!--id:FIX-791-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Intermittent RSC-fetch NetworkError on www.civitics.com — suspect Cloudflare challenge on fetch()/RSC requests + www/apex canonicalization** — Observed once in the FIX-795 post-deploy QA (2026-07-12): 'Failed to fetch RSC payload for https://www.civitics.com/institutions/<id>. Falling back to browser navigation. TypeError: NetworkError'. Next degrades gracefully (hard navigation), so UX impact is a full page load, not breakage. Two suspects, neither related to the [[FIX-796]]/[[FIX-797]] changes (a rate-limit 429 is an HTTP response, never a NetworkError): (1) Cloudflare bot protection challenging a fetch()-issued RSC request — CF 403s all scripted fetches to civitics.com ([[FIX-513]] territory; check the CF security-events dashboard for mitigations on /institutions/* fetch requests around the timestamp); (2) the www host — the site canonical is apex civitics.com (sitemap), so confirm what serves/redirects www.civitics.com and whether browsing on www leaves RSC fetches crossing a redirect. Investigate: reproduce while watching the network entry's response (cf-mitigated header / challenge HTML vs connection failure), and decide www→apex 301 at the CF/Vercel layer if canonicalization is the trigger. If it recurs rarely and is CF-transient, document and close as accepted noise. <!--id:FIX-799-->

- [ ] 🟢 S — **Moderation-harness pollution check doesn't watch auth.users/public.users** — The SF-P3 moderation harness's zero-pollution self-check (POLLUTION_TABLES in packages/data/src/pipelines/moderation-harness/index.ts) counts only 8 public tables before/after each run and does NOT watch auth.users or public.users. Its fixtures create ephemeral auth.users + public.users rows inside a rolled-back txn; if a rollback ever fails to take, leaked rows go undetected while the run still reports 'zero pollution'. That is exactly how ~30 bare-id auth.users rows leaked and poisoned admin.listUsers ([[FIX-660]]). FIX-660 hardened the three inserts (createUser / createAgedUser / createSyntheticUser) to be GoTrue-complete so a leak is now harmless, but leak DETECTION is still missing. Fix: add auth.users + public.users (schema-qualified counts) to the harness pollution watch so a future rollback failure surfaces loudly. Low priority — the harmless-leak hardening already shipped. <!--id:FIX-825-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Revoke default anon/authenticated EXECUTE on route-gated RPCs** — FIX-695 follow-up ([[FIX-695]]). Supabase grants EXECUTE to anon+authenticated on every new function by default, so any route-gated RPC lacking a REVOKE can be called directly via POST /rest/v1/rpc/<fn>, bypassing its Next route allow-list. Audited all 195 anon/authenticated-executable public functions on prod (89 SECURITY DEFINER); classified keep/revoke/needs-Craig with caller-client evidence (docs/audits/2026-07-13-rpc-execute-grants.md). Safety-gated on empty RLS-policy refs, empty view/generated-column refs, and a nested-INVOKER anon-keep closure (24 fns). Revoked anon+authenticated EXECUTE on 123 service_role/pipeline/cron/direct-pg-only functions (52 SECURITY DEFINER) + anon-only on 2 authenticated grant-checks; kept service_role. 13 no-caller read-only fns flagged needs-Craig (donor rollups, get_pv_* analytics, has_active_official_grant), left anon-open pending decision. <!--id:FIX-834-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Revoke anon/authenticated EXECUTE on the 13 no-caller RPCs (FIX-834 needs-Craig set)** — Follow-up to [[FIX-834]]. Zero callers anywhere (no .rpc, cron, proc, direct-pg, nested, view, or RLS reference) so revoking anon+authenticated EXECUTE is safe; kept service_role. Anon/auth-executable public fn count drops 72 → 59. <!--id:FIX-835-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **graph/spending treemap_recipients_by_contracts RPC >8s on cache-miss — materialize via FIX-507 contract-flow MV** — [[FIX-780]] audit finding (docs/audits/2026-07-16-fix780-internal-agg-rpc-audit.md). treemap_recipients_by_contracts (graph/spending treemap mode) live-aggregates ALL 3.24M contract-type financial_relationships joined to financial_entities + entity_tags, GROUP BY recipient, top-N. - [ ] 🟢 M — **Cross-source FE dedup: multi-binding org clusters (edge-confirmed merge)** — Follow-up from FIX-544 (2026-07-16). FIX-544's org-only merge used an exactly-one-binding gate: it folds a same-name (canonical_name+entity_type) cluster only when a SINGLE source-bound row is the unambiguous survivor. Multi-binding clusters — a non-committee org whose cluster has >=2 source-bound rows (e.g. AMERICAN CHEMISTRY COUNCIL 'other' and CLUB FOR GROWTH 'other', each carrying two littlesis ids) — were deliberately excluded and remain as same-name duplicates on prod. To merge them safely, design a shared-edge / shared-identifier confirmation (overlap in external_relationships/financial_relationships neighbors, or a matching normalized website/EIN/CIK) to pick the true survivor rather than merging on name alone. Reuse the FIX-544 merge routine (data:merge-fe-collisions) FK surface + verification once a survivor is chosen. See docs/audits/2026-07-16-fix544-cross-source-collision-investigation.md. Cross-ref [[FIX-544]], [[FIX-271]], [[FIX-273]]. <!--id:FIX-840-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **EDGAR SCT multi-year <br> comp cells concatenate into out-of-range values; officers silently dropped** — Surfaced by 2026-07-19 Sunday cron (GHA 29674748463): parseUsdCents strips newlines, so 3-fiscal-years-per-cell SCT layouts (cellText converts <br> to newline) concatenate into one oversized integer that the [[FIX-742]] guard drops. Every numeric cell in the row fails the same way, so the officer row is dropped — deterministic zero-officer coverage for this layout class. Fix: split on newlines, parse first digit-bearing line (= most recent FY, matching effectiveYear). Measurement: 2026-07-26 weekly run should show zero multi-line out-of-range warnings. <!--id:FIX-870-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **fetchJson gives up on 502/503/504 after one flat 30s retry; OpenStates state pages truncate on gateway blips** — 2026-07-19 run: MN bills page 2 hit 504 then 502, exhausting the fetchJson 2-attempt retry budget; the per-state loop breaks, so MN synced 20 of up to 60 recently-updated bills.
