Diagnosed 2026-08-08 while investigating unexpected prod write load. **The loop:** FEC published `indiv2026.zip` Sun 2026-08-02 15:45 UTC; [[FIX-903]]'s weekday drop-probe (`packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/drop-check.ts`) sees a file newer than `pipeline_state.fec_indiv_watermark` and fires `fec_bulk` on NON-Sunday nights (the Sunday gate in `weekly-gate.ts` is intact and not involved) — **but the FIX-903 probe is only ONE of the two triggers; see correction (3) below**; the stage grinds through part of an 840,338-row donor-entity upsert, then **the job is guillotined by the 150-minute GHA `timeout-minutes` cap**; the [[FIX-754]] checkpoint survives, so the next night resumes and repeats. **Prod evidence 2026-08-08 05:00 UTC:** `fec_bulk_run_state` = cycle 2026, donor-entities cursor 484,000/840,338, `started_at 2026-08-06T06:42:47Z` (same run-state alive 2 days), `fec_last_modified "Sun, 02 Aug 2026 15:45:35 GMT"`; `fec_indiv_watermark` 2026 = `"Sun, 26 Jul 2026 15:18:36 GMT"` (a week behind). **Run history:** Jul 31 10m OK, Aug 1 10m OK, Aug 2 (Sun) 1h44m OK, Aug 3 4h03m cancelled, Aug 4 1h54m fail, Aug 5 1h44m fail, Aug 6 4h04m cancelled, Aug 7 1h44m fail, Aug 8 cancelled manually at 1h36m. **Why it can never converge:** FEC republishes `indiv{yy}.zip` every Sunday ~15:20 UTC; when the new file lands the resume identity (`fec_last_modified`/etag) stops matching and `resolveResumePoint` resets the cursor to 0 — the stage cannot finish inside one nightly budget, so the weekly republish always laps it. **Collateral:** a 20s `pg_stat_statements` delta during the run showed trivial request volume (0.05-0.30 calls/sec, no bot) but badly degraded latency — proposals-list RPC 1,568ms avg, officials-list 841ms, a scalar p_id RPC 4,625ms; plus autovacuum on `financial_entities` running 26+ min and two `data_sync_log` rows stuck `running`. **Fix directions (not yet chosen):** cut the donor-entity stage's wall-clock (or give it a bigger budget) so it fits inside the 150-min cap instead of being guillotined by it; make resume survive a same-cycle republish rather than resetting to 0; and/or move the indiv stage off the nightly onto its own dispatch-only workflow with a larger budget (cf. the [[FIX-740]] USASpending precedent). **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-09 — three factual errors in the original bullet, re-measured across all six nights' GHA logs and run history.** **(1) It never OOM'd.** No `FATAL ERROR`, no `Reached heap limit`, no `Ineffective mark-compacts` appears in ANY of the six nights' logs. The dominant mechanism is the **150-minute GHA `timeout-minutes` cap** — 08-03 and 08-06 both log `exceeded the maximum execution time of 2h30m0s` — plus two UNRELATED prod statement timeouts (08-04 congress-officials; 08-05 `loadExistingCandidateNames`). 08-07's `The hosted runner lost communication with the server` log blob returns `BlobNotFound` against an empty step conclusion: one night of six, mechanism unknown. **`NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=12288` was never load-bearing**, so 'cap the heap' is not a fix direction. **(2) 'It has never once completed' is false.** On 08-04 fec_bulk finished **cycle 2022** in full and logged `⟳ Cleared fec_bulk_run_state — cycle 2022 complete (FIX-754)` — the 08-03 → 08-04 resume chain converged. Cycle 2026 is the stuck one and only started 08-06. **(3) The trigger was BOTH mechanisms, not [[FIX-903]] alone:** 08-03/04/08 fired via the [[FIX-754]] resume state, 08-05/06 via the FIX-903 drop probe. `pipelines/index.ts` evaluates the probe ONLY when `fecResumeState === null`, so the two branches substitute for each other and neither can be blamed in isolation. Pairs with [[FIX-996]] (checkpoint write path); [[FIX-997]] may be partly downstream of this. <!--id:FIX-995-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **fec_bulk resume checkpoint is written over PostgREST, times out under its own load** — `saveRunState` / `loadRunState` / `clearRunState` in `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/run-state.ts` persist the [[FIX-754]] resume cursor through the PostgREST client (`db.from("pipeline_state").upsert(...)`, line ~248). That path is subject to the `service_role` 8s `statement_timeout` and the ~100s gateway cap, so the checkpoint is written over the congested path at exactly the moment the DB is saturated by the very bulk writes being checkpointed. **Observed live in the 2026-08-08 nightly run:** `Upserting 840,338 individual donor entities...` / `individual-donor resuming at row 296,000/840,338 (FIX-754)` / `[fec-resume] failed to persist run state (continuing): upstream request timeout`. It is intermittent rather than total — the cursor did advance 296,000 → 484,000 across that run — but every dropped checkpoint is re-upserted work on the next resume, and the failure rate is highest precisely when progress matters most. The writer already holds a direct `pg.Client` against the session pooler for the bulk upserts (the [[FIX-444]] pattern for anything expected to exceed 8s); the state wrappers should reuse it rather than round-tripping through PostgREST. The `catch` blocks are deliberately best-effort by design (a save failure must never abort the pipeline) — that contract stays, only the transport changes. Surfaced by the [[FIX-995]] investigation. <!--id:FIX-996-->

These are intermittent — the same jobs succeed on other days (`abuse-events-retention` succeeded 08-07 and 08-06; `refresh-derived-mvs-daily` succeeded 08-07 in 1h07m). The ~10-30s time-to-failure is consistent with pg_cron giving up waiting for a background worker slot. **Check whether the failures correlate with the nightly FEC window ([[FIX-995]]) holding workers/connections** — `refresh-derived-mvs-daily` at 06:00 and `rule-taggers-daily` at 06:30 both sit inside the window where the fec-phase job is mid-run, and `max_worker_processes` on the Pro Micro is small; if so, several may clear once [[FIX-995]] is fixed and the real item here is alerting. **No retry or alerting exists on pg_cron failures today** — these were only found by manually reading `cron.job_run_details`. <!--id:FIX-997-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Hold nightly fec_bulk behind FEC_NIGHTLY_BULK_DISABLED while FIX-995 is open** — One env flag (`FEC_NIGHTLY_BULK_DISABLED=true`, read via `FLAGS.FEC_NIGHTLY_BULK_ENABLED`) checked in the nightly orchestrator AHEAD of all three fec_bulk trigger branches — the Sunday weekly gate, the [[FIX-754]] resume-state load, and the [[FIX-903]] weekday drop probe. Guarding one branch is not a guard: `pipelines/index.ts` evaluates the drop probe only `if (runFec && !isWeekly && fecResumeState === null)`, so clearing the run state to stop the resume trigger merely hands the trigger to FIX-903. Measured on the 2026-08-03..08-08 fec-phase logs, BOTH triggers fired on different nights (08-03/08-04/08-08 = FIX-754 resume; 08-05/08-06 = FIX-903 drop probe), so the hand-off is observed, not theoretical. Set in `.github/workflows/nightly.yml` fec-phase env; `fec-backfill.yml` is deliberately NOT touched so the ingest can run by dispatch meanwhile.
