If it fails the same way with the moves in place, the cause is the unexplained 14:00-16:08 tail rather than the EC-rebuild overlap. Cross-ref [[FIX-904]], [[FIX-1052]], [[FIX-903]], [[FIX-740]]. **✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-18 (cc-prompt-66) — CLEAN RE-FIRE SUCCEEDED; NO DEFECT IN IRS-990.** Re-fired off-peak. GHA run **32085175163**, workflow_dispatch, started 2026-08-18T00:36:42Z, **2m2s, conclusion success**, and the pipeline's own data_sync_log row closed `complete` 00:37:09 → 00:38:38 (rows_inserted 0 — the index HEAD-watermark short-circuit on unchanged years, which is the documented cheap path, not a silent no-op). THE HYPOTHESIS IN THIS BULLET IS CONFIRMED AND SHARPENED, and one honest correction belongs on the record: the FIRST re-fire attempt tonight (run 32084489817, 00:26:00Z) ALSO failed, with `officials load failed: canceling statement due to statement timeout` at exactly 8.4s — the service_role 8s ceiling. That was **this session's own collateral**: a heavy multi-scan diagnostic query of mine was running against prod across 00:26:25–00:26:33, the precise failure window. Re-run with the box quiet, the identical 38-page paginated officials load completes in **5.6s total with page 1 at 1074ms**, and the pipeline runs green. So IRS-990 is not defective at any layer: the Monday 15:28 failure was PostgREST unable to serve a schema-cache query during the saturation window, tonight's first failure was contention I created, and with the box idle the same code path is fine. THAT IS THREE INDEPENDENT DEMONSTRATIONS that the saturation window degrades the REQUEST PATH, which is what this bullet raised the priority on — now filed with a named mechanism as [[FIX-1058]] (background-worker slot exhaustion; `max_worker_processes=12` against `cron.max_running_jobs=32`). The 2026-08-24 Monday firing remains the read for whether the [[FIX-1052]] schedule moves cleared the window; that read now belongs to FIX-1058. Cross-ref [[FIX-1058]], [[FIX-1056]]. <!--id:FIX-1055-->
- [x] 🔴 L — **EC rebuild cannot finish inside 6h because the CONTRACTS arm's throughput collapsed 3.2x — growth alone does not explain it, and FIX-1034 did not help** — Fix-shape spec for the next rollup-lane prompt. Diagnosed 2026-08-17 (cc-prompt-63); no rework done in that session by design. THE THROUGHPUT TABLE is the whole diagnosis — edges_total and runtime are both in data_sync_log/cron.job_run_details for every run, so this is measured, not inferred: 07-15 695,640 edges in 1,077s = 646 edges/s; 07-20 1,349,007 in 2,252s = 599/s; 07-22 1,313,229 in 2,206s = 595/s; 07-29 3,184,880 in 5,350s = 595/s; 08-17 4,011,180 in 21,615s = 186 edges/s. Throughput held flat at 595-646 edges/s across a 4.6x growth in edge count, then COLLAPSED to 186/s — a 3.2x regression. GROWTH ALONE DOES NOT EXPLAIN THE FAILURE: at the old steady 595 edges/s, even 4.0M edges finishes in about 6,700s, comfortably inside the 6h ceiling. It is the rate regression that pushes it past the wall, not the size. THE COLLAPSE IS LOCALIZED AND NAMED: the 08-17 run's cancel_detail is 'rebuild_entity_connections_contracts: canceling statement due to statement timeout', so the contracts arm specifically is where the 6h is spent. chunk_failures is 0, so this is not error-retry churn — it is one arm running slow. FIX-1034 DID NOT HELP: it landed 08-14 and 08-17 was the first post-statistics Monday. Runtime 21,615s vs the pre-statistics 08-10 run's 22,380s — inside noise, both pinned at the ceiling. The FR/FE planner statistics corrected a different query family; the contracts arm needs its own EXPLAIN. THE STEP CHANGE IS DATED: 07-29 completed in 5,350s, 08-03 was already dead at the ceiling. Whatever changed sits in that window and should be the first thing looked for — a plan flip (cf. the FIX-1030 nested-loop/Memoize mechanism, same shape and the same random_page_cost=1.1 cost model), or bloat in a contracts-side table with no vacuum owner (cf. FIX-884's all-visible collapse, and note per FIX-943 that entity_connections gets a vacuum but the contracts SOURCE tables may not). THE COMPOUNDING DEFECT — it never banks progress. The 08-17 run wrote 4,011,180 edges and then closed status='partial' with no resume marker; contrast donor_rollup_refresh, which closes 'budget exhausted — resumable at recipien...' and picks up next firing (FIX-1002/1021). So every EC firing restarts from the top, burns 6h, and dies at the same place — it can never win no matter how many times it runs, which is why the rebuild has not completed since 2026-07-29 and why [[FIX-1053]]'s recipient_count_reconcile arm (called only from the tail of run_entity_connections_rebuild) has not run since. FIX SHAPE, in order: (1) EXPLAIN the contracts arm on prod and find the 3.2x — do this BEFORE any budget work, because if it is a plan flip the budget becomes unnecessary; (2) give the rebuild the FIX-1002/1021 treatment — an explicit time budget and a durable resume checkpoint per arm, so a firing that runs out of budget banks its edges and the next one continues; (3) only then consider splitting the arms into separate jobs. Do NOT simply raise the 6h statement_timeout: [[FIX-985]] set that ceiling deliberately, and a job that needs longer than the gap to its next firing is the failure mode [[FIX-971]] corrected the record on. Cross-ref [[FIX-985]], [[FIX-1002]], [[FIX-1021]], [[FIX-1034]], [[FIX-1030]], [[FIX-884]], [[FIX-943]], [[FIX-1052]], [[FIX-1053]]. **✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-18 (cc-prompt-66) — AND THE DIAGNOSIS ABOVE IS CORRECTED ON ITS CENTRAL POINT.** Step 1 was run before any budget work, as specified, and it refutes the contracts-arm attribution. MEASURED ON PROD (read-only, idle box, bounded at 300s, work_mem=256MB — exactly what the procedure sets) the contracts arm's aggregate is **128.5 seconds**, not hours: GroupAggregate (actual time=125131.882..128408.178 rows=189949 loops=1), Sort Method: external merge Disk: 359544kB, Bitmap Heap Scan on financial_relationships (actual time=768.903..95472.569 rows=3908956, Heap Blocks: exact=295464), Buffers: shared hit=9 read=301515 temp read=44943 written=44944, Execution Time: 128456.054 ms. cancel_detail names contracts because that is where the AXE FELL, not where the time went — an important general lesson about reading cancel messages as attribution. WHERE THE 6 HOURS ACTUALLY WENT, measured rather than inferred, from the per-arm clock the run leaves behind: each incremental arm stamps its watermark row with now(), which is that chunk transaction's start, so pipeline_state.entity_connections_donations updated_at=2026-08-17 08:00:00 is arm 1's start and entity_connections_votes updated_at=2026-08-17 11:17:04 is arm 2's start, i.e. arm 1's end; the cancel landed at 14:00:24. **The DONATIONS arm ran 11,824s — 3h17m, 55% of the budget** — and votes+cosponsors+appointments+oversight+holds+gifts+contracts shared the remaining 9,800s. WHY: donations is incremental on an updated_at watermark, and the FEC merge/re-split work ([[FIX-933]]/[[FIX-934]]/[[FIX-954]]/[[FIX-955]]) bulk-rewrote financial_relationships — by week of updated_at, 2026-07-27 touched 1,201,953 rows across 820,602 distinct donors and 2026-08-03 touched 2,721,613 rows across 1,358,662 donors — so the incremental arm degenerated into a near-full rebuild and wrote 4,011,180 edges against the 5,706,655 donation edges that exist, ~70% of the table, matching the ~71% of donors the dirty set covered. That IS inside the 07-29..08-03 window this bullet correctly dated; the bullet attributed it to the wrong arm. THE THROUGHPUT METRIC WAS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO IT: the contracts arm's cost scales with its INPUT (3.9M FR rows) while its OUTPUT (189,949 edges) barely moves, so no edges-per-second reading can see contracts growth at all — and conversely the donations blow-up looked like a rate collapse because the edges were real but the work per edge was not. A SECOND, INDEPENDENT REGRESSION IS ALSO REAL BUT IS NOT THE 6h: contract+grant FR rows went 1,467,389 → 3,822,744 across the weeks of 2026-07-06 and 2026-07-13 (a USASpending ingest, EARLIER than the dated window), which pushed the contracts sort past work_mem into a 359MB external merge; and the group estimate is 2,327,285 against 189,949 actual, a 12.3x over-estimate off from_id n_distinct=-0.268211 ([[FIX-1034]]). ALSO CORRECTED: the arm functions' SET statement_timeout TO '30min' clause is DECORATIVE — statement_timeout is armed at CALL start and a nested function cannot re-arm it, which is why the run died at the 6h mark rather than 30 minutes into contracts. WHAT SHIPPED (step 2, which the spec rightly said ships regardless of step 1): migration 20260818000000 — a 5h wall-clock budget checked at ARM BOUNDARIES so the exit is orderly rather than axed mid-statement (1h under the 6h ceiling so the terminal bookkeeping and cursor write always land; the ceiling itself is NOT raised, per [[FIX-985]]), a durable per-arm resume checkpoint in pipeline_state.entity_connections_rebuild_cursor so a budget exit banks its arms and the next firing skips them, and per-arm elapsed seconds in metadata.arm_timings — that last being the observability gap that permitted the misattribution in the first place. The budget is overridable via pipeline_state.entity_connections_rebuild_budget so the repros exercise shipped code rather than a test variant. Reproduced locally against prod-clone data, both paths: budget exit banked arm 1 and closed partial with 'budget exhausted — resumable at arm rebuild_entity_connections_votes (1 of 11 arms banked)', the next firing logged 'resuming cycle ... 1 arm(s) already banked', SKIPPED donations and banked votes+cosponsors; and a forced 5s statement_timeout closed the row partial with canceled=true, elapsed_seconds=5, the cancelled arm correctly NOT banked. Step 3 (arm splitting) stays DEFERRED and is now much less obviously needed. Follow-ups filed: [[FIX-1058]] (the bgworker starvation this session's log pull named), [[FIX-1059]] (the retired-vs-broken detector gap that [[FIX-1053]] surfaced). <!--id:FIX-1056-->
- [ ] 🟠 S — **The request-path probe is blind to the Cloudflare layer — it curls the Vercel origin, and cannot be repointed while Under Attack is on** — Found 2026-08-17 (cc-prompt-63) while adding the 526 note to docs/CLOUDFLARE.md. THE DEFECT: the [[FIX-1026]] request-path-probe job in .github/workflows/platform-snapshot.yml inherited the snapshot job's APP_URL default, which is the VERCEL ORIGIN (civitics-civitics.vercel.app). The probe exists to answer 'can a user load the site', but a user's request traverses Cloudflare then Vercel — so probing the origin tests only the second half and is structurally blind to every edge-layer failure. NOT THEORETICAL, TWICE OVER: on 2026-08-12 civitics.com served Cloudflare 526 for much of the day (expired ORIGIN certificate, renewals failing silently behind the proxy) while the vercel.app URL kept serving — platform-snapshot stayed GREEN through the whole outage. That is the same shape as the 2026-08-11 522 that FIX-1026 was built for; the job was pointed at the one host that cannot see it. THE OBVIOUS FIX IS BLOCKED, AND THAT IS THE REAL FINDING. Repointing at civitics.com today makes the job fail every hour forever, because the zone is still at security_level=under_attack and challenges non-browser clients. Measured against the live zone the same day: GET / -> 403, /officials -> 403, /api/officials/<id>/responsiveness -> 403, every one carrying 'Cf-Mitigated: challenge', and the 403 persists with a browser User-Agent. A permanently-red pager is a disabled pager, so this is strictly worse than the blind spot and was NOT shipped. THE UNDER ATTACK STATE IS ITSELF A CORRECTION: a planning note recorded Under Attack as turned off on 08-12, but the zone says otherwise and so does the platform's own loop — pipeline_state.cf_mitigation_loop.tripped is null, which by the CLOUDFLARE.md rule means the loop did not set the level, and the loop logged skip_already_escalated twice on 2026-08-16 ('3 breach hours, but the zone is already at security_level=under_attack and this loop did not set it'). So it is a human setting still in force, and docs/CLOUDFLARE.md has been corrected accordingly. SAME ROOT BLOCKS THE OTHER HALF OF THE MONITORING GAP: FIX-1026's header already notes that true minutes-scale detection needs an external uptime service, and Under Attack blocks those too — so the zone posture, not the repo, is the binding constraint on request-path monitoring. WHAT SHIPPED THIS SESSION: the probe env now reads a dedicated PROBE_URL secret first, falling back to CIVITICS_APP_URL then the origin default, so the eventual flip is a secret change with no code change; and the workflow carries the measured 403s plus the two conditions that unblock it. FIX SHAPE, pick one: (1) take the zone off under_attack (already the standing Pending item in CLOUDFLARE.md, and the real test of whether Common Exploit Paths holds alone), then set PROBE_URL to https://civitics.com; or (2) leave the posture and add a WAF skip rule admitting this probe specifically — a secret header is the cleanest key, since UA and IP are both weak. Prefer (1); it fixes the external-uptime gap in the same move. Whichever is chosen, verify with the three curls above BEFORE flipping the secret. ALSO CORRECTED while reading the live zone: Always Use HTTPS is On, not Off as the posture table said, and there are three custom WAF rules (Common Exploit Paths, the WAF - SSL ACME skip, Meta), not two. Cross-ref [[FIX-1026]], [[FIX-1045]], [[FIX-799]]. <!--id:FIX-1057-->
- [ ] 🟠 M — **Monday/Wednesday starvation window is bgworker-slot exhaustion, not query contention — pg_cron may request 32 concurrent jobs from a 12-slot pool** — Found 2026-08-18 (cc-prompt-66) while hunting the Monday 09:xx-16:08 starvation window named in [[FIX-1052]]. THE MECHANISM IS NAMED, THE CONSUMER IS NOT. Pulled the Supabase Analytics postgres_logs for 2026-08-17 13:50-16:25 UTC. Through the entire 14:00-16:08 tail — that is, AFTER the EC rebuild was cancelled at 14:00:24 and with nothing at all in data_sync_log — the log carries three independent message classes that all mean "a process could not be started in time": (1) jobid 40 (derived-mvs-unit-watchdog, every 2 min) logs "cron job 40 starting" followed EXACTLY 10 seconds later by "cron job 40 job startup timeout", firing after firing for hours — 10s is pg_cron cron.task_start_timeout, so the bgworker never registered; (2) "autovacuum worker took too long to start; canceled" and "autovacuum worker started without a worker entry", which the autovacuum launcher emits when a requested worker does not register in time; (3) "could not accept SSL connection: EOF detected" and "Connection reset by peer" in a continuous drip, i.e. clients abandoning during the TLS handshake because the postmaster cannot complete accepts. THE DECISIVE NEGATIVE RESULT — IT IS NOT A HEAVY QUERY. auto_explain.log_min_duration is 10000 on prod, so EVERY statement over 10s in that window was logged. The only ones that appear are Supabase platform telemetry: SUM(pg_database_size(...)) at 10.4s and 17.6s, and the pg_stat_statements roll-ups at 12.7s, 13.5s, 13.9s and 16.5s. Not one pipeline query, not one user query. That is precisely why data_sync_log is silent across the tail and why FIX-1052 could not find a culprit in it: none of our work was running. The box was not busy executing — it could not START processes. THE STRUCTURAL OVER-SUBSCRIPTION, measured on prod the same night: max_worker_processes=12 (configuration file), cron.max_running_jobs=32 (default, and per the Supabase config note NOT overridable), max_parallel_workers=2, max_logical_replication_workers=4, max_connections=60, autovacuum_max_workers=3. pg_cron is permitted 32 concurrent job workers out of a 12-slot pool it shares with parallel query and logical replication, and nothing enforces the difference — the ceiling is discovered at runtime as "job startup timeout". Note this is the same mwp=12 that failed its first Monday. WHAT WAS SHIPPED, AND WHAT IT IS FOR: a self-limiting bgworker census sampler (migration 20260818002000) — an UNLOGGED bgworker_census table plus sample_bgworker_census(), scheduled every 2 minutes as jobname bgworker-census, deliberately on the SAME cadence as the jobid-40 watchdog whose startup timeout is the symptom, so a MISSING sample is itself data (this job could not get a worker either). It no-ops unless pipeline_state.bgworker_census.expires_at is in the future, armed for 48h from 2026-08-18, so it disarms itself even if the cron job outlives the investigation. NEXT READ: Wednesday 2026-08-19 08:00 UTC and its tail, then Thursday read the census to answer WHICH backends hold the slots. TEARDOWN, one line, do not leave this armed: SELECT cron.unschedule(bgworker-census); DROP TABLE public.bgworker_census; DELETE FROM pipeline_state WHERE key = bgworker_census; (quote the two literals). CANDIDATE FIXES once the consumer is known, none shipped tonight because guessing the remedy before the consumer is exactly the error [[FIX-1056]] corrected: raise max_worker_processes; thin the pg_cron job population in the window; or stagger the */2 watchdog off the boundary minutes. Cross-ref [[FIX-1052]], [[FIX-1056]], [[FIX-1055]], [[FIX-1030]], [[FIX-968]]. <!--id:FIX-1058-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **The rollup watch registry cannot tell RETIRED from BROKEN — recipient_count_reconcile has paged for 6 weeks about a job FIX-736 deliberately unscheduled** — Found 2026-08-18 (cc-prompt-66) while resolving [[FIX-1053]], which this replaces as the real defect. FIX-1053 read recipient_count_reconcile as "gated behind an EC rebuild that no longer completes" and predicted it would resolve when the rebuild could finish. IT WILL NOT, because the arm is not gated — it is RETIRED, and has been since 2026-07-05. THE EVIDENCE, from the prod catalog rather than from inference: (1) run_entity_connections_rebuild() mentions reconcile_recipient_count exactly twice and BOTH are inside a comment ("recipient_count is reconciled out-of-band (reconcile_recipient_count(), monthly post-VACUUM)"). There is no CALL. A catalog-wide search over pg_get_functiondef for every function and procedure in public returns exactly two hits: the procedure itself, and that comment. (2) No cron job invokes it — cron.job has no command matching recipient. (3) Migration 20260705000200_fix736_recipient_count_fr_derived.sql is explicit about why: "Its cron job (ec-recipient-count-reconcile) is unscheduled below — the routine FR orphan sweep is now owned by financial-entity-totals-reconcile", and the migration does SELECT cron.unschedule(jobname) FROM cron.job WHERE jobname = ec-recipient-count-reconcile. FIX-736 repurposed the procedure as an FR-based BREAK-GLASS full recompute and deliberately left it callerless. So the last recipient_count_reconcile row being 2026-07-05 is not a symptom of anything — it is the last firing before the job was retired ON PURPOSE, which also answers the open sub-question FIX-1053 left standing (the call was never added to the rebuild; only a stale comment said it was, and that comment is corrected in migration 20260818000000). THE ACTUAL DEFECT IS THE DETECTOR. [[FIX-977]]s derived rollup watch registry and packages/data/src/scripts/canary-check.ts still watch recipient_count_reconcile and escalate on its staleness — canary-check.ts even carries the comment "recipient_count_reconcile escalate at 812h against a 730h monthly job". There is no way to mark a pipeline RETIRED, so a deliberate retirement is indistinguishable from a broken pipeline, and the detector will page about this one forever. That is the inverse of the standing rule that a red canary is a true positive: this red is a FALSE positive, and false positives are how a real one gets ignored. FIX SHAPE: give the registry an explicit retired set (or a retired_at column on whatever supplies the watch list), seeded with recipient_count_reconcile, so a retirement is a recorded decision rather than a permanent alarm; and require that any future cron.unschedule of a data_sync_log-writing job either lands in that set or is called out as an intentional gap. WHILE THERE, sweep for the same shape: any pipeline in the watch registry whose cron job no longer exists is by definition in this class, and the registry can detect that itself by anti-joining against cron.job. Cross-ref [[FIX-1053]], [[FIX-736]], [[FIX-977]], [[FIX-978]], [[FIX-968]]. <!--id:FIX-1059-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **fec-backfill/nightly indiv heap ceilings are now dead tuning — retire 14336/12288 and size the sort buffer instead** — FIX-961 PR 3a replaced the indiv stages in-memory aggregation maps with an external sort (packages/data/src/lib/external-sort.ts), so the stages live set no longer scales with cycle size. Measured on the full cycle-2026 file (30,632,248 lines): external completes at heap 512 MB / buffer 25k with peak RSS 424 MB, at heap 1024 / buffer 100k with 900 MB, and at heap 2048 / buffer 400k with 2,256 MB — identical output in all three, flat wall-clock 86-93s. The two workflow heap settings are therefore tuning a number that no longer moves with the data: fec-backfill.yml carries NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=14336 and nightly.yml fec-phase carries 12288, both sized for the OLD accumulator. Both are now oversized and the divergence tracked by FIX-1000 is moot in its original form. Direction: after the FIX-961 phase-4 indiv20 backfill proves the new path on the largest presidential file, drop both to a common modest value (2048-4096 is ample) and set FEC_INDIV_SORT_BUFFER explicitly rather than leaving the default implicit; that also frees runner RAM the 16 GB ubuntu-latest box currently reserves for nothing. Do NOT do this before phase 4 lands — the whole point is to change one variable at a time. Note the new resource to watch is sort DISK (~2 percent of extracted-text size; 112 MB against a 5.4 GB extract), not heap. Cross-ref FIX-961, FIX-1000, FIX-995, FIX-254, FIX-689. <!--id:FIX-1060-->
- [ ] 🟡 M — **indiv writer stages still materialize whole-cycle arrays — the next indiv memory ceiling, now that streaming is bounded** — FIX-961 PR 3a bounded the indiv STREAMING stage but not the WRITE stages. fec-bulk/index.ts still builds three whole-cycle arrays before each upsert: donorInputs (one object per donor), indivRelInputs (one per donor x candidate pair) and indivCmteRelInputs (one per donor x committee pair), because upsertIndividualDonorsBatch / upsertIndividualDonationsBatch / upsertIndividualToCommitteeDonationsBatch take arrays. Measured cycle-2026 scale: 879,782 donors, 762,891 cand pairs, 553,717 cmte pairs — so roughly 2.2M objects, order 400-600 MB, and cycle-2020 is materially bigger. This did not surface as the OOM because the streaming maps blew first; with those gone it becomes the binding constraint, and PR 3b makes it worse (the phase-0 measurement projects +50 percent FR rows platform-wide for cycle 2026, 1,316,608 to 1,980,786). Direction: the writers already chunk internally and carry a FIX-754 resume cursor keyed on processedRows/totalRows, so the shape to aim for is an async-iterable input with a known total rather than an array — the sorted files the stage now produces are re-readable and already carry their group counts, so totalRows is available without materializing. Do this AFTER phase 4 proves the current shape on indiv20, so a regression is attributable. Cross-ref FIX-961, FIX-995, FIX-754, FIX-1060. <!--id:FIX-1061-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **mark-killed depends on PostgREST, so it fails exactly when a run dies of DB overload — third distinct failure mode** — Measured 2026-08-18: the fec-backfill run 32097136492 was SIGTERMd at its 350-min cap at 09:44:20; its mark-killed backstop step then failed at 09:45:31 with "[mark-killed] data_sync_log query failed: Could not query the database for the schema cache. Retrying." and the job ended seconds later without writing the marker. Re-running the same command locally failed identically. Cause: mark-killed.ts goes through createAdminClient (PostgREST), and PostgREST was returning 503 PGRST002 because the box was too overloaded to serve its schema-cache catalog query (see FIX-1063). This is a structural coupling, not bad luck: a run is most likely to be killed precisely when the database is overloaded, which is precisely when PostgREST is least likely to be serving — so the backstop is unavailable exactly when it is needed. It succeeded only after the prod restart (marker then written: pipeline=nightly_killed, started_at 08-18 03:54, error_message=workflow-timeout-or-sigterm). This is the THIRD distinct mark-killed failure mode after FIX-962 (window-hours shorter than the job cap) and FIX-963 (no-op guard not bound to the own run) — all three share the shape "the backstop fails in the situation it exists for". FIX SHAPE: give mark-killed a direct pg.Client path (SUPABASE_DB_URL / direct connection) with PostgREST as fallback rather than the only route — the FIX-444 precedent for going direct when PostgREST is unsuitable already exists in this codebase, and the write is a single UPDATE/INSERT on data_sync_log. Cross-ref FIX-962, FIX-963, FIX-290, FIX-1063, FIX-444. <!--id:FIX-1065-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Six weekly pg_cron jobs are stacked into Tuesday 06:00-10:00 UTC and starve each other of background workers** — Measured on prod for Tuesday 2026-08-18, which was the worst observed instance because the cc-65 phase-4 FEC backfill also held the box 03:54-09:44. NINE jobs fire inside four hours: jobid 9 daily-MVs 06:00 (1,355s ok), jobid 11 rule-taggers-daily 06:30 STARVED, jobid 10 weekly-MVs 07:00 STARVED, jobid 25 agency-staffing 07:30 STARVED, jobid 26 treemap 08:15 (ran 6,477s unbounded and took the REST API down - see FIX-1063), jobid 17 donor-party 08:45 STARVED, jobid 24 donor-rollup 09:00 and jobid 13 FE-totals 09:00 (SAME SECOND - both stamped 09:00:01), jobid 12 rule-taggers-weekly 10:00 killed by the recovery restart. Four of the nine failed with pg_cron job-startup-timeout, which is worker-launch starvation, not query cancellation (the FIX-1052 finding: 191 startup timeouts vs 3 real statement timeouts over 08-10..08-17). SIX of these are WEEKLY jobs that have no reason to share a peak with the three daily ones - their combined healthy runtime is about 2.5h and Tuesday afternoon is completely empty. The 09:00 same-second collision between jobids 24 and 13 is the sharpest instance: a daily job and a weekly job contending for the same worker slot every Tuesday. Fix shape: serialize the weekly six across Tuesday afternoon with cron.alter_job by NAME, keeping clear of the 11:05-11:18 and 17:05-17:18 vacuum waves and of jobid 24 at 09:00/12:00. Strictly Tuesday-only, so it cannot perturb the Wednesday 08:00 entity-connections rebuild that FIX-1056 is being tested by. Cross-ref FIX-1052, FIX-1063, FIX-1031, FIX-1033, FIX-985. <!--id:FIX-1066-->

## COMMUNITY & AUTH
- [ ] 🟡 M — **User flagging on data rows — data_flags table + FlagDataButton + admin triage** — Today's flagging stops at user-generated content (comments + civic-initiative arguments via [[FIX-057]]). Action: (a) new data_flags table — (id, entity_type, entity_id, source, reason ENUM('wrong'|'outdated'|'missing_source'|'misspelled'|'other'), comment TEXT, flagger_user_id REFERENCES users, created_at, classification TEXT NULL, classification_confidence NUMERIC NULL, auto_resolved_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL, admin_resolved_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL, resolution ENUM('valid'|'invalid'|'duplicate'|'no_action') NULL). The classification / confidence / auto_resolved_at fields are forward-design slots for a future enrichment_queue auto-moderator (separate FIX, NOT built here) — a Haiku worker reads WHERE classification IS NULL, scores, auto-resolves obvious spam, escalates the rest. (b) FlagDataButton.tsx integrated alongside SourceBadge ([[FIX-399]]), opens a reason picker + optional comment. Auth-required (matches existing content_flags pattern). Rate-limited per-user-per-day. (c) Extend ModerationSection.tsx with a data_flags tab — admin sees WHERE admin_resolved_at IS NULL AND (classification IS NULL OR classification_confidence < 0.8), marks resolved with one of the four resolution values. (d) NOT in scope: the enrichment_queue auto-moderator (separate future FIX), per-source quality scoring, correction-suggestion workflow (Wikipedia-grade — separate initiative, see Citizens Council in PLATFORM_REBUILD_SPEC). Net: a participant's district at the time of an EARLIER position is unrecoverable, so any time-series or "where did support move" reading of the C2-E choropleth is permanently impossible for data written before the column exists. Fix is one column plus one line in `set_entity_position`, which already computes `v_juris` for the `entity_positions` write — add it to the `position_events` INSERT too. No consumer today: the C2-E choropleth as specced reads current positions only, so this is capture-ahead-of-consumer, justified solely by unreconstructability. Cross-ref the C2 brainstorm outcome doc (`Claude/civitics/c2-deliberation-integrity-brainstorm.md`). <!--id:FIX-907-->
- [ ] 🟠 M — **Evidence-rubric schema (PR A) — investigation_type, measure claim_type, record-level citations, completeness read** — [[FIX-593]] is filed as "content authoring, not code" — that premise is wrong, and it blocks the seed. The evidence-rubric schema from `design-investigation-types-rubric.md` §5 was never filed, so four required slots of the first drafted case (HR 26 / roll-call `2025-house-035`) have nowhere to be written. Scope: (1) `investigations.investigation_type` as a first-class column, not `metadata`, so it can be filtered, indexed and joined to the rubric config (rubric definitions live in code, versioned). (2) `evidence_cards.claim_type` must accept **`measure`** — payload in `metadata` carrying pattern name, parameters, result, denominator, coverage and `computed_at`, so a reader can re-run it. **Measured 2026-07-28: the data holds only `context` and `edge`.** The HR 26 case needs measure cards for `timing`, `comparison`, `specificity` and `tag_composition`, so it is roughly half unwritable until this lands. (3) `citations` must accept a roll-call vote row and a `financial_relationships` row as targets, not just the six searchable entity types; both tables already carry what a receipt needs. (4) A completeness read — required-slots-filled per investigation, derivable from `(investigation_type, evidence_cards.claim_type + metadata.slot)` — which drives display, a sort key and the review gate. **Two decisions to settle inside this PR, not before it:** (a) `financial_relationships.fec_filing_id` is listed as a cited field in the `donations` slot but is **NULL on all 4,459,559 donation rows**, and `source_url` is committee-level (9,257 distinct URLs across those rows) so it resolves the donor, not the donation — either drop the field from the slot definition or block on populating it; (b) the R8 topical-fit affirmation currently has no home, and the proposed `metadata.affirmed_by` + `metadata.affirmed_at` is an untyped JSON convention with nothing enforcing it — decide whether a typed column or a check constraint is warranted while there are 2 investigations rather than 200. Do NOT rewire the corroboration trigger in this PR (rubric §5): the meaningful corroboration for a measure card is that the computation still reproduces, so let it carry a re-verification timestamp instead. Also fold the reader-facing register rules into the type definition — the `topical_rationale` card and the claim are the only two blocks an ordinary visitor reads, and they must be plain language; see `case-hr26-draft-2026-07-29.md` §10 for the worked example. Cross-ref [[FIX-593]], [[FIX-585]]. <!--id:FIX-927-->
- [ ] 🟡 S — **entity_grants has no uniqueness constraint — one account holds 13 duplicate active staff grants** — Measured on the prod-clone 2026-07-29: the seeded platform account `OpenLedgerO` holds **13 identical rows** in `entity_grants` — same `user_id`, `role='staff'`, `target_type='global'`, `status='active'`. There is no uniqueness constraint on `(user_id, role, target_type)`, so a grant-issuing path is not idempotent and has re-run. Harmless for *granting* — the exemption check in `create_investigation()` is an `EXISTS`, which short-circuits — but **the revoke path is the risk and needs checking**: if revocation updates `status` on a single row rather than all matching rows, revoking leaves the other 12 active and the user keeps the privilege silently. Verify the revoke path before assuming severity. Fix: dedupe the existing rows, then add a partial unique index on `(user_id, role, target_type) WHERE status = 'active'`. Related tidy-up found at the same time: the live `platform_admin` account has a NULL `display_name`, which would render badly if it ever surfaced in UI; and a `civitics.platform` account is about to be created for the [[FIX-593]] seed — create its grant exactly once. Cross-ref [[FIX-593]]. <!--id:FIX-928-->

## DOCUMENTATION (Open Source Readiness)
- [ ] 🟢 S — **FIX-961 bullet records only the 12 GB OOM — its candidate fix (a) was executed on 2026-08-05 and falsified at 14 GB** — Evidence correction, no code. FIX-961s bullet documents run 30767905956 (2026-08-02, cycles=2020,2022) dying at ~53M of ~69M lines with NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=12288, and lists candidate fix (a) raise heap to ~13.5-14 GB - marginal. That candidate was then executed: commit 8da5e209 raised fec-backfill.yml to --max-old-space-size=14336 on 2026-08-02, and run 30965259079 (2026-08-05 01:03 UTC, cycles=2020, log confirms NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=14336 on every step) OOMd again at ~64M of ~69M lines — 93 percent of the way through, exit 134, same V8 Reached heap limit. So the bullets remediation list reads as untried when one of its four options was tried and failed, and that failure is the strongest available argument for the spill-to-disk option (c) that FIX-961 PR 3a actually shipped. FIX-963s bullet references the same run 30965259079 for a different reason (the mark-killed no-op guard) and likewise does not record that it was a second heap OOM at a raised ceiling. Action: append the 14 GB datapoint to the FIX-961 bullet under the append-only contract, or let the FIX-961 closure commit carry it.
